For the same reason,
Q
is not contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

To prove (
4.11
), assume that
\ell(Q)\leq A_{0}^{-2}\ell(R)
. Otherwise the inequality is immediate.
By Lemma
3.3
, since all the ancestors
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{m}
of
Q
that are contained in
e^{\prime}(R)
are not
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, it follows that
Q_{1}
(the parent of
Q
) satisfies

{\mathcal{P}}(Q_{1})\lesssim A_{0}^{-m/2-1}\,{\mathcal{P}}(Q_{m}).

Because
Q_{m}\subset e^{\prime}(R)\subset 2R
and
\ell(Q_{m})=A_{0}^{-1}\ell(R)
, it is easy to see that
{\mathcal{P}}(Q_{m})\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R)\lesssim C_{d}\,\Theta(R)
, and so

{\mathcal{P}}(Q_{1})\lesssim A_{0}^{-m/2}\,\Theta(R)\approx\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R).

By the definition of
{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
, we know that
{\mathcal{P}}(Q_{1})\geq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R)
, which together with
the previous estimate yields (
4.11
).
∎

The cubes from
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))
need not be
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, which is problematic for some of the estimates involving the Riesz transform localized around the
trees
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))
that will be required later. For this reason, we need to consider enlarged versions of these trees. For
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we let
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
be the family made up of the following cubes:

•

the cubes from
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
,

•

the cubes that are contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
which are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling and, moreover, are maximal.

Notice that all the cubes from
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, with the exception of the ones from
{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
We let
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
be the family of cubes that are contained in
e^{\prime}(R)
and are not
strictly contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

4.3.
Tractable trees

Given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we say that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is tractable (or that
R
is tractable) if

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R)))\leq B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))).

In this case we write
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
.

Our next objective consists in showing how we can associate a family of tractable trees to any
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
.
First we need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
be such that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is not tractable. Then there exists a family
{\mathsf{GH}}(R)\subset{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{MDW}}
satisfying:

(a)

The balls
B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))
, with
Q\in{\mathsf{GH}}(R)
are pairwise disjoint.

(b)

For every
Q\in{\mathsf{GH}}(R)
,
\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\geq\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))\geq B^{1/2}\sigma(Q)
.

(c)

B^{1/4}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{GH}}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\gtrsim\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))).

The name “
{\mathsf{GH}}
” stands for “good high (density)”. Remark that the property (c) and the fact that
R\not\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
yield

(4.12)

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{GH}}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\gtrsim B^{3/4}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

which is suitable for iteration.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

Let
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
be such that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is not tractable. Notice first that

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R)))\overset{\eqref{eq:sigmae'lesigmae}}{\leq}B^{1/4}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))\leq B^{-3/4}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))).

Let
I\subset{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))
be the subfamily of the cubes
Q
such that

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))<B^{1/2}\sigma(Q).

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle\leq B^{1/2}\sum_{Q\in I}\sigma(Q)\leq B^{1/2}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R)))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B^{1/2}}{B^{3/4}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R)))\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))).

Therefore,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle=\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R)))-\sum_{Q\in I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))).

Next we will choose a family
J\subset{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I
satisfying

(i)

The balls
B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))
, with
Q\in J
, are pairwise disjoint.

(ii)

B^{1/4}\sum_{Q\in J}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\gtrsim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)).

Then, choosing
{\mathsf{GH}}(R)=J
we will be done. Indeed, the property (a) in the statement of the lemma is the same as (i), and the property (b) is a consequence of the fact that

J\subset I^{c}
and the definition of
I
. This also implies that
{\mathsf{GH}}(R)\subset{\mathsf{MDW}}
. Finally, the property (c) follows from (
4.13
) and (ii).

Let us see how
J
can be constructed. By the covering Theorem 9.31 from
[
To5
]
, there
is a family
J_{0}\subset{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I
such that

1)

The balls
B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))
, with
Q\in J_{0}
, have finite superposition, that is,

\sum_{Q\in J_{0}}\chi_{B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))}\leq C,

and

2)

\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))\subset\bigcup_{Q\in J_{0}}(1+8A_{0}^{-1})\,B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q)),

Actually, in Theorem 9.31 from
[
To5
]
the result above is stated for a finite family of
balls. However, it is easy to check that the same arguments work as soon as the family
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I
is countable and can be ordered so that
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I=\{Q_{1},Q_{2},\ldots\}
,
with
\ell(Q_{1})\geq\ell(Q_{2})\geq\ldots
. Further, one can check that the constant
C
in 1)
does not exceed some absolute constant times
A_{0}^{n+1}
.

From the finite superposition property 1), by rather standard arguments which are analogous to the
ones in the proof of Besicovitch’s covering theorem in
[
Ma
, Theorem 2.7]
, say,
one deduces that
J_{0}
can be split into
m_{0}
subfamilies
J_{1},\ldots,J_{m_{0}}
so that, for each
k
, the balls
\{B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q)):Q\in J_{k}\}
are pairwise disjoint, with
m_{0}\leq C(A_{0})
.

Notice that the condition 2) and Lemma
4.2
applied to
Q
ensure that

(4.14)

\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}Q\subset\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))\subset\bigcup_{Q\in J_{0}}(1+8A_{0}^{-1})\,B(e^{\prime\prime}(Q))\subset\bigcup_{Q\in J_{0}}B(e^{(8)}(Q)).

Next we choose
J:=J_{k}
to be the family such that

\sum_{Q\in J_{k}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

is maximal among
J_{1},\ldots,J_{m_{0}}
, so that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in J}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{m_{0}}\,\sum_{Q\in J_{0}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:sigmae'lesigmae}}{\geq}\frac{1}{m_{0}\,B^{1/4}}\sum_{Q\in J_{0}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{(8)}(Q)))

\displaystyle\overset{{(\ref{equni98-1})}}{\geq}\frac{1}{m_{0}\,B^{1/4}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{0}\,B^{1/4}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus I}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)).

This proves (ii).
∎

Given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we will construct now a subfamily of cubes from
{\mathsf{MDW}}
generated by
R
,
which we will denote
{\mathsf{Gen}}(R)
, by iterating the construction of Lemma
4.4
.
The algorithm goes as follows.
Given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we denote

{\mathsf{Gen}}_{0}(R)=\{R\}.

If
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
, we set
{\mathsf{Gen}}_{1}(R)=\varnothing
, and otherwise

{\mathsf{Gen}}_{1}(R)={\mathsf{GH}}(R),

where
{\mathsf{GH}}(R)
is defined in Lemma
4.4
.
For
j\geq 2
, we set

{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R)=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Trc}}}{\mathsf{GH}}(Q).

For
j\geq 0
, we also set

{\mathsf{Trc}}_{j}(R)={\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Trc}},

and

{\mathsf{Gen}}(R)=\bigcup_{j\geq 0}{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R),\qquad{\mathsf{Trc}}(R)=\bigcup_{j\geq 0}{\mathsf{Trc}}_{j}(R).

Lemma 4.5
.

For
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we have

(4.15)

\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}(R)}Q\subset\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}(R)}Q\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R)).

Also,

(4.16)

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}B^{-j/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{j}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

Proof.

The first inclusion in (
4.15
) holds because
{\mathsf{Trc}}(R)\subset{\mathsf{Gen}}(R)
. So we only have to show the second inclusion.

By construction, for any
R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
,
{\mathsf{GH}}(R^{\prime})\subset{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R^{\prime}))
, and thus any
Q\in{\mathsf{GH}}(R^{\prime})
is contained in
e^{\prime}(R^{\prime})
. This implies that

|x_{R^{\prime}}-x_{Q}|\leq r(B(e^{\prime}(R^{\prime})))+\frac{1}{2}\,\ell(Q)\leq\Big(1+2A_{0}^{-1}+\frac{1}{2}\,A_{0}^{-1}\Big)\ell(R^{\prime})\leq 1.1\,\ell(R^{\prime}).

Then, given
Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R)
,
x\in Q
, and
0\leq k\leq j
, if we denote by
R_{k}
the cube from
{\mathsf{Gen}}_{k}(R)
such that
Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-k}(R_{k})
, we have

\displaystyle|x_{R}-x|

\displaystyle\leq|x_{R}-x_{R_{1}}|+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}|x_{R_{k}}-x_{R_{k+1}}|+|x_{Q}-x|

\displaystyle\leq r(B(e^{\prime}(R)))+\frac{1}{2}\,A_{0}^{-1}\,\ell(R)+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}1.1\,A_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)+\frac{1}{2}\,A_{0}^{-1}\,\ell(R)

\displaystyle\leq r(B(e^{\prime}(R)))+2\,A_{0}^{-1}\,\ell(R),

which shows that
Q\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R))
.

To prove the second statement in the lemma, observe that, for
Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Trc}}
,
by (
4.12
) applied to
Q
we have

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{GH}}(Q)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(P)))\geq c\,B^{3/4}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\geq B^{1/2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))),

assuming
\Lambda
, and thus
B
, big enough. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(P))

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Trc}}}\,\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{GH}}(Q)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(P)))

\displaystyle\geq B^{1/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Trc}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

So,

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\leq\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{j-1}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))+B^{-1/2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(P))).

Iterating this estimate, and taking into account that, by the polynomial growth of
\mu
,

{\mathsf{Gen}}_{j-1}(R)=\varnothing
for some large
j
, we get (
4.16
).
∎

5.
The Riesz transform on the tractable trees: the approximating measures
\eta
,
\nu
, and the variational argument

In this section,
for a given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is tractable (i.e.,
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
),
we will define a suitable measure
\eta
that approximates
\mu
at the level of the cubes from

{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and we will estimate
\|{\mathcal{R}}\eta\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}
from below. To this end,
we will apply a variational argument in
L^{p}
by techniques inspired by

[
RT
]
and
[
JNRT
]
. In the next section we will transfer these
estimates to
{\mathcal{R}}\mu
.

5.1.
The suppressed Riesz transform and a Cotlar type inequality

Let
\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to[0,\infty)
be a
1
-Lipschitz function.
Below we will need to work with the suppressed Riesz kernel

(5.1)

K_{\Phi}(x,y)=\frac{x-y}{\bigl(|x-y|^{2}+\Phi(x)\Phi(y)\bigr)^{(n+1)/2}}

and the associated operator

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)=\int K_{\Phi}(x,y)\,d\alpha(y),

where
\alpha
is a signed measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
.
For a positive measure
\omega
and
f\in L^{1}_{loc}(\omega)
, we write
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\omega}f={\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}(f\,\omega)
.
The kernel
K_{\Phi}
(or a variant of this) appeared for the first
time in the work of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg in connection with Vitushkin’s conjecture (see

[
Vo
]
).
This is a Calderón-Zygmund kernel which satisfies the properties:

(5.2)

|K_{\Phi}(x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\big(|x-y|+\Phi(x)+\Phi(y)\big)^{n}}

and

(5.3)

|\nabla_{x}K_{\Phi}(x,y)|+|\nabla_{y}K_{\Phi}(x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\big(|x-y|+\Phi(x)+\Phi(y)\big)^{n+1}}

for all
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
.

Also, if
{\varepsilon}\approx\Phi(x)
, then we have

(5.4)

\bigl|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\varepsilon}}\alpha(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)\bigr|\lesssim\sup_{r>\Phi(x)}\frac{|\alpha|(B(x,r))}{r^{n}},

with the implicit constant in the inequality depending on the implicit constant in the comparability
{\varepsilon}\approx\Phi(x)
.
See Lemmas 5.4 and 5.5 in
[
To5
]
.

The following result is an easy consequence of a
Tb
theorem of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg.
See Chapter 5 of
[
To5
]
, for example. We will use this to prove (
5.39
).

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\omega
be a Radon measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
and let
\Phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to[0,\infty)
be a
1
-Lipschitz function. Suppose that

(a)

\omega(B(x,r))\leq c\,r^{n}
for all
r\geq\Phi(x)
, and

(b)

\sup_{{\varepsilon}>\Phi(x)}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\omega(x)|\leq C
.

Then
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\omega}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\omega)
, for
1<p<\infty
, with a bound on its norm depending only on
p
,
c
and

C
. In particular,
{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\omega)
on the set
\{x:\Phi(x)=0\}
.

We define the energy
W_{\omega}
(with respect to
\omega
) of a set
F\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
as

W_{\omega}(F)=\iint_{F\times F}\frac{1}{{\rm diam}(F)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\omega(x)d\omega(y).

We say that a ball
B\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
is
(a,b)
-doubling

\omega(aB)\leq b\,\omega(B).

We denote by
{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}f
the usual centered maximal Hardy-Littlewood operator applied to
f
:

{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}f(x)=\sup_{r>0}\,\frac{1}{\omega(B(x,r))}\int_{B(x,r)}|f|\,d\omega,

and by
{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}^{(r_{0},a,b)}f
the version

{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}^{(r_{0},a,b)}f(x)=\sup\frac{1}{\omega(B(x,r))}\int_{B(x,r)}|f|\,d\omega,

where the
\sup
is taken over all radii
r>r_{0}
such that the ball
B(x,r)
is
(a,b)
-doubling.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
x\in R
,
r_{0}>0
, and
\theta_{1}>0
.
Suppose that for all
r\geq r_{0}

\theta_{\omega}(x,r)\leq\theta_{1}

and

W_{\omega}(B(x,r))\leq\theta_{1}\,\omega(B(x,r))\qquad\mbox{if $B(x,r)$ is $(16,128^{n+2})$-doubling.}

Then

(5.5)

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\geq r_{0}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\omega(x)|\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}^{(r_{0},16,128^{n+2})}({\mathcal{R}}\omega)(x)+\theta_{1}.

Proof.

For a given
{\varepsilon}\geq r_{0}
, let
k\geq 0
be minimal so that, for
r=128^{k}\,{\varepsilon}
, the ball
B(x,r)
is
(128,128^{n+2})
-doubling (in particular, this implies that
B(x,8r)
is
(16,128^{n+2})
-doubling). It is easy to see that such
k
exists using the assumption
\theta_{\omega}(x,r)\leq\theta_{1}
.
By a standard estimate (see Lemma 2.20 from
[
To5
]
), it follows that

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\omega(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)|+C\,\frac{\omega(B(x,8r))}{(8r)^{n}}\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)|+C\,\theta_{1}.

It is immediate to check that for any
x^{\prime}\in B(x,2r)
,

(5.6)

|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)-{\mathcal{R}}\chi_{B(x,4r)^{c}}\omega(x^{\prime})|\lesssim\theta_{1}.

Consider radial
C^{1}
functions
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
such that

\chi_{B(x,4r)}\leq\psi_{1}\leq\chi_{B(x,8r)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\chi_{B(x,r)}\leq\psi_{2}\leq\chi_{B(x,2r)},

and
\text{Lip}(\psi_{1})\leq r,\ \text{Lip}(\psi_{2})\leq r.
Given a function
f\in L^{1}_{loc}(\omega)
, denote by
m_{\psi_{2}\omega}f
the
(\psi_{2}\,\omega)
-mean of
f
, i.e.,

m_{\psi_{2}\omega}f=\frac{1}{\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{1}(\omega)}}\int f\,\psi_{2}\,d\omega.

Notice that

\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}({\mathcal{R}}\omega)\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{\omega(B(x,r))}\int_{B(x,2r)}|{\mathcal{R}}\omega|\,d\omega\approx\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2r)}|{\mathcal{R}}\omega|\,d\omega\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}^{(r_{0},16,128^{n+2})}({\mathcal{R}}\omega)(x).

From (
5.6
) we deduce that

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)-m_{\psi_{2}\omega}\bigl({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{B(x,4r)^{c}}\omega)\bigr)\big|\leq m_{\psi_{2}\omega}\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)-{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{B(x,4r)^{c}}\omega)|\bigr)\lesssim\theta_{1}.

Then we have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}_{8r}\omega(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\theta_{1}+|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{B(x,4r)^{c}}\omega)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\theta_{1}+\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}\bigl({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{B(x,4r)^{c}}\omega)-{\mathcal{R}}((1-\psi_{1})\omega)\bigr)\bigr|+\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}\bigl({\mathcal{R}}(\psi_{1}\omega)\bigr)\bigr|+\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}({\mathcal{R}}\omega)\bigr|

\displaystyle\lesssim\theta_{1}+\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}\omega}\bigl({\mathcal{R}}(\psi_{1}\omega)\bigr)\bigr|+{\mathcal{M}}_{\omega}^{(r_{0},16,128^{n+2})}({\mathcal{R}}\omega)(x).

To estimate the middle term on the right hand side we use the antisymmetry of
{\mathcal{R}}
:

\displaystyle\bigl|m_{\psi_{2}}\bigl({\mathcal{R}}(\psi_{1}\omega)\bigr)\bigr|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{1}(\omega)}}\left|\iint K(y-z)\,\psi_{1}(y)\,\psi_{2}(z)\,d\omega(y)\,d\omega(z)\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{1}(\omega)}}\left|\iint K(y-z)\,\bigl(\psi_{1}(y)\,\psi_{2}(z)-\psi_{1}(z)\,\psi_{2}(y)\bigr)\,d\omega(y)\,d\omega(z)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\omega(B(x,r))}\iint_{B(x,8r)\times B(x,8r)}\!\frac{1}{r\,|y-z|^{n-1}}\,d\omega(y)\,d\omega(z)\approx\frac{W_{\omega}(B(x,8r))}{\omega(B(x,8r))}\lesssim\theta_{1}.

∎

Remark 5.3
.

In fact, the proof of the preceding lemma shows that, given any measure
\omega
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
and

{\varepsilon}>0
,

(5.7)

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\omega(x)|\lesssim\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}|{\mathcal{R}}\omega|\,d\omega+\sup_{r\geq{\varepsilon}}\frac{\omega(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}+\frac{W_{\omega}(B(x,8{\varepsilon}^{\prime}))}{\omega(B(x,8{\varepsilon}^{\prime}))},

where
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}=2^{7k}{\varepsilon}
, with
k\geq 0
minimal such that the ball
B(x,{\varepsilon}^{\prime})
is
(128,128^{n+2})
-doubling.

5.2.
The family
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and the approximating measure
\eta

In the remaining of this section we fix a cube
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is tractable.

We need to define some regularized family of ending cubes for
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
First, let

d_{R}(x)=\inf_{Q\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))}\big({\rm dist}(x,Q)+\ell(Q)\big).

Notice that
d_{R}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function.
Given
0<\ell_{0}\ll\ell(R)
, we denote

(5.8)

d_{R,\ell_{0}}(x)=\max\big(\ell_{0},d_{R}(x)\big),

which is also
1
-Lipschitz.
For each
x\in e^{\prime}(R)
we take the largest cube
Q_{x}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}

such that
x\in Q_{x}
with

(5.9)

\ell(Q_{x})\leq\frac{1}{60}\,\inf_{y\in Q_{x}}d_{R,\ell_{0}}(y).

We consider the collection of the different cubes
Q_{x}
,
x\in e^{\prime}(R)
, and we denote it by
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
(this stands for “regularized cubes”).

The constant
\ell_{0}
is just an auxiliary parameter that prevents
\ell(Q_{x})
from vanishing. Eventually
\ell_{0}
will be taken extremely small. In particular, we assume
\ell_{0}
small enough
so that

(5.10)

\mu\bigg(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)):\ell(Q)\geq\ell_{0}}Q\bigg)\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\mu\bigg(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))}Q\bigg).

We let
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
be the family of cubes made up of
R
and all the cubes of the next generations which are contained in
e^{\prime}(R)
but are not
strictly contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

Lemma 5.4
.

The cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
are pairwise disjoint and satisfy the following properties:

(a)

If
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and
x\in B(x_{P},50\ell(P))
, then
10\,\ell(P)\leq d_{R,\ell_{0}}(x)\leq c\,\ell(P)
,
where
c
is some constant depending only on
n
.

(b)

There exists some absolute constant
c>0
such that if
P,\,P^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
satisfy
B(x_{P},50\ell(P))\cap B(x_{P^{\prime}},50\ell(P^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing
, then

c^{-1}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq c\,\ell(P).

(c)

For each
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
, there are at most
C
cubes
P^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
such that

B(x_{P},50\ell(P))\cap B(x_{P^{\prime}},50\ell(P^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing,

where
C
is some absolute constant.

The proof of this lemma is standard. See for example
[
To4
, Lemma 6.6]
.

Next we define a measure
\eta
which, in a sense, approximates
\mu{\lfloor}_{e^{\prime}(R)}
at the level of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
We let

\eta=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))}\mu(P)\frac{{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}{\lfloor}_{\tfrac{1}{2}B(P)}}{{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}(\tfrac{1}{2}B(P))},

where
{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}
stands for the Lebesgue measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
.
With each
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{Reg}}}(e^{\prime}(R))
we associate another “cube”
Q^{(\eta)}
defined as follows:

Q^{(\eta)}=\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R)):P\subset Q}\tfrac{1}{2}B(P).

Further, we consider a lattice
{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}
associated with the measure
\eta
which is made up of the cubes

Q^{(\eta)}
with
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{Reg}}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and other cubes which are descendants of the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
We assume that
{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}
satisfies the first two properties of Lemma
2.1
with the same parameters
A_{0}
and
C_{0}
as
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
. It is straightforward to check that
{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}
can be constructed in this way.
For
S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}
such that
S=Q^{(\eta)}
with
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{Reg}}}(e^{\prime}(R))
, we let
Q=S^{(\mu)}
. Further, we write

\ell(S):=\ell(Q)
,
B_{S}:=B_{Q}
, and
\Theta(S):=\Theta(Q)
.

5.3.
The auxiliary family
{\mathsf{H}}

Given
p\geq 1
and a family
I\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
, we denote

\sigma_{p}(I)=\sum_{P\in I}\Theta(P)^{p}\,\mu(P),

so that
\sigma(I)=\sigma_{2}(I)
.
Recall that

{\mathsf{HD}}(R)={\mathsf{hd}}^{k_{\Lambda}}(R),

and that

{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))={\mathsf{Stop}}(e(R))\cap{\mathsf{HD}}(R),\qquad{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))={\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{HD}}(R).

For
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
and
j\geq 0
, denote

{\mathsf{H}}_{j}(e^{\prime}(R))={\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{hd}}^{k_{\Lambda}+j}(R),

so that
{\mathsf{H}}_{0}(e^{\prime}(R))={\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

Remark 5.5
.

Note that for
j>k_{\Lambda}+2
we have
{\mathsf{H}}_{j}(e^{\prime}(R))=\varnothing
. Indeed, by the definition of
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
, for each
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
there exists
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
such that
2B_{Q}\subset 2B_{P}
and
\ell(Q)\leq\ell(P)\leq A_{0}^{2}\ell(Q)
. Thus,

\frac{\mu(2B_{Q})}{\ell(Q)^{n}}\leq\frac{\mu(2B_{P})}{\ell(Q)^{n}}\leq A_{0}^{2n}\frac{\mu(2B_{P})}{\ell(P)^{n}}.

Since for each
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
we have
\Theta(P)\leq\Lambda^{2}\Theta(R)
, it follows that
\Theta(Q)\leq A_{0}^{2n}\Lambda^{2}\Theta(R)
.

The fact that
\max_{j\geq 0}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{j}(e^{\prime}(R)))
may be much larger than
\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))
may cause problems in some estimates. For this reason,
we need to introduce an auxiliary family
{\mathsf{H}}
. We deal with this issue in this section.

Recall that, for
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu,k}
,

e(R)=e_{h(R)}(R)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad e^{\prime}(R)=e_{h(R)+1},

where

e_{i}(R)=R\cup\bigcup Q,

with the union running over the cubes
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu,k+1}
such that

{\rm dist}(x_{R},Q)<\frac{\ell(R)}{2}+2i\ell(Q).

For
j\geq 0
, we set

e_{i,j}(R)=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu,k+1}:Q\subset e_{i}(R)}e_{j}(Q),

and we let
{\mathsf{H}}_{k}(e_{i,j}(R))
be the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathsf{H}}_{k}(e^{\prime}(R))
which are contained
in
e_{i,j}(R)
.

From now on, we let
{\varepsilon}_{n}
be some positive constant depending just on
n
.
The precise value of
{\varepsilon}_{n}
is chosen at the end of Section
6
(see (
6.62
)).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
p\in(1,2]
.
For any
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
there exist some
j,k
, with
10\leq j\leq A_{0}/4
and
0\leq k\leq k_{\Lambda}+2
such that

(5.11)

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{m}(e_{h(R),j+1}(R)))\leq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k}(e_{h(R),j}(R)))\quad\mbox{for all $m\geq 0$,}

assuming
A_{0}
big enough (possibly depending on
n
).

Proof.

For each
j
, we denote by
0\leq k_{j}\leq k_{\Lambda}+2
the integer such that

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j}}(e_{h(R),j}(R)))=\max_{0\leq k\leq k_{\Lambda}+2}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k}(e_{h(R),j}(R))).

The lemma can be rephrased in the following way: there exists
10\leq j\leq A_{0}/4
such that

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j+1}}(e_{h(R),j+1}(R)))\leq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j}}(e_{h(R),j}(R))).

We prove this by contradiction. Suppose the estimate above fails for all
10\leq j\leq A_{0}/4
, and let
j_{0}
be the largest integer smaller than
A_{0}/4
.
Then we have

(5.12)

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j_{0}}}(e_{h(R),j_{0}}(R)))\geq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j_{0}-1}}(e_{h(R),j_{0}-1}(R)))\\
\geq\ldots\geq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}(j_{0}-10)}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{10}}(e_{h(R),10}(R)))\geq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}(j_{0}-10)}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{0}(e_{h(R),10}(R)))\\
\geq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}(j_{0}-10)}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{0}(e_{h(R)}(R)))\overset{{(\ref{eql00})}}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}(j_{0}-10)}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

Concerning the left hand side of the inequality above, since
e_{h(R),j_{0}}(R)\subset 2R
and
k_{j_{0}}\leq k_{\Lambda}+2
, we have

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j_{0}}}(e_{h(R),j_{0}}(R)))\leq A_{0}^{2np}\Lambda^{2p}\,\Theta(R)^{p}\mu(2R).

Due to the fact that
R
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, as in (
4.7
) we have

\mu(2R)\leq C_{0}\,C_{d}\,A_{0}^{n+1}\mu(R).
Thus,

(5.13)

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k_{j_{0}}}(e_{h(R),j_{0}}(R)))\leq C_{0}\,C_{d}\,A_{0}^{2np+n+1}\Lambda^{2p}\sigma_{p}(R).

Concerning the right hand side of (
5.12
), observe that, denoting
\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})=\Lambda\Theta(R)=\Theta(Q)
for any
Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))
, we have

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))=\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p-2}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))\overset{\eqref{eq:MDWdef2}}{\geq}B^{-1}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p-2}\sigma(R)\geq\Lambda^{-1}\Lambda^{p-2}\sigma_{p}(R).

We deduce from (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and the above, that

\frac{1}{2}\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}(j_{0}-10)}\Lambda^{p-3}\sigma_{p}(R)\leq C_{0}\,C_{d}\,A_{0}^{2np+n+1}\Lambda^{2p}\sigma_{p}(R).

Since
\Lambda\geq A_{0}^{n}
and
j_{0}\approx A_{0}
, it is clear that this inequality is violated if
A_{0}
is big
enough, depending just on
n
.
∎

Let
j(R),\,k(R)
be such that
10\leq j(R)\leq A_{0}/4
,
0\leq k(R)\leq k_{\Lambda}+2
and

\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{m}(e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R)))\leq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}_{k(R)}(e_{h(R),j(R)}(R)))\quad\mbox{for all $m\geq 0$,}

We denote

{\mathcal{S}}_{\mu}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:Q\subset e_{h(R),j(R)}(R)}Q,\qquad{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:Q\subset e_{h(R),j(R)}(R)}\tfrac{1}{2}B(Q)

and

{\mathcal{S}}_{\mu}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:Q\subset e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R)}Q,\qquad{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:Q\subset e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R)}\tfrac{1}{2}B(Q).

Notice that, by construction,

(5.14)

{\rm dist}(\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus e^{\prime}(R),{\mathcal{S}}_{\mu}^{\prime})\geq cA_{0}^{-1}\ell(R),

where
c>0
is an absolute constant.

For
m=1,2,3,4
, denote by
V_{m}
the
mA_{0}^{-3}\ell(R)
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}
.
Let
{\varphi}_{R}
be a
C^{1}
function which equals
1
in
V_{3}
, vanishes out of
V_{4}
, and such that
\|{\varphi}_{R}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and

\|\nabla{\varphi}_{R}\|_{\infty}\leq 2A_{0}^{3}\ell(R)^{-1}
.
Observe that, for
x\in\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus e^{\prime}(R)
,
{\rm dist}(x,V_{4})\gtrsim\ell(R)
.
In fact, from (
5.14
) one can derive that

(5.15)

{\rm dist}(Q,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus e^{\prime}(R))\gtrsim\ell(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))$ such that
$B_{Q}\cap V_{4}\neq\varnothing$,}

taking into account that
\ell(Q)\leq\frac{A_{0}^{-1}}{60}\,\ell(R)
for every
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

We consider the measure

\nu={\varphi}_{R}\,\eta

and the function

G(x)=2A_{0}^{3}\int_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}\setminus V_{2}}\frac{1}{\ell(R)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\eta(y).

Notice that

G(x)\lesssim\Theta(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in V_{1}$.}

To shorten notation, we write

{\mathsf{H}}={\mathsf{H}}_{k(R)}(e_{h(R),j(R)}(R)),\qquad{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{H}}_{k(R)}(e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R))

and

H=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{H}}}Q^{(\eta)},\qquad H^{\prime}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}Q^{(\eta)}.

We also set

\Theta({\mathsf{H}})=A_{0}^{(k_{\Lambda}+k(R))n}\,\Theta(R),

so that for any
Q\in{\mathsf{H}}
we have

\Theta(Q)=\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

5.4.
Some technical lemmas

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
A\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
be the set of those
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
which belong to some

(16,128^{n+2})
-doubling (with respect to
\nu
) ball
B\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
such that

W_{\nu}(B)\geq M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\nu(B)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\theta_{\eta}(\gamma B)\leq c_{1}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\quad\mbox{for all $\gamma\geq 1$.}

Then, for
M\geq 1
big enough,

\nu(A)\lesssim\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H).

Proof.

Observe first that, for any ball
B\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
with
r(B)\in[A_{0}^{-k-1},A_{0}^{-k}]
,

\displaystyle W_{\nu}(B)

\displaystyle\lesssim\theta_{\nu}(B)\,\nu(B)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\geq k+1}\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta,j}:Q\subset 2B}\int_{x\in Q}\int_{y:A_{0}^{-j-2}<|x-y|\leq A_{0}^{-j-1}}\frac{1}{r(B)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\nu(x)\,d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B)}\,\theta_{\nu}(2B_{Q})\,\nu(Q).

To prove the lemma, we apply Vitali’s
5r
-covering lemma to get a family of
(16,128^{n+2})
-doubling balls
B_{i}
,
i\in I
,
which satisfy the following:

•

the balls
2B_{i}
,
i\in I
, are pairwise disjoint,

•

A\subset\bigcup_{i\in I}10B_{i}
,

•

W_{\nu}(B_{i})\geq M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\nu(B_{i})
and
\theta_{\eta}(\gamma B_{i})\leq c_{1}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})
and for all
i\in I
and
\gamma\geq 1
.

Then we deduce

(5.16)

\displaystyle\nu(A)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in I}\nu(10B_{i})\lesssim\sum_{i\in I}\nu(B_{i})\leq\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{i\in I}W_{\nu}(B_{i})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\nu}(2B_{Q})\,\nu(Q).

Now we take into account that all the cubes
Q
which are not contained in any cube
P
with
P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}

satisfy
\theta_{\nu}(2B_{Q})\leq\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})
. Then, for each
i\in I
,

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\nu}(2B_{Q})\,\nu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}\,\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\,\eta(Q)

\displaystyle\quad+\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\nu(Q).

Observe that the last term on the right hand side is bounded above by
\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\nu(2B_{i})\approx\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\nu(B_{i})
.
So plugging the previous estimate into (
5.16
), we get

\displaystyle\nu(A)\lesssim\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}\,\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\,\eta(Q)+\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i\in I}\nu(B_{i}).

Since
B_{i}\subset A
for each
i
, the last term is at most
\frac{1}{2}\nu(A)
for
M
big enough. Thus,

(5.17)

\nu(A)\lesssim\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}\,\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\,\eta(Q).

To estimate the term on the right hand side above, we denote by
{\mathsf{F}}_{k}
the family of cubes from

{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}
which are contained in some cube
Q^{(\eta)}
with
Q\in{\mathsf{H}}_{k}^{\prime}:={\mathsf{H}}_{k}(e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R))
and are not contained
in any cube
P^{(\eta)}
with
P\in{\mathsf{H}}_{k+1}^{\prime}:={\mathsf{H}}_{k+1}(e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R))
. Notice that

\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k+1})\approx\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k}),

where
\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})=\Theta(Q^{\prime})
for
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{H}}_{k}^{\prime}
(this does not depend on the specific cube
Q^{\prime}
). Then, for each
i\in I
, we have

(5.18)

\displaystyle\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\eta}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\eta(Q)

\displaystyle=\!\!\sum_{k\geq k(R)}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{k}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\theta_{\eta}(2B_{Q})\eta(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\geq k(R)}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{k}:Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\,\eta(Q).

We claim now that for
Q
in the last sum, we have

(5.19)

\ell(Q)\lesssim A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))}\,r(B_{i}).

To check this, let
P(Q,k)\in{\mathsf{H}}_{k}^{\prime}
be such that
Q\subset P(Q,k)
. From the condition

(5.20)

\theta_{\eta}(\gamma B_{i})\leq c_{1}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\quad\mbox{for all $\gamma\geq 1$}

and the fact that
P(Q,k)\cap 2B_{i}\neq\varnothing
(because
Q\subset 2B_{i}
) we infer that

r(B_{i})\geq\ell(P(Q,k))
for
k
big enough. Otherwise we would find a ball
\gamma B_{i}
containing
P(Q,k)
with radius comparable to
\ell(P(Q,k))
, so that

\theta_{\eta}(\gamma B_{i})\geq c\,\Theta(P(Q,k))>c_{1}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})

for
k
big enough (depending on
c_{1}
), contradicting (
5.20
). So we have
P(Q,k)\subset 6B_{i}
and thus

c_{1}\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\geq\theta_{\eta}(6B_{i})\gtrsim\frac{\ell(P(Q,k))^{n}}{r(B_{i})^{n}}\,\Theta(P(Q,k))=\frac{\ell(P(Q,k))^{n}}{r(B_{i})^{n}}\,A_{0}^{n(k-k(R))}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

This gives

\ell(Q)\leq\ell(P(Q,k))\lesssim A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))}\,r(B_{i})

and proves (
5.19
) for
k
big enough, and thus for all
k
if we choose the implicit constant in (
5.19
) suitably.

From (
5.19
) we deduce that the right hand side of (
5.18
)
does not exceed

\sum_{k\geq k(R)}A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))/2}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{Q\subset 2B_{i}\cap P}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{r(B_{i})}\right)^{1/2}\,\eta(Q)\\
\lesssim\sum_{k\geq k(R)}A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))/2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\eta(2B_{i}\cap P).

Plugging this estimate into (
5.17
) and recalling that the balls
2B_{i}
are disjoint, we get

\displaystyle\nu(A)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{k\geq k(R)}A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))/2}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\,\eta(2B_{i}\cap P)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{k\geq k(R)}A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))/2}\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\,\eta(P).

By Hölder’s inequality, for each
k\geq k(R)
we have

\sum_{P^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}_{k})\eta(P)\leq\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k})^{1/p}\,\eta(H^{\prime})^{1/p^{\prime}}.

We can estimate the right hand side using (
5.11
):

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}),

\displaystyle\eta(H^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}^{\prime})}{\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}}\leq\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\frac{\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})}{\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}}=\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\eta(H).

Thus,

\nu(A)\lesssim\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}\sum_{k\geq k(R)}A_{0}^{-(k-k(R))/2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})^{1/p}\,\eta(H)^{1/p^{\prime}}\approx\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\eta(H)\approx\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H).

∎

Let
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}
denote the family of all cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}

made up of
R
and all the cubes of the next generations which are contained in
e_{h(R),j(R)+1}(R)
but are not
strictly contained in any cube from from
{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}
. We consider the function

(5.21)

\Psi(x)=\inf_{Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}}\big(\ell(Q)+{\rm dist}(x,Q)\big).

Notice that
\Psi
is a
1
-Lipschitz function. We also have the following result, which will be proven
by quite standard arguments.

Lemma 5.8
.

For all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
, we have

(5.22)

\sup_{r\geq\Psi(x)}\frac{\nu(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}\leq\sup_{r\geq\Psi(x)}\frac{\eta(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}$.}

Proof.

The first inequality in (
5.22
) is trivial. Concerning the second one,
in the case
r>\ell(R)/10
we just use that

\eta(B(x,r))\leq\mu(e^{\prime}(R))\lesssim\mu(R)\lesssim\Theta(R)\,\ell(R)^{n}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,r^{n}.

So we may assume that
\Psi(x)<r\leq\ell(R)/10
.
By the definition of
\Psi(x)
, there exists
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}
such that

\ell(Q)+{\rm dist}(x,Q)\leq r.

Therefore,
B_{Q}\subset B(x,4r)
and so there exists an ancestor
Q^{\prime}\supset Q
which belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{H}}^{\prime}}
such that
B(x,r)\subset 2B_{Q^{\prime}}
, with
\ell(Q^{\prime})\approx r
.
Then,
\Theta(Q^{\prime})\leq\Theta({\mathsf{H}})
and

\eta(B(x,r))\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R)):P^{(\eta)}\cap 2B_{Q^{\prime}}\neq\varnothing}\eta(P^{(\eta)})=\sum_{P^{(\eta)}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R)):P\cap 2B_{Q^{\prime}}\neq\varnothing}\mu(P).

Observe now that if
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
satisfies
P^{(\eta)}\cap 2B_{Q^{\prime}}\neq\varnothing
, then
\ell(P)\lesssim\ell(Q^{\prime})
(this is an easy consequence of Lemma
5.4
(b) and the fact that
Q^{\prime}
contains some cube from
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
). So we deduce that

P\subset CB_{Q^{\prime}}
. Hence,

\eta(B(x,r))\leq\mu(CB_{Q^{\prime}})\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\ell(Q^{\prime})^{n}\approx\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,r^{n}.

∎

In the next subsection we are going to use a variational argument to show that for some constant
c_{0}
, depending at most
2
2

2

The constant
c_{0}

will be chosen of the form
c_{0}=A_{0}^{C(n)}
, and it will not depend on
\Lambda
,
{\varepsilon}_{n}
, or other
parameters of the construction.
on
n
and
A_{0}
, we have

\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)|-G(x)-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\nu(x)\gtrsim\Lambda^{-p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

See Lemma
5.13
for details. We prove this estimate by contradiction, so that in particular we will assume that

(5.23)

\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)|-G(x)-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\nu(x)\leq\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Below we prove a few consequences of (
5.23
) that will be useful in the proof of Lemma
5.13
.

We denote

{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(x)=\sup_{{\varepsilon}>\Psi(x)}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)\big|.

Lemma 5.9
.

Suppose that (
5.23
) holds. Then,

(5.24)

\nu\big(\big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\,{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(x)>M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\big\}\big)\leq C_{2}\,\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H).

Proof.

Recall that we denote by
V_{1}
the
A_{0}^{-3}\ell(R)
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}

and that

G(x)\lesssim\Theta(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in V_{1}$.}

Then the assumption in the lemma implies that

(5.25)

\int_{V_{1}}\big|({\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)-2c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}|^{p}\,d\nu(x)\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

By Remark
5.3
, for any
x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}
and
{\varepsilon}>\Psi(x)
,

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\lesssim\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}|{\mathcal{R}}\nu|\,d\nu+\sup_{r\geq{\varepsilon}}\frac{\nu(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}+\frac{W_{\nu}(B(x,8{\varepsilon}^{\prime}))}{\nu(B(x,8{\varepsilon}^{\prime}))},

where
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}=2^{7k}{\varepsilon}
, with
k\geq 0
minimal such that the ball
B(x,{\varepsilon}^{\prime})
is
(128,128^{n+2})
-doubling.
In the case
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{2}A_{0}^{-3}\,\ell(R)
, by standard arguments,

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}\nu(x)|+C\,\frac{\nu(B(x,{\varepsilon}^{\prime}))}{({\varepsilon}^{\prime})^{n}}\leq C\,\Theta(R)<M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

for
\Lambda
(or
M
) big enough.

In the case
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}A_{0}^{-3}\ell(R)
, we have
B(x,2{\varepsilon}^{\prime})\subset V_{1}
and thus

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}(\chi_{V_{1}}{\mathcal{R}}\nu)(x)+\sup_{r\geq\Psi(x)}\frac{\nu(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}+\sup_{r\in D(x):r\geq\Psi(x)}\\
\frac{W_{\nu}(B(x,r))}{\nu(B(x,r))},

where
D(x)
denotes the set of radii
r>0
such that
B(x,r)
is
(16,128^{n+2})
-doubling.
Also, as shown in (
5.22
),

\sup_{r\geq\Psi(x)}\frac{\nu(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

We deduce that in any case (i.e., for any
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
), assuming
M
larger than some absolute constant,

\displaystyle\nu\big(\big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\,{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(x)

\displaystyle>M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\big\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\nu\Big(\Big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}(\chi_{V_{1}}{\mathcal{R}}\nu)(x)>\frac{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}{3}\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\!\!+\nu\Big(\Big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\!\!\!\sup_{r\in D(x):r\geq\Psi(x)}\!\frac{W_{\nu}(B(x,r))}{\nu(B(x,r))}>\frac{M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})}{3}\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}.

To deal with the term
T_{1}
, notice that if
{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}(\chi_{V_{1}}{\mathcal{R}}\nu)(x)>\frac{M}{3}\Theta({\mathsf{H}})
, then

{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}((|\chi_{V_{1}}{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-\frac{M}{6}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+})(x)>\frac{M}{6}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

and thus using the weak
L^{p}(\nu)
boundedness of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}
and (
5.25
) we obtain, assuming
M
big enough,

T_{1}\leq\nu\Big(\Big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\nu}^{(\Psi(x),16,128^{n+2})}((|\chi_{V_{1}}{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-\frac{M}{6}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+})(x)>\frac{M}{6}\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\Big\}\Big)\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{(M\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))^{p}}\int_{V_{1}}\big|({\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)-\frac{M}{6}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}|^{p}\,d\nu\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{p}}\,\nu(H).

Concerning
T_{2}
, by Lemma
5.7
,

T_{2}\lesssim\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H).

So we have

\nu\big(\big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}:\,{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(x)>M\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\big\}\big)\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{p}}\,\nu(H)+\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H)\lesssim\frac{\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}{M}\,\nu(H).

∎

For
\gamma>1
, we let

Z(\gamma)=\big\{x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(x)>\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\big\}.

Notice that, under the assumption (
5.23
), by Lemma
5.9

(with
M=\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}
), we have

(5.26)

\nu(Z(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\leq C_{2}\gamma^{-1}\,\nu(H).

For
x\in Z(\gamma)
, let

(5.27)

e(x,\gamma)=\sup\{{\varepsilon}:{\varepsilon}>\Psi(x),\,|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|>\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\}

We define the exceptional set
Z^{\prime}(\gamma)
as

Z^{\prime}(\gamma)=\bigcup_{x\in Z(\gamma)}B(x,e(x,\gamma)).

The next lemma shows that
\nu(Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})
is small if
\gamma
is taken big enough.

Lemma 5.10
.

If
y\in Z^{\prime}(\gamma)
, then
{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(y)>(\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}-c_{2})\Theta({\mathsf{H}})
,
for some
c_{2}>0
.
Thus, under the assumption (
5.23
),
if
\gamma\geq 2c_{2}
, then

(5.28)

\nu(Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\leq\frac{2C_{2}}{\gamma}\,\nu(H).

Proof.

The arguments are similar to the ones in Lemma 5.2 from

[
To5
]
. However, for the reader’s convenience we will explain here the basic details.

By standard arguments (which just use the fact that the Riesz kernel is a Calderón-Zygmund kernel),
for all
y\in B(x,e(x,\gamma))
, with
x\in Z(\gamma)
,
we have

|{\mathcal{R}}_{e(x,\gamma)}\nu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{2e(x,\gamma)}\nu(y)|\lesssim\sup_{r\geq e(x,\gamma)}\frac{\nu(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

taking into account that
e(x,\gamma)\geq\Psi(x)
and recalling (
5.22
) for the last estimate.
So we have

|{\mathcal{R}}_{2e(x,\gamma)}\nu(y)|\geq|{\mathcal{R}}_{e(x,\gamma)}\nu(x)|-c_{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\geq\gamma\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})-c_{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

Observe now that

\Psi(y)\leq\Psi(x)+|x-y|<2e(x,\gamma).

So

{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(y)\geq|{\mathcal{R}}_{2e(x,\gamma)}\nu(y)|>(\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}-c_{2})\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

which proves the first statement of the lemma.

In particular, taking
\gamma\geq 2c_{2}
, from the last estimate we derive

{\mathcal{R}}_{*,\Psi}\nu(y)\geq\frac{\gamma}{2}\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

and so
y\in Z(\gamma/2)
, which shows that
Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\subset Z(\gamma/2)
. Thus,
by (
5.26
),

\nu(Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\leq\nu(Z(\gamma/2)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\leq 2C_{2}\gamma^{-1}\,\nu(H).

∎

We choose
\gamma=\max(1,2c_{2},4C_{2})
, so that, under the assumption (
5.23
),

(5.29)

\nu(Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\nu(H).

Also we define

\Phi(x)=\max(\Psi(x),{\rm dist}(x,{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus Z^{\prime}(\gamma)).

Notice that
\Phi
is a
1
-Lipschitz function which coincides with
\Psi(x)
away from
Z^{\prime}(\gamma)
and
that

(5.30)

\Phi(x)\geq e(x,\gamma)\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in Z(\gamma)$.}

Lemma 5.11
.

The suppressed operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\nu)
, with

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\|_{L^{p}(\nu)\to L^{p}(\nu)}\lesssim\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Theorem
5.1
and the construction of
\Phi
above.
Indeed, by (
5.22
),

\nu(B(x,r))\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,r^{n}\quad\mbox{ for all $r\geq\Phi(x)$.}

Also, by (
5.30
),

\sup_{{\varepsilon}>\Phi(x)}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\leq\sup_{{\varepsilon}>e(x,\gamma)}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\leq\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in Z(\gamma)$.}

On the other hand, by the definition of
Z(\gamma)
we also have

\sup_{{\varepsilon}>\Phi(x)}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\leq\sup_{{\varepsilon}>\Psi(x)}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}\nu(x)|\leq\gamma\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\quad\mbox{ for $x\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}\setminus Z(\gamma)$.}

So we can apply Theorem
5.1
, taking

\omega=(C\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))^{-1}\,\nu
with an appropriate absolute constant
C
, and
then the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Under the assumption (
5.23
),
there exists a subset
H_{0}\subset H
such that:

(a)

\eta(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\eta(H)
,

(b)

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi,\eta}
is bounded from
L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})
to
L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\eta}})
, with

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}\|_{L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})\to L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\eta}})}\lesssim\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

Proof.

We let

H_{0}=H\setminus Z^{\prime}(\gamma),

so that, by (
5.29
),

\eta(H_{0})=\nu(H_{0})\geq\nu(H)-\nu(Z^{\prime}(\gamma)\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\nu(H)=\frac{1}{2}\,\eta(H).

To prove (b), take
f\in L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})
and observe that for
x\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\eta}
, by (
5.4
),

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}(f\eta)(x)|=|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}(f\nu)(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(f\nu)(x)|+{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(f\nu)(x),

where
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}
is the
\Psi(x)
-truncated Riesz transform and

(5.31)

{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}\alpha(x)=\sup_{r>\Psi(x)}\frac{|\alpha|(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}

for any signed Radon measure
\alpha
. Taking into account that
\Phi(x)\geq\Psi(x)
, we can split

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(f\nu)(x)={\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi(x)}(f\nu)(x)+\int_{y\in H_{0}:\Psi(x)<|x-y|\leq\Phi(x)}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,f(y)\,d\nu(y).

To estimate the last integral, notice that for
y\in H_{0}
,
\Psi(y)=\Phi(y)
, and then the condition
\Psi(x)<|x-y|
implies that

\Phi(x)\leq\Phi(y)+|x-y|=\Psi(y)+|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)+2|x-y|<3|x-y|.

So the last integral above is bounded by

\int_{\Phi(x)/3<|x-y|\leq\Phi(x)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n}}\,|f(y)|\,d\nu(y)\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(f\nu)(x).

From the preceding estimate and (
5.4
) we derive that

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}(f\eta)(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi(x)}(f\nu)(x)|+C\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(f\nu)(x)\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}(f\nu)(x)|+C\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(f\nu)(x).

From the last inequality and Lemma
5.11
, taking into account that
\eta
coincides with
\nu
on
V_{1}
, we deduce that

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}\|_{L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})\to L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{V_{1}})}\lesssim\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}})+\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}\|_{L^{p}(\eta)\to L^{p}(\eta)}.

From the growth condition (
5.22
) and standard covering lemmas, it follows easily that

\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}\|_{L^{p}(\eta)\to L^{p}(\eta)}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

On the other hand, using the fact that
{\rm dist}(H_{0},{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus V_{1})\gtrsim\ell(R)
, it is immediate (by Schur’s criterion, for example) to check that also

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}\|_{L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})\to L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}\setminus V_{1}})}\lesssim\Theta(R)\leq\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

∎

5.5.
The variational argument

Lemma 5.13
.

There is a constant
c_{0}>0
, depending at most on
n
and
A_{0}
, such that for any
p\in(1,2]
, if
\Lambda
is big enough, we have

\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)|-G(x)-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\nu(x)\gtrsim\Lambda^{-p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}),

where
p^{\prime}=p/(p-1)
and the implicit constant depends on
p
.

Proof.

Suppose that, for a very small
0<\lambda<1
to be fixed below,

(5.32)

\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-G-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\bigr)_{+}\bigr|^{p}\,d\nu\leq\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Let
H_{0}\subset H
be the set found in Lemma
5.12
.
Consider the measures of the form

\tau=a\,\nu
, with
a\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
,
a\geq 0
, and let
F
be the functional

\displaystyle F(\tau)

\displaystyle=\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-G-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\bigr)_{+}\bigr|^{p}\,d\tau+\lambda\,\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\lambda\,\frac{\nu(H_{0})}{\tau(H_{0})}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Let

m=\inf F(\tau),

where the infimum is taken over all the measures
\tau=a\,\nu
, with
a\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
. We call such measures admissible.
Since
\nu
is admissible we infer that

(5.33)

m\leq F(\nu)\leq 3\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

So the infimum
m
is attained over the collection of
measures
\tau=a\nu
such that
\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\leq 3
and
\tau(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{3}\,\nu(H_{0})
. In particular, by taking a weak-
*
limit in
L^{\infty}(\nu)
, this guaranties
the existence of a minimizer.

Let
\tau
be an admissible measure such that

(5.34)

m=F(\tau)\leq 3\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

To simplify notation, we denote

f(x)=G+c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}).

We claim that

(5.35)

\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau(x)|-f(x)\bigr)_{+}|^{p}+p\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}\Bigl[\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\Bigr](x)\lesssim\lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}\quad\text{\!\!in $\operatorname{supp}\tau$,}

where for a vector field
U
, we wrote

{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}U={\mathcal{R}}^{*}(U\tau)=-\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(U_{i}\,\tau).

Assume (
5.35
) for the moment. Since the function on the left hand side is subharmonic
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus\operatorname{supp}\tau

and continuous in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
(recall that
\tau
and
\eta
are absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, with bounded densities), we deduce that the same estimate holds in the whole
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
.

Next we need to construct an auxiliary function
{\varphi}
. First we claim that
there exists a subfamily of cubes
{\mathsf{H}}^{0}\subset{\mathsf{H}}
such that

(i)

the balls
3B_{Q}\equiv 3B_{Q^{(\mu)}}
, with
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
, are disjoint,

(ii)

\frac{1}{12}\nu(Q)\leq\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\leq 3\nu(Q)
for all
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
, and

(iii)

\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\approx\nu(H)
.

The existence of the family
{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
follows easily from the fact that

\tau(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{3}\nu(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{6}\,\nu(H)
and
\tau=a\,\nu
with
\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\leq 3
. Indeed,
notice that the family
I
of cubes
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}
such that

\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\leq\frac{1}{12}\nu(Q)
satisfies

\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in I}\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\leq\frac{1}{12}\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in I}\nu(Q)\leq\frac{1}{12}\,\nu(H)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\tau(H_{0}).

So

\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I}\tau(Q)\geq\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I}\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\tau(H_{0}).

By Vitali’s 5r-covering lemma, there exists a subfamily
{\mathsf{H}}^{0}\subset{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I

such that the balls
\{3B_{Q}\}_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}
are disjoint and the balls
\{15B_{Q}\}_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}
cover

\bigcup_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I}Q
. From the fact that the cubes from
{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling and the properties of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
in Lemma
5.4
, we have

\nu(Q)\approx\nu(15B_{Q})
if
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
, and thus

\displaystyle\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\tau(Q\cap H_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{12}\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\nu(Q)\approx\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\nu(15B_{Q})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I}\nu(Q)\geq\frac{1}{3}\,\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}\setminus I}\tau(Q)\geq\frac{1}{6}\,\tau(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{36}\,\nu(H).

The converse estimate
\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\tau(Q\cap H_{0})\lesssim\nu(H)
follows trivially from
\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\leq 3
.

We consider the function

(5.36)

{\varphi}=\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\Theta(Q)\,{\varphi}_{Q},

where
\chi_{B_{Q}}\leq{\varphi}_{Q}\leq\chi_{1.1B_{Q}}
,
\|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\ell(Q)^{-1}
.
Remark that

{\mathcal{R}}^{*}(\nabla{\varphi}\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})=\tilde{c}\,{\varphi},

where
\tilde{c}
is some non-zero absolute constant.
Notice also that

(5.37)

\|\nabla{\varphi}\|_{1}\lesssim\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\Theta(Q)\,\ell(Q)^{n}\approx\tau(H_{0}).

Multiplying the left hand side of (
5.35
) by
|\nabla{\varphi}|
, integrating with respect to the Lebesgue measure
{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}
, taking into account that the estimate in
(
5.35
) holds on the whole
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
, and using (
5.37
), we get

(5.38)

\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}+p\!\int{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}\Bigl[\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\Bigr]\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\\
\lesssim\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Next we estimate the second integral on the left hand side of the preceding inequality, which we denote by
I
. For that purpose we will also need the estimate

(5.39)

\int|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})|^{p}\,d\tau\lesssim\Lambda^{p{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}),

which we defer to Lemma
5.14
.
Using this inequality we get

|I|=\left|\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\cdot{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})\,d\tau\right|\\
\leq\left(\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,d\tau\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}\left(\int|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})|^{p}\,d\tau\right)^{1/p}\\
\leq(F(\tau))^{1/p^{\prime}}\left(\int|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})|^{p}\,d\tau\right)^{1/p}\\
\overset{{(\ref{e.admis2})},\eqref{eqacpsi}}{\lesssim}\bigl(\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})\bigr)^{1/p^{\prime}}\bigl(\Lambda^{p{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})\bigr)^{1/p}=\lambda^{1/p^{\prime}}\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

From (
5.38
) and the preceding estimate we deduce
that

\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\lesssim\lambda^{1/p^{\prime}}\,\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Notice now that, for all
x\in\operatorname{supp}{\varphi}
,

G(x)=2A_{0}^{3}\int_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}\setminus V_{2}}\frac{1}{\ell(R)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\eta(y)\lesssim\frac{\eta({\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime})}{\ell(R)\,{\rm dist}({\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}^{\prime}\setminus V_{2},{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta})^{n-1}}\lesssim\Theta(R),

and so

|f(x)|\leq C\,\Theta(R)+c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\leq 2c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\qquad\mbox{for all $x\in\operatorname{supp}{\varphi}$,}

taking into account that
\Lambda\gg 1
.
So we have

(5.40)

\displaystyle\int|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}+\int|f|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl(\lambda^{1/p^{\prime}}\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}+c_{0}^{p}\bigr)\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

To get a contradiction, note that by the construction of
{\varphi}
and by the properties of
\tau
, we have

(5.41)

\left|\int{\mathcal{R}}\tau\cdot\nabla{\varphi}\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\right|=\left|\int{\mathcal{R}}^{*}(\nabla{\varphi}\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})\,d\tau\right|=|\tilde{c}|\int{\varphi}\,d\tau\\
\geq|\tilde{c}|\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\tau(B_{Q})\gtrsim|\tilde{c}|\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\tau(H_{0})\geq\frac{|\tilde{c}|}{6}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\nu(H).

However, by Hölder’s inequality, (
5.37
), and (
5.40
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int{\mathcal{R}}\tau\cdot\nabla{\varphi}\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{p}\,|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\right)^{1/p}\left(\int|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl(\lambda^{1/(p\,p^{\prime})}\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}/p}+c_{0}\bigr)\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\nu(H),

which contradicts (
5.41
) if
c_{0}
is chosen small enough and

\lambda\leq c\,\Lambda^{-p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}},

with
c
small enough.
∎

Proof of (
5.35
).

Recall that
\tau=a\,\nu
is a minimizer for
F(\cdot)
that in particular satisfies

F(\tau)\leq 3\lambda\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}),
by (
5.34
).
We have to show that, for all
x\in\operatorname{supp}\tau
,

\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau(x)|-f(x)\bigr)_{+}|^{p}+p\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}\Bigl[\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\Bigr](x)\lesssim\lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}.

Let
x_{0}\in\operatorname{supp}\tau

and let
B=B(x_{0},\rho)
, with
\rho>0
small. For
0<t<1
we consider the competing measure
\tau_{t}=a_{t}\,\tau
, where
a_{t}
is defined as follows:

a_{t}=(1-t\,\chi_{B})a.

Notice that, for each
0<t<1
,
a_{t}
is a non-negative function such that

\|a_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\leq\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}.

Taking the above into account we deduce that

\displaystyle F(\tau_{t})

\displaystyle=\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau_{t}|-f\bigr)_{+}\bigr|^{p}\,d\tau_{t}+\lambda\,\|a_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\lambda\,\frac{\nu(H_{0})}{\tau_{t}(H_{0})}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})

\displaystyle\leq\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau_{t}|-f\bigr)_{+}\bigr|^{p}\,d\tau_{t}+\lambda\,\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu)}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\lambda\,\frac{\nu(H_{0})}{\tau_{t}(H_{0})}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})

\displaystyle=:{\widetilde{F}}(\tau_{t}).

Since
{\widetilde{F}}(\tau)=F(\tau)\leq F(\tau_{t})\leq{\widetilde{F}}(\tau_{t})
for
t\geq 0
, we infer that

(5.42)

\frac{1}{\tau(B)}\,\frac{d}{dt}\,{\widetilde{F}}(\tau_{t})\biggr|_{t=0+}\geq 0.

Using that

\frac{d}{dt}|{\mathcal{R}}\tau_{t}(x)|\biggr|_{t=0}=\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{R}}\tau(x)|}{\mathcal{R}}\tau(x)\cdot{\mathcal{R}}(-\chi_{B}\tau)(x),

an easy computation gives

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\,{\widetilde{F}}(\tau_{t})\biggr|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=-\int_{B}\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\quad+p\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}\,\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\cdot{\mathcal{R}}(-\chi_{B}\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\,\frac{\nu(H_{0})}{\tau(H_{0})^{2}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})\,\tau(B).

Recalling that
\tau(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{3}\,\nu(H_{0})\geq\frac{1}{6}\,\nu(H)
, from (
5.42
) and the preceding calculation we derive

(5.43)

\frac{1}{\tau(B)}\int_{B}\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,d\tau+\frac{p}{\tau(B)}\int\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}\,\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\cdot{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{B}\tau)\,d\tau\\
\leq 3\lambda\,\frac{\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})}{\tau(H_{0})}=3\lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}\,\frac{\nu(H)}{\tau(H_{0})}\leq 18\lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}.

We rewrite the left hand side of (
5.43
) as

\frac{1}{\tau(B)}\int_{B}\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p}\,d\tau+\frac{p}{\tau(B)}\int_{B}{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}\Bigl[\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}\,|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\Bigr]\,d\tau.

Taking into account that the functions in the integrands are continuous on
\operatorname{supp}(\tau)
,
letting the radius
\rho
of
B
tend to
0
, it turns out that the above expression converges to

\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau(x_{0})|-f(x_{0})\bigr)_{+}|^{p}+p\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\tau}^{*}\Bigl[\bigl|\bigl(|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|-f\bigr)_{+}|^{p-1}\,|{\mathcal{R}}\tau|^{-1}{\mathcal{R}}\tau\Bigr](x_{0}).

The desired estimate (
5.35
) follows from the above and (
5.43
).
∎

In order to complete the proof of Lemma
5.13
we need the following technical result.

Lemma 5.14
.

Suppose that (
5.32
) holds with
\lambda\leq 1
and let
{\varphi}
be as in (
5.36
).
Then,

\int|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})|^{p}\,d\nu\lesssim\Lambda^{p{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}),

Proof.

Recall that

{\varphi}=\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\Theta(Q)\,{\varphi}_{Q},

with
\chi_{B_{Q}}\leq{\varphi}_{Q}\leq\chi_{1.1B_{Q}}
,
\|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\ell(Q)^{-1}
.
We consider the function

g=\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}g_{Q},

where
g_{Q}=c_{Q}\,\chi_{Q\cap H_{0}}
, with
c_{Q}=\Theta(Q)\int|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}\,\eta(Q\cap H_{0})^{-1}
.
Observe that

(5.44)

\int g_{Q}\,d\eta=\Theta(Q)\int|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}

and that

0\leq c_{Q}\lesssim\frac{\Theta(Q)\,\ell(Q)^{n}}{\eta(Q\cap H_{0})}\approx\frac{\mu(Q^{(\mu)})}{\eta(Q)}=1.

The first step of our arguments consists in comparing
{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)
to

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g\,\eta)(x)
, with
\Psi
given by (
5.21
).
We will prove that, for each
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
,

(5.45)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}(\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|

\displaystyle\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Theta(Q)\,\ell(Q)^{n+1}}{{\rm dist}(x,Q)^{n+1}+\ell(Q)^{n+1}}+\chi_{2B_{Q}}(x)\,|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{c\Psi(x)\leq|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{g_{Q}(y)}{|x-y|^{n}}\,d\eta(y),

for some fixed
c>0
.
The arguments to prove this estimate are quite standard.

Suppose first that
x\in 2B_{Q}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}(\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Theta(Q)\int_{1.1B_{Q}}\frac{1}{\ell(Q)\,|x-y|^{n}}\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}(y)\lesssim\Theta(Q),

which yields

|{\mathcal{R}}(\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|\lesssim\Theta(Q)+|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|,

and shows that (
5.45
) holds in this situation.

In the case
x\not\in 2B_{Q}
we write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(\Theta(Q)|

\displaystyle\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}\big(\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}-g_{Q}\,\eta\big)(x)+\int_{|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{n+1}}g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:gQintegral}}{=}\int\left(\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{n+1}}-\frac{x-x_{Q}}{|x-x_{Q}|^{n+1}}\right)\bigl[\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}(y)|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}(y)-g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y)\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\int_{|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{n+1}}g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}(x)+I_{2}(x).

Concerning the term
I_{1}(x)
, recalling that
\operatorname{supp}{\varphi}_{Q}\cup\operatorname{supp}g_{Q}\subset 1.1\overline{B_{Q}}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle|I_{1}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\frac{\ell(Q)}{|x-x_{Q}|^{n+1}}\bigl[\Theta(Q)|\nabla{\varphi}_{Q}(y)|\,d{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}(y)+g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y)\bigr]\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{|x-x_{Q}|^{n+1}}\,\eta(Q).

Regarding
I_{2}(x)
, we write

I_{2}(x)\leq\int_{y\in Q:|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n}}\,g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y).

Notice that for
y\in Q
, since
x\notin 2B_{Q}
, we have

C|x-y|\geq\ell(Q)\overset{\eqref{eqdefPsi1}}{\geq}\Psi(y)\geq\Psi(x)-|x-y|.

Thus,
|x-y|\geq c\,\Psi(x)
, and so

I_{2}(x)\leq\int_{c\Psi(x)\leq|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n}}\,g_{Q}(y)\,d\eta(y).

Gathering the estimates for
I_{1}(x)
and
I_{2}(x)
we see that (
5.45
) also holds in this case.

From (
5.45
) we infer that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})

\displaystyle(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g\,\eta)(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\frac{\Theta(Q)\,\ell(Q)^{n+1}}{{\rm dist}(x,Q)^{n+1}+\ell(Q)^{n+1}}+\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\chi_{2B_{Q}}(x)\,|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{c\Psi(x)\leq|x-y|\leq\Psi(x)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n}}\,g(y)\,d\eta(y)

\displaystyle=S_{1}(x)+S_{2}(x)+S_{3}(x).

We split

(5.46)

\int|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g\,\eta)(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int|S_{i}(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x).

We estimate the first summand by duality. Consider a function
h\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\eta)
. Then,

(5.47)

\int S_{1}(x)\,h(x)\,d\eta(x)=\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\eta(Q)\int\frac{\ell(Q)}{{\rm dist}(x,Q)^{n+1}+\ell(Q)^{n+1}}\,h(x)\,d\eta(x).

For each
Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}
,
using the fact that
\eta(\lambda Q)\leq C\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\ell(\lambda Q)^{n}
for every
\lambda\geq 1
,
we obtain

\int\frac{\ell(Q)}{{\rm dist}(x,Q)^{n+1}+\ell(Q)^{n+1}}\,h(x)\,d\eta(x)\lesssim C\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\inf_{y\in Q}{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}h(y).

Therefore, the right side of (
5.47
) does not exceed

\displaystyle C\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\eta(Q)\,\inf_{y\in Q}{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}h(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\int_{H}{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}h(y)\,d\eta(y)

\displaystyle\leq\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\eta(H)^{1/p}\,\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}h\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\eta)}\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})\,\eta(H)^{1/p}\,\|h\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\eta)}.

So we deduce that

\int|S_{1}(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Regarding the summand in (
5.46
) involving
S_{2}
, since the balls
2B_{Q}
are disjoint, we have

\int|S_{2}(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x)=\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\int_{2B_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\leq\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(\cdot)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p},

where
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(\cdot)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)={\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(x)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)(x)
.
Finally, to estimate the last summand in (
5.46
), we take into account that

|S_{3}(x)|\lesssim{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(g\,\eta)(x),
where
{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(g\,\eta)
is the maximal operator from (
5.31
).
Hence,

\int|S_{3}(x)|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\lesssim\int|{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(g\,\eta)|^{p}\,d\eta\lesssim\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}\,\|g\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

Gathering the estimates obtained for
S_{1}
,
S_{2}
,
S_{3}
, by (
5.46
) we get

\|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(\cdot)}(g_{Q}\,\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}+\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi(\cdot)}(g\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}.

From (
5.4
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}(g_{Q}\,\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}+\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}(g\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{Q^{(\mu)}\in{\mathsf{H}}^{0}}\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(g_{Q}\,\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}+\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}(g\,\eta)\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}.

Using now that, by Lemma
5.12
,

{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi,\eta}
is bounded from
L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})
to
L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\eta}})
with

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi}\|_{L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{H_{0}})\to L^{p}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{\eta}})}\lesssim\Lambda^{{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}}),

and that the same happens for
{\mathcal{M}}_{\Psi,n}
, we get

\|{\mathcal{R}}(|\nabla{\varphi}|\,{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1})\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})+\Lambda^{p{\varepsilon}_{n}}\Theta({\mathsf{H}})^{p}\,\|g\|_{L^{p}(\eta)}^{p}\lesssim\Lambda^{p{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}}).

∎

5.6.
Lower estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}\eta

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
be such that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
and let
V_{4}
and
\eta
be as in Section
5.4
.
Assume
\Lambda>0
big enough and let
c_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.13
.
Then we have

\int_{V_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\gtrsim\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

for any
p\in(1,\infty)
, with the implicit constant depending on
p
.

Proof.

Recall that
\nu={\varphi}_{R}\,\eta
. For all
x\in\operatorname{supp}\eta
, using that
\|\nabla{\varphi}_{R}\|_{\infty}\leq 2A_{0}^{-3}\ell(R)^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\varphi}_{R}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)-{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)\big|

\displaystyle=\big|{\varphi}_{R}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)-{\mathcal{R}}({\varphi}_{R}\eta)(x)\big|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\frac{({\varphi}_{R}(x)-{\varphi}_{R}(y))\,(x-y)}{|x-y|^{n+1}}\,d\eta(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{0}^{3}\int_{y\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\eta}\setminus V_{2}}\frac{1}{\ell(R)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\eta(y)

\displaystyle\quad+2A_{0}^{3}\int_{y\in V_{2}:{\varphi}_{R}(y)\neq{\varphi}_{R}(x)}\frac{1}{\ell(R)\,|x-y|^{n-1}}\,d\eta(y).

Recall that
{\varphi}_{R}
equals
1
on
V_{3}
and vanishes out of
V_{4}
.
Then it is clear that the last integral on the right hand side above vanishes if
x\in V_{3}
and
it does not exceed
C\,\Theta(R)
if
x\in V_{3}^{c}
.
So in any case

\big|{\varphi}_{R}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)-{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)\big|\leq G(x)+C_{3}\,\Theta(R).

From the preceding estimate we infer that

\displaystyle|{\varphi}_{R}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq|{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)|-G(x)-C_{3}\,\Theta(R)-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq|{\mathcal{R}}\nu(x)|-G(x)-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Therefore, since
|(\;\cdot\;)_{+}|^{p}
is non-decreasing,

\displaystyle\int_{V_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\geq\int\big|(|{\varphi}_{R}\,{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\geq\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-G-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\geq\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-G-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\nu.

By Lemmas
5.13
and
5.6
,

\int\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\nu|-G-c_{0}\,\Theta({\mathsf{H}}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\nu\geq c\Lambda^{-p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{H}})\geq c\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

and so the lemma follows.
∎

6.
Lower estimates for the Haar coefficients of
{\mathcal{R}}\mu
for cubes near a typical tractable tree

For
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
and
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
, we write
P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
if there exists some

P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
such that

(6.1)

A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\,\ell(P)\quad\text{ and }\quad 20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing.

We say that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is a typical tractable tree, and we write
R\in{\mathsf{Ty}}
if

(6.2)

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))).

The reason for the terminology “typical tree” is due to the fact that such trees are prevalent, in a sense, because of Lemma

12.4
below.

In all this section we assume that
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
is such that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
is tractable and typical, and we consider the measure
\eta
constructed in Section
5.2
.
Roughly speaking, our objective is to transfer the lower estimate we obtained for
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
in Lemma
5.15
to
the Haar coefficients of

{\mathcal{R}}\mu
for cubes close to
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
Recall that
{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)
exists
\mu
-a.e. as a principal value under the assumptions
of Proposition
3.6
.

6.1.
The operators
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
, and
\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}

To simplify notation, in this section we will write

{\mathsf{End}}={\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R)),\quad\;{\mathsf{Reg}}={\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R)),\;\quad\;{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R)),\quad\,\text{and}\quad\;{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}={\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(e^{\prime}(R)).

We need to consider an enlarged version of the generalized tree
{\mathcal{T}}
, due to some technical difficulties that arise because the cubes from
{\mathsf{Neg}}:={\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{End}}
are not
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling.
To this end, denote by
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
the family of the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
which are contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{Neg}}
.
Let
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
be the subfamily of the cubes
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
for which there exists some

{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube
S\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
that contains
P
. By the definition of
{\mathsf{Neg}}
, such cube
S
should be contained in the cube from
Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
such that
P\subset Q
.
We also denote by
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
the family of maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which belong to
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
and are contained in some
cube from
{\mathsf{Neg}}
, so that, in particular, any cube from
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
is contained in another from
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
.

We define

{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}=({\mathsf{End}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}})\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}},

and we let
{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}={\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(R))
be the family of cubes that belong to
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
but are not strictly contained in any cube from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
.
Further, we write

(6.3)

Z=Z(e^{\prime}(R))=e^{\prime}(R)\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{End}}}Q\quad\text{ and }\quad{\widetilde{Z}}={\widetilde{Z}}(e^{\prime}(R))=e^{\prime}(R)\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}}Q

Then we denote

{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\chi_{Q}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x),

{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)=\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}}\chi_{Q}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x),

and

\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)=\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}}\chi_{Q}(x)\,\big(m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)+\chi_{Z}(x)\big({\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big).

Remark that the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
have the advantage over the cubes from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
that their sizes change smoothly so that, for example, neighboring cubes have comparable side lengths. However, they need not be
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling or doubling, unlike the cubes from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
. In particular, it is not clear
how to estimate their energy
{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}
in terms of the condition
{\mathsf{DB}}
(recall that the cubes from
{\mathsf{DB}}
are asked to be
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling).

For
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
, we define

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,\mu(P),

where

D(P,Q)=\ell(P)+{\rm dist}(P,Q)+\ell(Q).

The coefficient
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)
will be used to bound some “error terms” in our transference
arguments. We will see later how they can be estimated in terms of the coefficients
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
. Notice that, unlike
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
, the coefficients
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)
depend on the family

{\mathsf{Reg}}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
such that
(Q\cup\frac{1}{2}B(Q))\cap V_{4}\neq\varnothing
and
x\in Q
,
y\in\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}\eta(y)\big|\lesssim\Theta(R)+{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

Proof.

By the triangle inequality, for
x
,
y
and
Q
as in the lemma, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}\eta(y)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus e^{\prime}(R)}(x)\big|+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{e^{\prime}(R)\setminus 2Q}(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{e^{\prime}(R)\setminus 2Q}(x_{Q})\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2Q\setminus Q}(x_{Q})\big|+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{e^{\prime}(R)\setminus Q}(x_{Q})-{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}\chi_{(\frac{1}{2}B(Q))^{c}}(x_{Q})\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}\chi_{(\frac{1}{2}B(Q))^{c}}(x_{Q})-{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}\chi_{(\frac{1}{2}B(Q))^{c}}(y)\big|+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}\chi_{\frac{1}{2}B(Q)}(y)\big|

\displaystyle=I_{1}+\ldots+I_{6}.

To estimate
I_{1}
, notice that, by (
5.15
),
{\rm dist}(Q,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus e^{\prime}(R))\gtrsim\ell(R)
.
Then it follows that

I_{1}\lesssim\frac{\mu(2R)}{\ell(R)^{n}}\lesssim\Theta(R).
