Arguing similarly one also gets

I_{3}\lesssim\frac{\mu(2Q)}{\ell(Q)^{n}}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q).

By standard arguments involving continuity of the Riesz kernel, we also deduce that

I_{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q),\quad I_{5}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q).

Concerning the term
I_{6}
, using that
\eta
is a constant multiple of
{\mathcal{L}}^{n+1}
on

\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
, we get

I_{6}\leq\int_{\frac{1}{2}B(Q)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n}}\,d\eta(y)\lesssim\frac{\eta(\tfrac{1}{2}B(Q))}{\ell(Q)^{n}}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q).

Finally we deal with the term
I_{4}
. To this end, recall that
\mu(P)=\eta(\frac{1}{2}B(P))
for all

P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
, and so

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\neq Q}\left|\int K(x_{Q}-z)\,d\big(\eta{\lfloor}_{\frac{1}{2}B(P)}-\mu{\lfloor}_{P}\big)(z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\neq Q}\int|K(x_{Q}-z)-K(x_{Q}-x_{P})|\,d\big(\eta{\lfloor}_{\frac{1}{2}B(P)}+\mu{\lfloor}_{P}\big)(z)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\neq Q}\frac{\ell(P)}{|x_{Q}-x_{P}|^{n+1}}\,\mu(P)\approx\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\neq Q}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(Q,P)^{n+1}}\,\mu(P)\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

For the estimates in the last line we took into account the properties of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}
described
in Lemma
5.4
.
Gathering the estimates above, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
and
x,y\in Q
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)-\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(y)\big|\lesssim\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2}.

Proof.

For
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
and
x,y\in Q
, we have

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)-\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(y)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x)-\big(m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)

\displaystyle=\big({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x)-m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)\big)-m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q}\mu))

\displaystyle\quad-\big(m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R^{c}}\mu))-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big).

To estimate the first term on the right hand side, notice that for all
x,z\in Q
, by standard arguments we have

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x)-{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(z)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q).

Averaging over
z\in Q
, we get

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x)-m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q).

To estimate
m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q}\mu))
we use the fact that, by the antisymmetry of the Riesz kernel,
m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{Q}\mu))=0
and
3
3

3

Here we use the assumption that
{\mathcal{R}}_{*}\mu\in L^{1}(\mu)
.
Indeed, this implies that
{\mathcal{R}}_{*}(\chi_{Q}\mu)\in L^{1}(\mu)
(using Lemma
2.6
, for example). Also, by antisymmetry we have

\int_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}(\chi_{Q}\mu)\,d\mu=0
for any
{\varepsilon}>0
. Then the
\mu
-a.e. convergence of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\varepsilon}(\chi_{Q}\mu)(x)

to the principal value
{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{Q}\mu)(x)
as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
and the dominated convergence theorem ensure that
\int_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{Q}\mu)\,d\mu=0
.

thus

\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q}\mu))\big|=\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q\setminus Q}\mu))\big|\leq\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in Q}\int_{\xi\in 2Q\setminus Q}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)d\mu(z).

By Cauchy-Schwarz and Lemma
2.6
, we have

(6.5)

\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in Q}\int_{\xi\in 2Q\setminus Q}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)d\mu(z)\\
\leq\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in Q}\int_{\xi\in 2Q\setminus 2B_{Q}}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)d\mu(z)+\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in Q}\int_{\xi\in 2B_{Q}\setminus Q}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)d\mu(z)\\
\leq{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+\bigg(\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in Q}\bigg(\int_{\xi\in 2B_{Q}\setminus Q}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)\bigg)^{2}d\mu(z)\bigg)^{1/2}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2}.

Finally we deal with the last term on the right hand side of (
6.4
). We split it:

\displaystyle\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R^{c}}\mu))-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R\setminus 2R}\mu))|+\big|m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R}\mu))\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R^{c}}\mu))-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R^{c}}\mu))\big|

\displaystyle=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}.

Clearly,

J_{1}\lesssim\frac{\mu(3R)}{\ell(R)^{n}}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R).

Also, by the antisymmetry of the Riesz kernel and arguing as in (
6.5
),

\displaystyle J_{2}=

\displaystyle\big|m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R}\mu))\big|=\big|m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R\setminus 2R}\mu))\big|

\displaystyle\leq\;\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int_{z\in 2R}\int_{\xi\in 3R\setminus 2R}\frac{1}{|z-\xi|^{n}}\,d\mu(\xi)d\mu(z)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}.

Regarding
J_{3}
, by standard methods, for all
z,z^{\prime}\in 2Q
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R^{c}}\mu)(z)-{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{3R^{c}}\mu)(z^{\prime})\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R).

Hence averaging for
z\in Q
,
z^{\prime}\in 2R
, we obtain

J_{3}\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R).

We showed that

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)-\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(y)\big|\lesssim\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2}+{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+{\mathcal{P}}(R).

To get rid of the last two terms, recall that the cubes in
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, and so is
R
. Hence, by the definition of the energy
{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)
, we have

{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2},

and an analogous estimate for
{\mathcal{P}}(R)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
1<p\leq 2
.
For any
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
such that
\ell_{0}\leq\ell(Q)
,

\int_{Q}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{p}\,d\mu\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(Q)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

Proof.

The lemma follows from Hölder’s inequality and the estimate

(6.6)

\int_{Q}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}(2Q),

which we prove below.

Notice first that the cube
Q
as above is covered by the cubes
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{Reg}}
. Indeed, if
Q\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, then
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{Reg}}}
and this is trivially true. On the other hand, if
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\setminus{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\subset{\mathsf{End}}
, then we have
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and the condition
\ell_{0}\leq\ell(Q)
implies that
d_{R,\ell_{0}}(x)\leq\ell(Q)
. Hence,

Q
is covered by the cubes
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{Reg}}
.

Observe now that, for
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
,
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
such that
P\subset Q
, and
x\in P
,

{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)={\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2P}\mu)(x)-{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x)={\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q\setminus 2P}\mu)(x).

Thus,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)\big|\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\subset S\subset Q}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S}).

Notice now that, if
{\widehat{Q}}
denotes the cube from
{\mathsf{End}}
that contains
Q
(which coincides with

Q
when
P\not\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
), we have

d_{R}(x)\gtrsim{\rm dist}(P,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus{\widehat{Q}})\geq{\rm dist}(P,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus Q)\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in P$.}

So by Lemma
5.4
we also have

(6.7)

\ell(P)\gtrsim{\rm dist}(P,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus Q)

Thus,

\sum_{S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\subset S\subset Q}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S}),

with
{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)
defined in (
2.9
).

So we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\subset Q}\int_{P}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\subset Q}\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})\bigg)^{2}\,\mu(P).

By Hölder’s inequality, for any
\alpha>0
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}

\displaystyle\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\alpha}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\alpha}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha}\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\alpha}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{Q}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\subset Q}\,\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q):S\supset P}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\alpha}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})^{2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\alpha}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:P\subset S}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\alpha}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{S})^{2}\,\mu(S).

By Lemma
2.9
, for
\alpha
small enough, the right hand side is bounded above by

C{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)
, so that (
6.6
) holds.
∎

6.2.
Estimates for the
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
coefficients of some cubes from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
and
{\mathsf{Reg}}

We will transfer the lower estimate obtained for the
L^{p}(\eta)
norm of
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
in Lemma

5.15
to
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mu
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu
, and
\Delta_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
by means of
Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.3
. To this end, we will need careful
estimates for the
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
coefficients of cubes from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
and
{\mathsf{Reg}}
. This is the
task we will perform in this section.

Given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, recall that

{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))={\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.
To shorten notation, we will write
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}={\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))
in this section.
Recall that
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}={\mathsf{End}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}}
. By (
4.8
), we have

(6.8)

{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}={\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}},

where we introduced the following notations:

•

{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}
is the subfamily of
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
of those maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained both in
e^{\prime}(R)
and in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}
.

•

{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}
is the subfamily of
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
of those maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained both in
e^{\prime}(R)
and in some cube from
{\mathsf{OP}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}
.

•

{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}
is the subfamily of
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
of those maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained in some cube

Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(Q^{\prime})\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(Q^{\prime})\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}
for some
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
.

•

{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}
is the subfamily of
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
of those maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained in some cube

Q\in{\mathsf{OP}}(Q^{\prime})\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(Q^{\prime})
for some
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
.

•

{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}=\bigcup_{Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}}({\mathsf{HD}}(Q^{\prime})\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(Q^{\prime}))
.

Remark that the splitting in (
6.8
) is disjoint. Indeed, notice that, by the definition
of
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, the cubes from
{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
do not belong to
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, since they are strictly contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
, which is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, in particular.

For
i=1,2
, we also denote by
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{i}}
the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
which are contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}_{i}
, and we define
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{i}}
,
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}
,
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, and

{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}
analogously.
4
4

4

Notice that
{\mathsf{D}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}={\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}
.

We let
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
be the “other” cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
: the ones which are not contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{End}}
(which, in particular, have side length comparable to
\ell_{0}
).
Also, we let
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}}
be the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
which are contained in some cube from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}
. Notice that we have the splitting

(6.9)

{\mathsf{Reg}}={\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}.

The families above may intersect the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}}
.

Given a family
{\mathcal{I}}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and
1<p\leq 2
, we denote

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{I}})=\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{p}\,\mu(Q),\qquad\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathcal{I}})=\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p}\,\mu(Q).

We also write
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{I}})=\Sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{I}})
,
\Sigma^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathcal{I}})=\Sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathcal{I}})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
,

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}(2Q).

Proof.

For all
S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}(S)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{P:S\subset P\subset Q}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\,\Theta(P)\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\,{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{P:S\subset P\subset Q}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\,\Theta(P)^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{P:S\subset P\subset Q}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\bigg)+{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P:S\subset P\subset Q}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\,\Theta(P)^{2}+{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)}{\mathcal{P}}(S)^{2}\,\mu(S)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)}\sum_{P:S\subset P\subset Q}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\,\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(S)+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)}{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(S).

Clearly, the last sum is bounded by
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q),
which in turn is bounded by
{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)
since
Q
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling. Concerning the first term,
arguing as in (
6.7
), we deduce that the cubes
P
in the sum belong to

{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)
. Thus, by Fubini,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)}\Theta(P)^{2}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:S\subset P}\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(P)}\,\mu(S)+{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}}_{\mu}^{int}(Q)}\!\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)+{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}(2Q).

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

We have:

(i)

If
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R)
.

(ii)

If
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\delta_{0}\,\Lambda\,\Theta(R)
.

(iii)

If
Q\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Lambda\,\Theta(R)
.

(iv)

If
Q\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)
.

(v)

If
Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/3}\,\Theta(R)
.

Proof.

The statements (i) and (ii) follow directly from the definitions of
{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}
and Lemma
3.3
.
The statement (iii) is due to the fact that, by the stopping conditions, the cubes
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))
satisfy
{\mathcal{P}}(Q^{\prime})\lesssim\Lambda\,{\mathcal{P}}(R)\approx\Lambda\,\Theta(R)
. Then
it just remains to notice that if

Q
is a maximal doubling cube contained in
Q^{\prime}
, from Lemma
3.3
it follows that

{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q^{\prime})
.

The property (iv) for the cubes in
{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}
follows by arguments analogous to the ones for (iii), taking into account that such cubes are maximal doubling cubes contained in cubes
from
{\mathsf{Stop}}(R^{\prime})
, for some
R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
with
{\mathcal{P}}(R^{\prime})\approx\Theta(R^{\prime})=\Lambda\,\Theta(R)
.
5
5

5

This argument shows that, in fact,
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{2}\Theta(R)
for all
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
.

On the other hand the cubes
Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
satisfy
\Theta(Q)=\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)
and are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling by construction.

Finally, we turn our attention to (v). By the definitions of
{\mathsf{Neg}}
and
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
and Lemma
3.3
, for all

S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
such that
Q\subset S\subset R
, we have

\Theta(S)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(R).

Thus,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}(Q)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\,{\mathcal{P}}(R)+\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\,\Theta(S)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\,\Theta(R)+\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(S)}\,\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\,\Theta(R)+\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\,\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\,\Theta(R)+\log\left(\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(Q)}\right)\,\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/3}\,\Theta(R).

∎

Remark 6.6
.

We will assume that

\delta_{0}\leq\Lambda^{-2}.

With this choice, it follows that, by the preceding lemma,

{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\delta_{0}^{1/2}\,\Theta(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}$.}

Lemma 6.7
.

Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
. Then

(6.10)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}})\approx\sigma({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}})\lesssim B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.11)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}})\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\leq\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.12)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}})\approx\sigma({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}})\leq M^{-2}\,\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.13)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.14)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\big(B\,M^{2}\,\delta_{0}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Also, for
1<p\leq 2
,

(6.15)

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\lesssim\Big(B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{p}{2}}+\Lambda^{\frac{-p}{6n}}\Big)\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.16)

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})\lesssim\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\approx\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\lesssim B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

(6.17)

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-p}{6n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

To prove (
6.10
), notice first that
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}})\approx\sigma({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}})
because
the cubes from
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling. Also, by (
6.8
) we have

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}})=\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}_{1})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}_{2})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{OP}}_{1})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{OP}}_{2})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}).

By Lemma
6.5
and the subsequent remark, we have

(6.18)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}_{1})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}_{2})\lesssim\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(R)=\delta_{0}\,\sigma(R).

Again by Lemma
6.5
, we have
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Theta(R)
for all
Q\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
and thus
6
6

6

We will obtain better estimates for
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})
below.

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\sigma(R).

Also, since
{\mathcal{T}}
is a tractable tree, we have

\sigma(R)\leq B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\quad\text{ and }\quad\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\approx\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\lesssim B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Regarding the family
{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}
, denote

{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}={\mathsf{Stop}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{OP}}(R)\cup\bigcup_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}}\big({\mathsf{Stop}}(S)\cap{\mathsf{OP}}(S)\big),

so that the cubes from
P\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}
are maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes contained in some cube from

{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}
. By Lemma
3.3
, if
P\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}
is contained in
Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}
,
then
{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
. In this case,
{\mathsf{OP}}(\,\cdot\,)={\mathsf{NDB}}(\,\cdot\,)
, and so there exists

Q^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}(Q)\in{\mathsf{DB}}
such that
\ell(Q^{\prime})=\ell(Q)
and
Q^{\prime}\subset 20Q
.
In particular, this implies that
Q^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and that
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\approx{\mathcal{P}}(Q^{\prime})\approx\Theta(Q^{\prime})
, and by the definition of
{\mathsf{DB}}
,

(6.19)

{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime})\gtrsim M^{2}\,\sigma(Q^{\prime}).

Thus,

(6.20)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)}{\mathcal{P}}(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)\approx\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}\sigma(Q^{\prime}(Q))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime}(Q))\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime}),

where in the last estimate we took into account that for each
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{DB}}
there is at most a bounded number of cubes
Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}
such that
Q^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}(Q)
(possibly depending on
n
and
A_{0}
).
Since
{\mathcal{T}}
is a typical tree, by the definition in (
6.2
), we have

(6.21)

\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime})\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

So we have

(6.22)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2})\lesssim M^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering the estimates above, and recalling that
B=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}
, the estimate (
6.10
) follows.

To prove (
6.11
), we apply Lemma
6.4
and the fact that
{\mathcal{T}}
is a typical tree again:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q))

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(9Q)\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q).

Recall that
{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}={\mathsf{End}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
. For
Q\in{\mathsf{End}}
we have have
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and so in particular
Q\sim{\mathcal{T}}
(see (
6.1
)). On the other hand, if
Q\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
, then by the construction of the family

{\mathsf{Reg}}
there exists some cube
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}
such that

(6.23)

\ell(Q)\leq\ell(Q^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\,\ell(Q)\quad\text{ and }\quad 20Q^{\prime}\cap 20Q\neq\varnothing.

Hence,
Q\sim{\mathcal{T}}
, and so

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{End}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{DB}}:S\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

taking into account that
{\mathcal{T}}
is a typical tree. This completes the proof of (
6.11
).
Notice also that (
6.12
) follows from (
6.11
) and the fact that the cubes from
{\mathsf{DB}}
satisfy
(
6.19
).

Next we turn our attention to (
6.13
). By Lemma
6.4
we have

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)+\sum_{Q\in({\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2})\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q).

By (
6.11
), the first term on the right hand side above does not exceed
C\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})
. Concerning the second term, we use the fact for the cubes
Q\not\in{\mathsf{DB}}
,
we have
{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim M^{2}\,\sigma(Q)
together with (
6.22
). Then we get

\sum_{Q\in({\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2})\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Regarding the estimate (
6.14
), by (
6.18
) and Lemma

6.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}(({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in({\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2})\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim M^{2}\,\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{2}\,\delta_{0}\,\sigma(R)\leq B\,M^{2}\,\delta_{0}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Together with (
6.11
), this yields (
6.14
).

To get (
6.15
), we apply Hölder’s inequality in the preceding estimate:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}(({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})

\displaystyle\leq(\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}(({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}}))^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(e^{\prime}(R))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(B\,M^{2}\,\delta_{0}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

Observe now that, writing
HD_{i}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{i}}Q
,

(6.24)

\mu(HD_{1})=\frac{1}{\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)^{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\geq\frac{1}{B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)^{2}}\,\sigma(R)=\frac{1}{B\,\Lambda^{2}}\,\mu(R).

Thus, using also that
M\leq\Lambda
,

(6.25)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}(({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(B\,M^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{n+2}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))^{\frac{p}{2}}\,(B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{p}{n+2}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

On the other hand, if we let
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}_{2}}
be the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}}
which
are contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
, by Hölder’s inequality, Lemma

6.4
, and (
6.21
), we get

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}_{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}_{2}})^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{1})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime})\bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{1})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{1})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}=\Lambda^{\frac{-p}{6n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering (
6.25
) and the last estimate, we get (
6.15
).

To prove (
6.16
), recall that
\Theta(Q)\leq\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)
for all
Q\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
This implies that also
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)
for all
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
, by Lemma
3.3
. Consequently,

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})\lesssim\Lambda^{2p}\,\Theta(R)^{p}\,\mu(HD_{2})=\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\approx\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2}).

On the other hand, since
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})

\displaystyle=\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})^{p-2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\leq B\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})^{p-2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle=B\,\frac{\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})^{p-2}}{\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p-2}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})=B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

which completes the proof of (
6.16
).

Finally, observe that (
6.17
) follows from (
6.13
) using Hölder’s inequality and the fact that
the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}
are contained in cubes from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
:

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})\leq\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})^{\frac{p}{2}}\mu(HD_{1})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\lesssim\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mu(HD_{1})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}=\Lambda^{\frac{-p}{6n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

∎

For the record, notice that (
6.20
) also shows that the family
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}
defined just above
(
6.19
) satisfies

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}})=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}{\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q^{\prime}),

which together with (
6.21
) yields

(6.26)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}})\lesssim M^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Lemma 6.8
.

Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
. Then

(6.27)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}^{2}\,B+M^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

and

(6.28)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}^{2}\,B\,\Lambda^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

Recall that
{\mathsf{Neg}}={\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))={\mathsf{Stop}}_{2}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R)),
by the definition of
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
. By Lemma
4.3
, the cubes from
{\mathsf{Neg}}
are not contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}
. Consequently, each cube from
{\mathsf{Neg}}
belongs either to
{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
or to
{\mathsf{OP}}(R)
, and in the latter case
{\mathsf{OP}}(R)={\mathsf{NDB}}(R)
. Thus,
if we denote

{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}}={\mathsf{Neg}}\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R)),\quad\;{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}}={\mathsf{Neg}}\cap{\mathsf{OP}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R)),

it is clear that

{\mathsf{Neg}}={\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}}\cup{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}}.

We can split
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
in an analogous way:

{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}={\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}}}.

Recall that the cubes from
{\mathsf{Neg}}
are not
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling and that
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/3}\,\Theta(R)
for all
Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
.

Regarding
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}})
, from the definition of
{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
we deduce that

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}})\leq\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{LD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))\leq\delta_{0}^{2}\,\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(e^{\prime}(R))\approx\delta_{0}^{2}\,\sigma(R)\leq\delta_{0}^{2}B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

where in the last estimate we used the
{\mathsf{MDW}}
condition.

To estimate
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}})
we just take into account that
{\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}}\subset{\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}}
,
and then by (
6.26
) we have

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}})\leq\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Stop}}_{\mathsf{OP}})\lesssim M^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering the estimates obtained for
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{LD}})
and
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}_{\mathsf{OP}})
we get

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}^{2}\,B+M^{-2}\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

as wished.
The fact that
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})
follows from the fact that if
P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}
satisfies
P\subset Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
, then
{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
because
P
is a maximal

{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube contained in
Q
.

Next we deal with (
6.28
).
We split

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})=\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}).

Notice that, for all
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}
such that
P\subset Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
, by Lemma
3.3
,

{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
. Then it follows that

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}})\lesssim\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}).

To estimate
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}})
, we apply Lemma
6.4
:

(6.29)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(S))\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{DB}}\cap{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S).

By the definition of
{\mathsf{DB}}
and the fact that for
S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}}
with
S\subset Q\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
we have
\Theta(S)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)
,
we derive

\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)\lesssim M^{2}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}\sigma(S)\lesssim M^{2}\,\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}}).

On the other hand, by (
6.11
),

\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{DB}}\cap{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Therefore,

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}})\lesssim M^{2}\,\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering the estimates above, and taking into account that
M<\Lambda
(by (
4.1
)), we deduce

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{2}\,\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}^{2}\,B\,M^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\leq\big(\delta_{0}^{2}\,B\,\Lambda^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

∎

Next lemma deals with
\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}})
. Below we write
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0})
to recall the dependence of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
on the parameter

\ell_{0}
in (
5.8
). Recall that the set
Z
was defined in (
6.3
).

Lemma 6.9
.

For
1\leq p\leq 2
, we have

(6.30)

\limsup_{\ell_{0}\to 0}\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0}))\lesssim\Lambda^{2p}\,\Theta(R)^{p}\,\mu(Z).

Consequently, if
\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
, then

(6.31)

\limsup_{\ell_{0}\to 0}\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0}))\lesssim B\,\Lambda^{4}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

If
x\in Q\in{\mathsf{End}}
with
\ell(Q)\geq\ell_{0}
, then

d_{R,\ell_{0}}(x)\leq\max(\ell_{0},\ell(Q))=\ell(Q)

(recall that
d_{R,\ell_{0}}
is defined in (
5.8
)), and thus
x
is contained in some cube
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
with
\ell(Q^{\prime})\leq\ell(Q)
, by the definition of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}
. So
Q^{\prime}\subset Q
and then
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
. Therefore,

(6.32)

\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}P\subset e^{\prime}(R)\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{End}}:\ell(Q)>\ell_{0}}Q,

and so

(6.33)

\limsup_{\ell_{0}\to 0}\mu\bigg(\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0})}P\bigg)\leq\mu\bigg(e^{\prime}(R)\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{End}}}Q\bigg)=\mu(Z).

To complete the proof of (
6.30
) it just remains to notice that, using the fact that
{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(R)
for all
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
, we have

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0}))\lesssim\Lambda^{2p}\,\Theta(R)^{p}\,\mu\bigg(\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0})}P\bigg).

Regarding the second statement of the lemma recall that, as in (
6.24
),

\mu(HD_{1})\geq\frac{1}{B\,\Lambda^{2}}\,\mu(R)
, which implies that

\mu(Z)\leq B\,\Lambda^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(HD_{1}).

Plugging this estimate into (
6.30
), we get

\limsup_{\ell_{0}\to 0}\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0}))\lesssim B\,\Lambda^{2+p}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p}\,\mu(HD_{1})\leq B\,\Lambda^{4}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

∎

Remark 6.10
.

By Lemma
6.7
, for
p\in(1,2)
, if
\Lambda\gg B\gg 1
and

\delta_{0}\ll\Lambda^{-1}
, we have

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})\ll\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Analogously, by Lemma
6.9
, if
{\varepsilon}_{Z}\ll(B\Lambda^{4})^{-1}
and
\ell_{0}
is small enough,

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}})\ll\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

To transfer the lower estimates for the
L^{p}(\eta)
norm of
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
in Lemma

5.15
to
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mu
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu
, and
\Delta_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
, it would be useful to have estimates for
\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}})
,
\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})
, and
\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Neg}})
analogous to the ones above. However, we have not been able to get them.
This fact originates some technical difficulties, which will be solved in the next lemmas.

Remark 6.11
.

By Lemma
6.7
, for
1<p\leq 2
, and
\ell_{0}
small enough we have

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})\lesssim\Big(B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\Big)\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Also, by Lemma
6.9
, for
\ell_{0}
small enough, if

\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
,

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}(\ell_{0}))\lesssim B\,\Lambda^{4}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

From now on we will assume that
\ell_{0}
small enough so that this holds. Remember also that we chose

B=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}.

We assume also
\delta_{0}
and
{\varepsilon}_{Z}
small enough so that

(6.34)

B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+B\,\Lambda^{4}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}+B\,\Lambda^{2}\,\delta_{0}\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}.

In this way, we have

(6.35)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

under the assumption that
\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
. Also, from (
6.28
), it follows that

(6.36)

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}})\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}\,B\,\Lambda^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Lemma 6.12
.

Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
and that
\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
.
We have

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})\lesssim B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

For
p_{0}=2-\frac{1}{18n}
, we have

\Sigma_{p_{0}}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

By (
6.35
) and (
6.16
), for
1<p\leq 2
,

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}})\\
\lesssim\Big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}+B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\Big)\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Also, by (
6.11
), (
6.13
), and (
6.36
),

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}})\lesssim\big(\delta_{0}\,B\,\Lambda^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\big)\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

by the assumptions on
\delta_{0}
,
\Lambda
, and
B
in Remarks
6.6
and
6.11
.
Choosing
p=2
above and adding the preceding estimates, we get the first statement in the lemma.

To get the second inequality in the lemma, we apply Hölder’s inequality and (
6.24
) in the last estimate, and we get

(6.37)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\big(B\,\Lambda^{2}\mu(HD_{1})\big)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\big(\Lambda^{3}\big)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

For
p=2-\frac{1}{18n}
, we have

\big(\Lambda^{3}\big)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{12n}},

and thus

(6.38)

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{DB}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{12n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Also,

B\,\Lambda^{p-2}=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}\,\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{18n}}=\Lambda^{\frac{-41}{900n}}<\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}},

and thus

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{DB}}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}})\\
\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering the estimates above, the lemma follows.
∎

Remark that in order to transfer the lower estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
to
{\mathcal{R}}\mu
we will need
to take
p
very close to
1
below, in particular with
p<p_{0}
.

Next lemma shows how one can estimate the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
coefficients in terms of the
{\mathcal{P}}
coefficients.

Lemma 6.13
.

For all
p\in(1,\infty)
,

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathsf{Reg}})\lesssim\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}).

Proof.

By duality,

(6.39)

\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{1/p}=\bigg(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p}\,\mu(Q)\bigg)^{1/p}=\sup\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)\,g_{Q}\,\mu(Q),

where the supremum is taken over all sequences
g=\{g_{Q}\}_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}
such that

(6.40)

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}|g_{Q}|^{p^{\prime}}\,\mu(Q)\leq 1.

We will identify the sequence
g
with the function

{\widetilde{g}}=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}g_{Q}\,\chi_{Q},

so that the sum in (
6.40
) equals
\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mu)}^{p^{\prime}}
.
By the definition of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
and Fubini we have

(6.41)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)\,g_{Q}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,\mu(P)\,g_{Q}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\bigg(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,g_{Q}\,\mu(Q)\bigg)\,\mu(P).

For each
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
, we have

(6.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 0}\;\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:2^{j}\ell(P)\leq D(P,Q)\leq 2^{j+1}\ell(P)}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\;\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:D(P,Q)\leq 2^{j+1}\ell(P)}\frac{2^{-j}}{(2^{j}\ell(P))^{n}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q).

Observe now that the condition

\ell(Q)+{\rm dist}(P,Q)\leq D(P,Q)\leq 2^{j+1}\ell(P),

implies that

Q\subset B(x_{Q},\ell(Q))\subset B(x_{P},2^{j+3}\ell(P)).

From (
6.42
) and this fact, we infer that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\;\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}:Q\subset B(x_{P},C2^{j}\ell(P))}\frac{2^{-j}}{(2^{j}\ell(P))^{n}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{2^{-j}}{(2^{j}\ell(P))^{n}}\,\int_{B(x_{P},C2^{j}\ell(P))}|{\widetilde{g}}|\,d\mu

Notice now that, for all
x\in P
,

\displaystyle\int_{B(x_{P},C2^{j}\ell(P))}|{\widetilde{g}}|\,d\mu

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x,C^{\prime}2^{j}\ell(P))}|{\widetilde{g}}|\,d\mu

\displaystyle\leq\mu(B(x,C^{\prime}2^{j}\ell(P)))\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x)\leq\mu(B(x_{P},C^{\prime\prime}2^{j}\ell(P)))\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x),

where
{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}
is the centered Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{2^{-j}\mu(B(x_{P},C^{\prime\prime}2^{j}\ell(P)))}{(2^{j}\ell(P))^{n}}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{-k}\theta_{\mu}(2^{k}B_{P})\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x)\approx{\mathcal{P}}(P)\,\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x).

Plugging this estimate into (
6.41
), and taking the infimum for
x\in P
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)\,|g_{Q}|\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{P}}(P)\,\inf_{x\in P}{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}(x)\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}{\mathcal{P}}(P)^{p}\,\mu(P)\bigg)^{1/p}\bigg(\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}}\int_{P}|{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}|^{p^{\prime}}\,d\mu\bigg)^{1/p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{1/p}\,\|{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}{\widetilde{g}}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mu{\lfloor}_{e^{\prime}(R)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{1/p}\,\|{\widetilde{g}}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mu)}\leq\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{1/p},

which concludes the proof of the lemma, by (
6.39
).
∎

6.3.
Transference of the lower estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
to
\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu

Denote

F=e^{\prime}(R)\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}}Q.

By the splitting (
6.9
), it is clear that
F
coincides with the union of the cubes in the family

{\mathsf{Reg}}_{F}:={\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Neg}}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}.

We also write

F_{\eta}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{F}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q),

so that the measure
\mu|_{F}
is well approximated by
\eta|_{F_{\eta}}
, in a sense.

Recall that in Lemma
5.15
we showed that

\int_{V_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\gtrsim\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

for any
p\in(1,2]
, with
c_{0}
as in Lemma
5.13
.
We will show below that a similar lower estimate holds if we restrict the integral on the
left side to
HD_{2}
. The first step is the next lemma.

We let
{\widetilde{V}}_{4}
be the union of the balls
\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
, with
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}
, that intersect
V_{4}
.

Lemma 6.14
.

Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
and also that
\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
and

\|\Delta_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Then, for
p_{0}=2-\frac{1}{18n}
, assuming
{\varepsilon}_{Z}\leq\Lambda^{-300n}
and
\ell_{0}
small enough,

\int_{F_{\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\eta\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Remark that the proof of this lemma takes advantage of the good estimates we have
obtained for
\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{Reg}})
in Lemma
6.12
. Later on we will show that an analogous estimate holds for all
p\in(1,p_{0})
(see Lemma
6.15
below).

Proof.

Denote

F_{a}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}\setminus{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}Q,\qquad F_{b}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}}Q,\qquad F_{\mathsf{Ot}}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}Q,

so that

F=F_{a}\cup F_{b}\cup F_{\mathsf{Ot}}
.
Write also

{\mathsf{End}}_{a}={\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}\cup{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}

and

{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}={\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}\cup{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}},

so that

F_{a}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}Q=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}}Q.

We also consider

F_{a,\eta}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q),\qquad F_{b,\eta}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q),\qquad F_{{\mathsf{Ot}},\eta}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q),

so that these sets approximate
F_{a},F_{b},F_{\mathsf{Ot}}
at the level of the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}
, in a sense. Moreover, we have
F_{\eta}=F_{a,\eta}\cup F_{b,\eta}\cup F_{{\mathsf{Ot}},\eta}
.

We split

\int_{F_{\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\eta=\int_{F_{a,\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\ldots+\int_{F_{b,\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\ldots+\int_{F_{{\mathsf{Ot}},\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\ldots=:I_{a}+I_{b}+I_{\mathsf{Ot}},

where “
\ldots
” stands for

\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\eta
.

Estimates for
I_{b}
.

We claim that
I_{b}=0
. Indeed, given
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}\setminus{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}
, notice that all the cubes
S
such that
Q\subset S\subset R
satisfy

\Theta(S)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R),

and so, for any
x\in Q
,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\theta_{\eta}(4B_{S})\lesssim\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\theta_{\mu}(CB_{S})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S:Q\subset S\subset R}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\Theta(R)+\Theta(R)\lesssim\Theta(R).

Hence, for
\Lambda
big enough,
(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}=0
, which proves our claim.

Estimates for
I_{a}
.

By Lemma
6.1
, for all
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}
such that
\frac{1}{2}B(Q)\subset{\widetilde{V}}_{4}
, all
x\in\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
, and all
y\in Q
,

|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|+C\Theta(R)+C{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+C{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

Thus, for
\Lambda
big enough, since
C\Theta(R)=C\Lambda^{-1}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})<\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})
,

(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\leq(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}+C{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+C{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

Consequently,

(6.43)

\displaystyle I_{a}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu(y)+\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}}({\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{p_{0}}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p_{0}})\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu+\Sigma_{p_{0}}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}),

where, in the last inequality, we used the fact that all the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}

are contained in some cube
S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}
and we applied Lemma
6.13
.

For each
S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}
, by the triangle inequality and the fact that
(\;\cdot\;)_{+}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function, we get

\displaystyle\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{S}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu

By Lemma
6.3
, the last integral does not exceed

C{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}
, and thus we deduce that

(6.44)

I_{a}\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}+\Sigma_{p_{0}}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}).

Next we apply Lemma
6.2
, which ensures that
for any
S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}\subset{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}
and all
x\in S
,

(6.45)

|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)|\leq|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)|+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2S)}{\mu(S)}\right)^{1/2}.

In case that
R\not\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, recalling that

\Lambda\geq\max(M^{\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}},CM)\gg M
by (
4.1
), we obtain

(6.46)

C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\,M\,\Theta(R)\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

In case that
R\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, since
R\in{\mathsf{Ty}}
, we have

{\mathcal{E}}(4R)\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}:Q\sim{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

and so we also get

(6.47)

C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\,\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Thus, in any case,

(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\leq|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)|+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2S)}{\mu(S)}\right)^{1/2}.

Plugging this estimate into (
6.44
), we get

(6.48)

I_{a}\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}\int_{S}|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}+\Sigma_{p_{0}}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}).

We deal with each term on the right hand side of the preceding inequality separately. First, by Hölder’s inequality and the assumptions in the lemma, we have

\int|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu\lesssim\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p_{0}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\leq\Lambda^{-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}.

Regarding the second term in (
6.48
), by Hölder’s inequality again,

\displaystyle\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}\mu(S)\bigg)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}.

We estimate the first factor on the right hand side using (
6.13
), (
6.14
), and (
6.28
):

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{End}}_{a}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{OP}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{OP}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{LD}}_{1}\cup{\mathsf{LD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Neg}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9S)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(B\Lambda^{-1}\!+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}+B\,M^{2}\,\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{n+2}}+B\,\Lambda^{7}\delta_{0}\big)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}},

by the assumption (
6.34
) on
\delta_{0}
.
In connection with the last summand on the right hand side of (
6.48
), by Lemma
6.12

we have

\Sigma_{p_{0}}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Therefore,

I_{a}\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

From (
6.24
) we derive that

\mu(HD_{1})\geq\Lambda^{-3}\,\mu(R)
, and then

(6.49)

\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\big(\Lambda^{3}\big)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})=\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{12n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

So we get

(6.50)

I_{a}\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Estimate of
I_{\mathsf{Ot}}
.

By the same arguments as in (
6.43
), just replacing
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}
by
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
, we obtain

I_{\mathsf{Ot}}\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu(y)+\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}({\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{p_{0}}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p_{0}})\,\mu(Q).

Thus, using again Lemmas
6.13
and
6.12
, we get

(6.51)

\displaystyle I_{\mathsf{Ot}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu(y)+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle=:{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

To estimate the integral
{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
on the right hand side, we split

\displaystyle{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle={\widetilde{I}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}},1}+{\widetilde{I}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}},2}.

Notice that, by definition we have
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
.
Recall that
{\mathsf{Neg}}={\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\cap{\mathsf{End}}
and thus we may have
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\neq\varnothing
.
In this case, we have
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))
(because
{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(R))
by construction).

The same argument used to show that
I_{b}=0
shows
that

{\widetilde{I}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}},2}=0.

To estimate
{\widetilde{I}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}},1}
,
denote by
{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
the family of maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))

and let

N_{\mathsf{Ot}}=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))}Q\setminus\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}P.

We claim that

(6.52)

{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}\quad\text{ and }\quad N_{\mathsf{Ot}}\subset Z.

To check this, for a given
P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
with
P\subset Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
, suppose there exists
S\in{\mathsf{End}}
such that
S\supset P
. As
P
is a contained in some
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
, we have

S\not\in{\mathsf{Neg}}
. Further,
S\subsetneq Q
because
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}
implies that
Q\not\subset S
. Since
S
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, we deduce that
P=S
, by the maximality of
P
as
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube contained in
Q
. An analogous argument shows that
N_{\mathsf{Ot}}\subset Z
.

By Hölder’s inequality and (
6.33
), for
\ell_{0}
small enough we have

\displaystyle{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{8}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{2}\,d\mu(x)\big)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}\mu(Q)\bigg)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}\int_{P}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu+\int_{N_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\bigg(\mu(Z)+o(\ell_{0})\bigg)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}},

with
o(\ell_{0})\to 0
as
\ell_{0}\to 0
.

Denote

{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\mu(x)=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\chi_{P}(x)\,{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2P}\mu)(x)

and

\Delta_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\chi_{P}(x)\,\big(m_{\mu,P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)+\chi_{Z}(x)\big({\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big).

Notice that, for
x\in P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
and
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(R))
such that
Q\supset P
, since there are no
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes
P^{\prime}
such that
P\subsetneq P^{\prime}\subset Q
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\mu(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)\big|=|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2Q\setminus 2P}\mu)(x)|\lesssim\sum_{P:P\subset P^{\prime}\subset Q}\Theta(P^{\prime})\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim\Lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Almost the same argument shows also that, for
x\in N_{\mathsf{Ot}}
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(x)\big|\lesssim\Lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Remark also that

\int_{N_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R}\mu)\big|^{2}\,d\mu\leq\int_{Z}\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R}\mu)\big|^{2}\,d\mu.

So we deduce that, for
\ell_{0}
small enough,

{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}\int_{P}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\mu\big|^{2}\,d\mu+\int_{Z}\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R}\mu)\big|^{2}\,d\mu+\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{2}\,\mu(R)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\big({\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)\big)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}.

Almost the same arguments as in Lemma
6.2
show that for
x\in P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}\mu(x)-\Delta_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+{\mathcal{P}}(P)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2P)}{\mu(P)}\right)^{1/2}

and that, for
x\in Z
,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)-\Delta_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}.

Therefore, by (
6.46
) and (
6.47
) and taking into account that
{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim\Lambda\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})
for

P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}
, we deduce

{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\lesssim\bigg(\int|\Delta_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{2}\,d\mu+\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2P)+\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{2}\,\mu(R)\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\big({\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)\big)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}.

By the orthogonality of the functions
\Delta_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
,
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
, the assumptions in the lemma,
and (
6.52
),
it is clear that

\int|\Delta_{{\mathsf{M}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{2}\,d\mu\leq\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

On the other hand, since the tree
{\mathcal{T}}
is typical,

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2P)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2P)+\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{T}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2P)\leq M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{M}}_{{\mathsf{Ot}}})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{2}\,\mu(R)+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\lesssim\Lambda^{4}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{2}\,\mu(R).

Thus, using also (
6.24
),

\displaystyle{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-p_{0}}{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\mu(R)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}+{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,\Lambda^{2p_{0}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p_{0}}\,\mu(R)

\displaystyle\lesssim(B\Lambda^{2})^{{}^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}}\Lambda^{\frac{-p_{0}}{2}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})+{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{2}}\,B\,\Lambda^{2+{2p_{0}}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

By the choice of
p_{0}
,
\Lambda
, and
B
and the assumption
{\varepsilon}_{Z}\leq\Lambda^{-300n},
we have

{\widetilde{I}}_{\mathsf{Ot}}\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{2}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Together with (
6.51
), this yields

I_{\mathsf{Ot}}\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Gathering the estimates obtained for
I_{a}
and
I_{\mathsf{Ot}}
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 6.15
.

Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
and also that
\mu(Z)\leq{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
and

\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Assume also that

{\varepsilon}_{Z}\leq\Lambda^{-300n}
and
\ell_{0}
is small enough.
Then

\eta\big(\big\{x\in{\widetilde{V}}_{4}:|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|>\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big\}\big)\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\mu(HD_{1}).

Also, for all
p\in(1,p_{0})
,

\int_{F_{\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

Denote

A=\big\{x\in{\widetilde{V}}_{4}:|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|>\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big\}.

By the definition of
F_{\eta}
we can split

\eta(A)\leq\eta(A\cap F_{\eta})+\eta\bigg(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q)\bigg).

Notice first that, since
R\in{\mathsf{Trc}}
,

(6.53)

\eta\bigg(\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\frac{1}{2}B(Q)\bigg)=\frac{1}{\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})^{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\leq\frac{B}{\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\leq\Lambda^{-1}\,\mu(HD_{1}).

On the other hand, for
x\in A
we have

(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\geq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

So, by Chebyshev and Lemma
6.14
,

\displaystyle\eta(A\cap F_{\eta})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p_{0}}}\int_{F_{\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})}{\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})^{p_{0}}}=\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\mu(HD_{1}),

which, together with (
6.53
), proves the first assertion of the lemma.

For the second statement in the lemma we use Hölder and Lemma
6.14
again:

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\eta}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{4}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{A\cap F_{\eta}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{A\cap F_{\eta}}\!\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p_{0}}d\eta\right)^{\frac{p}{p_{0}}}\!\eta(A\cap F_{\eta})^{1-\frac{p}{p_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p_{0}}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{p_{0}}}\,\big(\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\mu(HD_{1})\big)^{1-\frac{p}{p_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

∎

Observe that, given
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}\cap{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
, from Lemmas
5.15
and
6.15
, under
the assumptions in those lemmas, we derive that

(6.54)

\displaystyle\int_{V_{4}\cap HD_{2}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta(x)

\displaystyle\geq c\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})-C\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{25n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\approx\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

for
p\in(1,p_{0}]
, assuming that
{\varepsilon}_{n}
and
p
are chosen so that

(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}\ll\frac{1}{25n}.

This is the main ingredient for the proof of the next lemma, which is the main result
of this section.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}\cap{\mathsf{Trc}}\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
. Let
\Lambda>0
be big enough and suppose that

{\varepsilon}_{Z}\leq\Lambda^{-300n}
.
Then one of the following alternatives holds:

(a)

\mu(Z)>{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(R)
, or

(b)

\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}>\Lambda^{-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Proof.

Suppose that none of the alternatives holds. Then, by Lemmas
5.15
and
6.15
,
as in (
6.54
), we have

(6.55)

I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}:=\int_{V_{4}\cap HD_{2}}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\eta(x)\gtrsim\Lambda^{-(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

for all
p\in(1,p_{0}]
,
assuming that
\ell_{0}
is chosen small enough and that

(6.56)

{(p^{\prime}+1){\varepsilon}_{n}}\leq\frac{1}{50n}.

The appropriate values of
{\varepsilon}_{n}
and
p
will be chosen at the end of the proof.

By arguments analogous to the ones we used to estimate the integral
I_{a}
in the proof of Lemma

6.14
we will “transfer” the estimate for
{\mathcal{R}}\eta
in (
6.55
) to
\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
, so that we will obtain a lower estimate for
\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}
which will contradict the assumption that (b) does not hold.
Although some of the estimates below are very similar to the ones to obtain the inequality
(
6.48
) in the proof of Lemma

6.14
, we will include the full details here for the reader’s convenience.
On the other hand, an important difference between the proof of that lemma and the current proof is that in Lemma

6.14
we took advantage of the fact that
p_{0}
is close to
2
, and the estimates there would not work for the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}
, while in the arguments below it is essential that we take
p
close to
1
, and the estimates work fine for the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}
,
while they would fail for the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{a}
.

By Lemma
6.1
, for all
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}
such that
\frac{1}{2}B(Q)\subset{\widetilde{V}}_{4}
, all
x\in\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
, and all
y\in Q
,

|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|\leq|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|+C\Theta(R)+C{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+C{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

Thus, for
\Lambda
big enough, since
\Theta(R)=\Lambda^{-1}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})<\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})
,

(|{\mathcal{R}}\eta(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\leq(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}+C{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+C{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q).

Therefore,

I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}}\!\int_{Q}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\mu(y)+\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}}\!\!\!\!({\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{p}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p})\,\mu(Q).

By (
6.16
) and Lemma
6.13
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}}({\mathcal{P}}(Q)^{p}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}(Q)^{p})\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})+\Sigma_{p}^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{Q}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})+\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

Also, recalling that
\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})\leq B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})
, we get

\mu(HD_{2})\leq B\,\Lambda^{-2}\,\mu(HD_{1})
. Then, by Lemma
6.12
, we obtain

\Sigma^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathsf{Reg}})^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\leq\big(B\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\big(B\,\Lambda^{-2}\,\mu(HD_{1})\big)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}=B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

So, since any cube from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}

is contained in some cube
S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
:

I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\!\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu(y)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\mu(y)+B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

For each
S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
, by the triangle inequality and the fact that
(\;\cdot\;)_{+}
is a
1
-Lipschitz function, we obtain

\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\mu\lesssim\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\mu\\
+\int_{S}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu-{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}\mu\big|^{p}\,d\mu

By Lemma
6.3
, the last integral does not exceed

C{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}
, and thus

(6.57)
