I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\lesssim\!\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\int_{S}\big|(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\big|^{p}\,d\mu+\!\!\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\!\!{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p}{2}}\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}+B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Next we apply Lemma
6.2
, which implies that
for any
S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}
and all
x\in S
,

(6.58)

|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)|\leq|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)|+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2S)}{\mu(S)}\right)^{1/2}.

In case that
R\not\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, recalling that

\Lambda\gg M
by (
4.1
), for
\Lambda
big enough we obtain

C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\,M\,\Theta(R)\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

If
R\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, then we use the fact that
R\in{\mathsf{Ty}}
, which ensures that

{\mathcal{E}}(4R)\lesssim\sum_{P\sim{\mathcal{T}}:P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

and so we also get

C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\,\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{6n}}\,\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Hence, in any case we have

(|{\mathcal{R}}_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}\mu(x)|-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\Theta({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}))_{+}\leq|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)|+C\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2S)}{\mu(S)}\right)^{1/2}.

Plugging this estimate into (
6.57
), we get

(6.59)

I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\lesssim\int_{HD_{2}}|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{p}\,d\mu+\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}+B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Next we will estimate each term on the right hand side. First, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{HD_{2}}|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu|^{p}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}\,\mu(HD_{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}\,(B\Lambda^{-2}\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\leq\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}(\Lambda^{-1}\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

Regarding the second term in (
6.59
), by Hölder’s inequality again,

(6.60)

\displaystyle\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)\bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(HD_{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}.

We estimate now the first factor on the right hand side:

\displaystyle\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)+\sum_{S\sim{\mathcal{T}}:S\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{2}\,\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}}\sigma(S)+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{2})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq B\,M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})+\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\lesssim B\,M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Hence, plugging this estimate into (
6.60
) and using that
\mu(HD_{2})\leq B\,\Lambda^{-2}\mu(HD_{1})
,

\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}{\mathcal{E}}(2S)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\mu(S)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\lesssim\big(B\,M^{2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,\big(B\,\Lambda^{-2}\mu(HD_{1})\big)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}=M^{p}\,B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Altogether, we deduce that

I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}\,(\Lambda^{-1}\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}+M^{p}\,B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Recalling the lower estimate for
I_{{\mathsf{HD}}_{2}}
in (
6.55
), using that
p^{\prime}\geq 2
we obtain

(6.61)

\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}\,(\Lambda^{-1}\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\geq c\,\Lambda^{-2p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})-C\,M^{p}\,B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

Recall now that by (
4.1
)

M\leq\Lambda^{1-\frac{1}{8n-1}}\ll\Lambda.

Notice that for
p
close enough to
1
, we have
M^{p}\,B\,\Lambda^{p-2}\ll 1
, so that the last term on the right hand side of (
6.61
) is much smaller than the first one, assuming
{\varepsilon}_{n}
close enough to
0
. To be more precise, let us take

p=1+\frac{1}{4(8n-1)}.

A straightforward calculation gives
M^{p}\,\Lambda^{p-2}\leq\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2(8n-1)}-\frac{1}{4(8n-1)^{2}}}
, so that

B\,M^{p}\,\Lambda^{p-2}\leq\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2(8n-1)}}\leq\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{4(8n-1)}}.

Then we choose
{\varepsilon}_{n}
so that, besides (
6.56
), it satisfies

(6.62)

{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq\frac{1}{16(8n-1)}\,(p-1)=\frac{1}{64(8n-1)^{2}},

and we derive

\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{p}\,(\Lambda^{-1}\mu(HD_{1}))^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\gtrsim\Lambda^{-2p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}),

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\big(\Lambda^{-2p^{\prime}{\varepsilon}_{n}}\,\,\Lambda^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\,\sigma_{p}({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\,\mu(HD_{1})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\big)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{-\frac{4{\varepsilon}_{n}}{p-1}+\frac{2}{p}-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1})\gg\Lambda^{-1}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}).

This contradicts the assumption that the alternative (b) in the lemma does not hold.
∎

Part 3: The proof of the First Main Proposition

In this part, corresponding to Sections
7
-
10
we choose
{\mathsf{OP}}(R)=\varnothing
for any
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
.

7.
The corona decomposition and the Main Lemma

In order to prove the Main Proposition
3.6
we have to use a suitable corona decomposition which splits
the lattice
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
into appropriate trees. We need first need to introduce some variant of the family
{\mathsf{HD}}(R)
, for
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
.
For
N=500n
, we denote

\Lambda_{*}=\Lambda^{\frac{N}{N-1}}=A_{0}^{\frac{N}{N-1}k_{\Lambda}n},

so that

\Lambda=\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}.
We assume that
k_{\Lambda}
is a multiple of
N-1
, so that
k_{\Lambda_{*}}:=\frac{N}{N-1}k_{\Lambda}

is an integer.
Then we set

{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)={\mathsf{hd}}^{k_{\Lambda_{*}}}(R).

Further, we assume that the constant
\delta_{0}
in the definition of the family
{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
in Section
4.1
is of the form

\delta_{0}=\Lambda_{*}^{-N_{0}-\frac{1}{2N}},

with
N_{0}
large enough so that moreover
\delta_{0}\leq\Lambda^{-4n^{2}}
, as required in Section
4.1
.

We denote by
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R)
the family of maximal cubes from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)\cup{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
which are contained in
R
.
Also, we let
{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)
be the family of maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained in some cube
from
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R)
. Notice that, by Lemma
3.2
, the cubes from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(4R)
are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, and thus
any cube from
{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R)\cap{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)
belongs to
{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)
. Finally, we let
{\rm Tree}(R)
denote the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(R)
which are not strictly contained in any cube
from
{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)
, and we say that
R
is the root of the tree.

Next we define the family
{\mathsf{Top}}
inductively.
We assume that
\operatorname{supp}\mu
coincides with a cube
S_{0}
, and then
we set
{\mathsf{Top}}_{0}=\{S_{0}\}
. Assuming
{\mathsf{Top}}_{k}
to be defined, we let

{\mathsf{Top}}_{k+1}=\bigcup_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}_{k}}{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R).

Then we let

{\mathsf{Top}}=\bigcup_{k\geq 0}{\mathsf{Top}}_{k}.

Notice that we have

{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}=\bigcup_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}{\rm Tree}(R).

Two trees
{\rm Tree}(R)
,
{\rm Tree}(R^{\prime})
, with
R,R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Top}}
,
R\neq R^{\prime}
can only intersect if one
of the roots is and ending cube of the other, i.e.,
R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)
or
R\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R^{\prime})
.

The main step for the proof of Main Proposition
3.6
consists of proving the following.

Main Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\mu
be a Radon measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
such that

\mu(B(x,r))\leq\theta_{0}\,r^{n}\quad\mbox{ for all $x\in\operatorname{supp}\mu$ and all $r>0$}.

Then, for any choice of
M>1
,

(7.1)

\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(R)\leq C\,\big(\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\big),

with
C
depending on
M
.

The rest of the current section, together with Sections
8
and
9
are devoted to the proof of this lemma. Later, in Section
10
we will complete the
proof of Main Proposition
3.6
.

Recall that a cube
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
belongs to

{\mathsf{MDW}}
if
R
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling and

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R))\geq B^{-1}\,\sigma(R),

where

B=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}.

Lemma 7.2
.

For
R\in{\mathsf{Top}}\setminus{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we have

(7.2)

\sigma({\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R))\leq 2\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}B^{-1}\,\sigma(R).

Proof.

We have

\sigma({\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R))=\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R))+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R):P\subset Q}\sigma(Q).

Clearly, since any cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)
is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}(R)
, we infer that

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)\cap{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R))

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{*}^{2}\,\Theta(R)^{2}\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)\cap{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)}\!\!\mu(Q)\leq\Lambda_{*}^{2}\,\Theta(R)^{2}\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)}\!\!\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda_{*}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R))\leq\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}B^{-1}\,\sigma(R),

where in the last inequality we used that
R\notin{\mathsf{MDW}}
.
For
P\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R)
, there exists some
Q
such that
P\subset Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}(R)
. By (
3.6
) we have

\Theta(P)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\leq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R),

and thus

\sigma({\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R))\leq\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}B^{-1}\,\sigma(R)+C\delta_{0}^{2}\,\sigma(R)\leq 2\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}B^{-1}\,\sigma(R),

since
\delta_{0}\ll B^{-1}
.
∎

Observe that
N=500n
is big enough so that

2\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}\,B^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Lemma 7.3
.

We have

\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})\lesssim\sigma({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|.

Proof.

For each
R\in\{S_{0}\}\cup({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})
, we denote

I_{0}(R)=\{R\}
, and for
k\geq 0
,

I_{k+1}(R)=\bigcup_{Q\in I_{k}(R)}{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(Q)\setminus{\mathsf{MDW}}.

In this way, we have

(7.3)

{\mathsf{Top}}=\bigcup_{R\in\{S_{0}\}\cup({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})}\;\bigcup_{k\geq 0}I_{k}(R).

Indeed, for each
Q\in{\mathsf{Top}}
, let
R
be the minimal cube from
{\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}}
that contains
Q
, and in case this does not exists, let
R=S_{0}
. Then it follows that

Q\in\bigcup_{k\geq 0}I_{k}(R).

Given
R\in\{S_{0}\}\cup({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})
, for each
k\geq 1
and
Q\in I_{k}(R)
,
since
I_{k}(R)\subset{\mathsf{Top}}\setminus{\mathsf{MDW}}
, by (
7.2
) we have

\sigma({\mathsf{End}}_{*}(Q)\setminus{\mathsf{MDW}})\leq 2\Lambda_{*}^{2/N}B^{-1}\,\sigma(Q)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\sigma(Q).

Then we deduce that

\sigma(I_{k+1}(R))=\sum_{Q\in I_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{End}}_{*}(Q)\setminus{\mathsf{MDW}})\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Q\in I_{k}(R)}\sigma(Q)=\frac{1}{2}\,\sigma(I_{k}(R)).

So

\sigma(I_{k}(R))\leq\frac{1}{2^{k}}\,\sigma(R)\quad\mbox{ for each $k\geq 0$.}

Then, by (
7.3
),

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\{S_{0}\}\cup({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})}\;\sum_{k\geq 0}\sigma(I_{k}(R))\leq\sum_{R\in\{S_{0}\}\cup({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})}\;\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\,\sigma(R)

\displaystyle\approx\sigma(S_{0})+\sigma({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})\lesssim\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|+\sigma({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}}).

∎

8.
The layers
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
and
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}

Consider an arbtrary subfamily
{\mathsf{F}}\subset{\mathsf{MDW}}
. Quite soon we will choose
{\mathsf{F}}={\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}}
, but in another application of this construction later on, we will choose
{\mathsf{F}}
to be a different subfamily.
For
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
we denote

{\mathsf{F}}_{j}=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{F}}:\,\Theta(R)=A_{0}^{nj}\big\},

so that

{\mathsf{F}}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}.

Next we split
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}
into layers
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
,
h\geq 1
, which are defined as follows:

{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{1}
is the family of maximal cubes from
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}
, and by induction

{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
is the family of maximal cubes from
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}\setminus\bigcup_{k=1}^{h-1}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h-1}
.
So we have the splitting

{\mathsf{F}}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,\bigcup_{h\geq 1}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}.

Our next objective is to choose a suitable subfamily
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}\subset{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
, for each
j,h
.
By the covering Theorem 9.31 from
[
To5
]
, there
is a family
J_{0}\subset{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
such that
7
7

7

Actually the property 1) is not stated in that theorem, however this can be obtained by preselecting a subfamily of maximal balls from
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}

with respect to inclusion and then applying the theorem to the maximal subfamily.

1)

no ball
B(e^{(4)}(Q))
, with
Q\in J_{0}
, is contained in any other ball

B(e^{(4)}(Q^{\prime}))
, with
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
,
Q^{\prime}\neq Q
,

2)

the balls
B(e^{(4)}(Q))
, with
Q\in J_{0}
, have finite superposition,
and

3)

every ball
B(e^{(4)}(Q))
, with
Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
, is contained in some ball

(1+8A_{0}^{-1})\,B(e^{(4)}(R))
, with
R\in J_{0}
. Consequently,

\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}B(e^{(4)}(Q))\subset\bigcup_{R\in J_{0}}(1+8A_{0}^{-1})\,B(e^{(4)}(R)).

From the finite superposition property 2), by rather standard arguments which are analogous to the
ones in the proof of Besicovitch’s covering theorem in
[
Ma
, Theorem 2.7]
, say,
one deduces that
J_{0}
can be split into

m_{0}
subfamilies
J_{1},\ldots,J_{m_{0}}
such that, for each
k
, the balls
\{B(e^{(4)}(Q)):Q\in J_{k}\}
are pairwise disjoint, with
m_{0}\leq C(A_{0})
.

By the condition 3),
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{2}A_{0}^{2nj}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q)}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{2}A_{0}^{2nj}\sum_{R\in J_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}:\\
B(e^{(4)}(Q))\subset(1+8A_{0}^{-1})B(e^{(4)}(R))\end{subarray}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q)}\mu(P).

Observe that, for
R\in J_{0}
and
Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
such that
B(e^{(4)}(Q))\subset(1+8A_{0}^{-1})\,B(e^{(4)}(R))
,
any cube
P\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q)
is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{(10)}(R))
since

\operatorname{supp}\mu\cap(1+8A_{0}^{-1})B(e^{(4)}(R))\subset e^{(10)}(R)
, by Lemma
4.2
.
Using also that the cubes from
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
are disjoint and Lemma
4.1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{2}A_{0}^{2nj}\sum_{R\in J_{0}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{(10)}(R))}\mu(P)

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in J_{0}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{(10)}(R)))\leq B^{1/4}\sum_{R\in J_{0}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))).

Next we choose
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}=J_{k}
to be the family such that

\sum_{Q\in J_{k}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

is maximal among
J_{1},\ldots,J_{m_{0}}
, so that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))\leq m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

So we have:

Lemma 8.1
.

The family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
satisfies:

(i)

no ball
B(e^{(4)}(Q))
, with
Q\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
, is contained in any other ball

B(e^{(4)}(Q^{\prime}))
, with
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
,
Q^{\prime}\neq Q
,

(ii)

the balls
B(e^{(4)}(Q))
, with
Q\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
, are pairwise disjoint,
and

(iii)

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(Q))\lesssim B^{1/4}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

We denote

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}=\bigcup_{h\geq 1}{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h},\qquad{\mathsf{L}}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{j}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,\bigcup_{h\geq 1}{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}.

By the property (iii) in the lemma, we have

(8.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{F}}}\!\!\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}},\,h\geq 0}\,\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R))

\displaystyle\lesssim m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}},\,h\geq 0}\,\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))=m_{0}B^{1/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))).

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
{\mathsf{F}}={\mathsf{MDW}}\cap{\mathsf{Top}}
. Then we have

\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})\lesssim B^{5/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|.

Recall that the families
{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
have been defined in Section
4.3
, just before Lemma
4.5
. We assume that
{\mathsf{OP}}(Q)=\varnothing
for any
Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
in this constrcution.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
7.3
and our earlier estimates:

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma({\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}})+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:MDWdef2}}{\lesssim}B\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}\cap{\mathsf{MDW}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R))+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|

\displaystyle\overset{{(\ref{eqover5})}}{\lesssim}B^{5/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqiter*44}}{\lesssim}B^{5/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|.

∎

To be able to apply later the preceding lemma, we need to get an estimate for
\#{\mathsf{L}}(P,k)
, where
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu},\,k\geq 0
and

(8.2)

{\mathsf{L}}(P,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}.

For
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
set also

(8.3)

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\},

so that
{\mathsf{L}}(P,k)=\bigcup_{j}{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
. The following important technical result is the main achievement in this section.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
{\mathsf{F}}={\mathsf{MDW}}\cap{\mathsf{Top}}
. In this case there exists some constant
C_{4}
such that, for all
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and all
k\geq 0
,

\#{\mathsf{L}}(P,k)\leq C_{4}\,\log\Lambda.

More precisely, for each
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and
k\geq 0

(8.4)

\#\{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}:{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\neq\varnothing\}\lesssim\log\Lambda,

and for each
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
,
k\geq 0
,

(8.5)

\#{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\leq C_{5}.

We prove first (
8.4
).

Proof of (
8.4
).

Let
{\widetilde{P}}_{1}
be the smallest
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube containing
P
, and let
{\widetilde{P}}_{2}
be be the smallest
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube strictly containing
{\widetilde{P}}_{1}
. Suppose that
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
. There are two cases to consider.

Case 1.
There exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
such that
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
. We claim that in this case we have
{\widetilde{P}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
. Indeed, if
P\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, then necessarily
{\widetilde{P}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, by the definition of
the family
{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
. If
P\notin{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q)),
then either:

•

P={\widetilde{P}}_{1},
which implies
P\in{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, in which case
{\widetilde{P}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, or

•

P\neq{\widetilde{P}}_{1}
and we have
{\widetilde{P}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, again by the definition of
{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.

Choosing
i\in\{1,2\}
such that
{\widetilde{P}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
we see by the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
that

\delta_{0}\,\Theta(Q)\lesssim\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{i})\leq\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(Q).

Since
\Theta(Q)=\Lambda^{k}\Theta(R)
(by the definition of
{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
), the above is equivalent to

\Lambda^{-2}\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{i})\leq\Lambda^{k}\Theta(R)\leq C\delta_{0}^{-1}\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{i})

We have
\Theta(R)=A_{0}^{nj}
because
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
, and so it follows that

-C\log\Lambda\leq j+c\,k\log\Lambda-c^{\prime}\log\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{i})\leq C|\log\delta_{0}|=C^{\prime}\log\Lambda.

Recall that
k\geq 0
is fixed, and
\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{i})
is equal to either
\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{1})
or
\Theta({\widetilde{P}}_{2})
, where both of these values depend only on
P
, which is fixed. Thus, there are at most
C^{\prime\prime}\log\Lambda
integers
j
such that there exists
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
and
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
for which
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.

Case 2.

Suppose now that there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
such that
P\in{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\subset{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.
In this case, by Lemma
4.3
,
\ell(P)\gtrsim\delta_{0}^{-2}\,\ell(Q)
. Hence, there
are at most
C\,|\log\delta_{0}|\approx\log\Lambda
cubes
Q
such that

P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.

For each such cube we have
\Theta(Q)=\Lambda^{k}\Theta(R)=\Lambda^{k}A_{0}^{nj}
. Thus, for each cube
Q
as above there is exactly one value of
j
such that there may exist
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
with
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
. It follows that there are at most

C^{\prime\prime\prime}\log\Lambda
values of
j
such that
there exists
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
and
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
for which
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.

Putting the estimates from both cases together we get that
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
is non-empty for at most
(C^{\prime\prime}+C^{\prime\prime\prime})\log\Lambda
integers
j
.
∎

The proof of (
8.5
) is more involved. Its key ingredient is the following auxiliary result.

Lemma 8.4
.

There exists some positive integer

N_{1}
depending on
n
(with
N_{1}\leq C\,N_{0}N
) such that the following holds.
For a given
\theta>0
, consider the interval

I_{\theta}=\big(\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4N}}\delta_{0},\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{1}{4N}}\Lambda\big).

Let
R_{1},R_{2},\ldots,R_{N_{1}}
be cubes from
{\mathsf{Top}}
such that
R_{k+1}\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{k})
for
k\geq 1
.
Then at least one of the cubes
R_{k}
, with
1\leq k\leq N_{1}
, satisfies

\Theta(R_{k})\not\in I_{\theta}.

Proof.

Recall that

\Lambda=\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\delta_{0}=\Lambda_{*}^{-N_{0}-\frac{1}{2N}},

so that

I_{\theta}=\big(\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-N_{0}-\frac{3}{4N}},\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{3}{4N}}\big).

Consider a sequence
R_{1},R_{2},\ldots,R_{N_{1}}
of cubes from
{\mathsf{Top}}
such that
R_{k+1}\in{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{k})
for
k\geq 1
. By the definition of
{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{k})
there are only two possibilities: either
R_{k+1}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{k})
, or
R_{k+1}
is a maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{k})
. Note that in the former case we have
\Theta(R_{k+1})=\Lambda_{*}\Theta(R_{k})
, and in the latter case we have
\Theta(R_{k+1})\leq C\delta_{0}\Theta(R_{k})
, by (
3.6
) and the definition of
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{k})
.

The key observation is the following:

(8.6)

\Theta(R_{k})\leq\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{3N}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mbox{either \;$\Theta(R_{k+1})\not\in I_{\theta}$ \;or\;
$\Theta(R_{k+1})=\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{k})$.}

This follows from the fact that, in the case
\Theta(R_{k+1})\in I_{\theta}
, we have

R_{k+1}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{k})
because otherwise

\Theta(R_{k+1})\leq C\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R_{k})\leq C\,\theta\,\delta_{0}\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{3N}}\leq\theta\,\delta_{0}\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{4N}}.

Analogously,

(8.7)

\Theta(R_{k})\geq\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-3}{4N}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mbox{either \;$\Theta(R_{k+1})\not\in I_{\theta}$ \;or\;
$\Theta(R_{k+1})\leq C\delta_{0}\Theta(R_{k})$,}

because, in the case
\Theta(R_{k+1})\in I_{\theta}
, we have

R_{k+1}\not\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{k})
,
since otherwise

\Theta(R_{k+1})=\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{k})\geq\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{3}{4N}}.

To prove the lemma, suppose that
\Theta(R_{1})\in I_{\theta}
. Otherwise we are done.
By applying (
8.6
)
N_{0}+1
times, we deduce that either

\Theta(R_{k})\not\in I_{\theta}
for some
k\in(1,N_{0}+2]
, or there exists some
k_{1}\in[1,N_{0}+1]
such that

\Theta(R_{k_{1}})\geq\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{3N}}.

Then, from (
8.7
), we deduce that either

\Theta(R_{k_{1}+1})\not\in I_{\theta}
, or

(8.8)

\Theta(R_{k_{1}+1})\leq C\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R_{k_{1}})\leq C\,\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{3}{4N}}\leq\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{1-\frac{1}{3N}}.

Now we have:

Claim 8.5
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
a\in(0,1)
. Suppose that

\Theta(R_{k})\in(\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{3N}},\,\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{a}).

Then, either there exists some

k_{2}\in[k+1,k+N_{0}+1]
such that
\Theta(R_{k_{2}})\not\in I_{\theta}
, or

\Theta(R_{k+N_{0}+1})\in(\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4N}},\,\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{a-\frac{1}{3N}}).

In case that
a\leq\frac{1}{12N}
, one should understand that the second alternative is not possible.

Proof.

Suppose that the first alternative in the claim does not hold.
Then we deduce that

\Theta(R_{k+j})=\Lambda_{*}^{j}\,\Theta(R_{k})\quad\mbox{ for $j=1,\ldots,N_{0}$,}

because, for all
j=1,\ldots,N_{0}-1
,

\Theta(R_{k+j})\leq\Lambda_{*}^{j}\,\Theta(R_{k})\leq\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{N_{0}-1+a}=\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{2N}-1+a}\leq\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{2N}},

and then (
8.6
) implies that
\Theta(R_{k+j+1})=\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{k+j})
.
So we infer that

\Theta(R_{k+N_{0}})=\Lambda_{*}^{N_{0}}\,\Theta(R_{k})\geq\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4N}+N_{0}}=\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{3}{4N}}.

Then, by (
8.7
) we have

\displaystyle\Theta(R_{k+N_{0}+1})

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R_{k+N_{0}})=C\delta_{0}\Lambda_{*}^{N_{0}}\,\Theta(R_{k})

\displaystyle=C\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{2N}}\,\Theta(R_{k})\leq C\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{-1}{2N}}\,\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{a}\leq\delta_{0}\,\theta\,\Lambda_{*}^{a-\frac{1}{3N}}.

∎

To complete the proof of the lemma observe that, by (
8.8
) and a repeated application of the preceding claim, we infer that
there exists some
k\in[k_{1}+2,k_{1}+CN_{0}\,N]
such that
\Theta(R_{k})\not\in I_{\theta}
, since after
CN
iterations the second alternative in the lemma is not possible. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We proceed with the proof of (
8.5
) from Lemma
8.3
, that is, the estimate
\#{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\leq C_{5}
.

Proof of (
8.5
).

For
h\geq 1
set

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k):={\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\cap{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}.

Notice that each family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)
consists of a single cube, at most.
Indeed, we have

(8.9)

R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\quad\Rightarrow\quad P\subset B(e^{(3)}(R))

because
P\in e^{\prime}(Q)
for some
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
and
Q\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R))
by Lemma
4.5
. Thus, if
R,R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)
,
then
B(e^{(3)}(R))\cap B(e^{(3)}(R^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing
, which can only happen if
R=R^{\prime}
(by Lemma
8.1
(ii)).

Let
R_{0}
be a cube in
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
with maximal side length, and let
h_{0}
be such that
R_{0}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{0}}(P,k)
. We will show that
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}(P,k)\neq\varnothing
implies
h_{0}\leq h_{1}\leq h_{0}+C_{5}
. Together with the observation
\#{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)\leq 1
this will conclude the proof of (
8.5
).

Claim 8.6
.

Let
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\setminus\{R_{0}\}
, and let
h_{1}
be such that
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}(P,k)
.
Then
h_{1}\geq h_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
h_{1}<h_{0}
.
Let
R_{0}^{h_{1}}
be the cube that contains
R_{0}
and belongs to

{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h_{1}}
. Observe that

P\overset{\eqref{eq:ljh-inclusion}}{\subset}B(e^{(3)}(R_{0}))\cap B(e^{(3)}(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{0}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{0}))\cap B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1}))\\
\subset B(x_{R_{0}^{h_{1}}},\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(R^{h_{1}}_{0})+\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{0}))\cap B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1})).

So the two balls
B(x_{R_{0}^{h_{1}}},\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(R^{h_{1}}_{0})+\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{0}))
and

B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1}))
have non-empty intersection. Since
\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{0})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{1})
(the last inequality follows by the choice of
R_{0}
), we deduce that

B(e^{(4)}(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{0}^{h_{1}}},\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(R^{h_{1}}_{0})+5\ell(R_{0}))\subset B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}^{h_{1}})).

However, these inclusions contradict the property (i) of the family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}
in Lemma

8.1
because
R_{1}\neq R_{0}^{h_{1}}
.
∎

Claim 8.7
.

Let
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\setminus\{R_{0}\}
, and let
h_{1}
be such that
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}(P,k)
.
Then

(8.10)

h_{1}\leq h_{0}+C.

Proof.

Suppose that
h_{1}>h_{0}+1
. This implies that there are cubes

\{R_{1}^{h}\}_{h_{0}+1\leq h\leq h_{1}-1}
, with
R_{1}^{h}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
for each
h
, such that

R_{1}^{h_{0}+1}\supsetneq R_{1}^{h_{0}+2}\supsetneq\ldots\supsetneq R_{1}^{h_{1}-1}\supsetneq R_{1}^{h_{1}}=R_{1}.

Observe now that
\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})<\ell(R_{0})
. Otherwise, there exists some cube

R_{1}^{h_{0}}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h_{0}}
that contains
R_{1}^{h_{0}+1}
with

\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{0}).

Since
P\subset B(e^{(3)}(R_{0}))\cap B(e^{(3)}(R_{1}))
, arguing as in the previous claim, we deduce that

B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}))\subset B(e^{(4)}(R_{1}^{h_{0}}))
, which contradicts again the property (i) of the family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{0}}
in Lemma
8.1
, as above. So we have

\ell(R_{1}^{h})\leq\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})<\ell(R_{0})\quad\mbox{ for $h\geq h_{0}+1$.}

By the construction of
{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R_{0})
, there exists a sequence of cubes

S_{0}=R_{0},S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k}=Q
such that

S_{i+1}\in{\mathsf{GH}}(S_{i})\;\mbox{ for $i=0,\ldots,k-1$,}

and
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(S_{k}))
. In case that
P
is contained in some
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(Q))={\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{k}))
, we write
S_{k+1}=Q^{\prime}
, and we let
\tilde{k}:=k+1
. Otherwise, we let
\tilde{k}:=k
. All in all, we have

(8.11)

S_{i+1}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))\quad\text{for $i=0,\dots,\tilde{k}-1$},

and
S_{\tilde{k}+1}:=P\subset e^{\prime}(S_{\tilde{k}})
is not strictly contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{\tilde{k}}))
.

Obviously we have
\ell(S_{i+1})<\ell(S_{i})
for all
i
.
So, for each
h
with
h_{0}+1\leq h\leq h_{1}
there is some
i=i(h)
such that

(8.12)

\ell(S_{i})>\ell(R_{1}^{h})\geq\ell(S_{i+1}),

with
0\leq i\leq\tilde{k}
.
We claim that either
i\lesssim 1
or
i={\widetilde{k}}
, with the implicit constant depending on
n
.
Indeed, in the case
i<{\widetilde{k}}
, let
T\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
be such that
T\supset S_{i+1}
and
\ell(T)=\ell(R_{1}^{h})
.
Notice that, since
2R_{1}^{h}\cap 2T\neq\varnothing
(because both
2R_{1}^{h}
and
2T
contain
P
) and

\ell(R_{1}^{h})=\ell(T)
, we have

(8.13)

{\mathcal{P}}(T)\approx{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h})\approx\Theta(R_{1}^{h})=\Theta(R_{0}),

where in the last equality we used the definition of
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
.
On the other hand,

(8.14)

{\mathcal{P}}(T)\geq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})

because otherwise

T
is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(S_{i})
, which would imply that
S_{i+1}
does not belong to
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
.
Thus, from (
8.13
) and (
8.14
)
we derive that

\Theta(R_{0})\gtrsim\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})=\delta_{0}\,\Lambda_{*}^{i}\,\Theta(R_{0}).

Hence
\Lambda_{*}^{i}\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}
, which yields
i\lesssim_{n}1
if
i<{\widetilde{k}}
, as claimed.

The preceding discussion implies that, in order to prove (
8.10
), it suffices to show that, for each fixed

i=0,\ldots,\tilde{k}
, there are at most
C=C(n)
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
8.12
) with this fixed
i
.

Case
i<\tilde{k}
. Assume first that
i<\tilde{k}
. Recall that
N_{1}
is the constant given by Lemma
8.4
, and suppose that there exist more than
N_{1}
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
8.12
). Since
\{R_{1}^{h}\}
is a nested sequence of cubes, this is equivalent to saying that there exists some
s\in[h_{0}+1,\,h_{1}-N_{1}]
such that

(8.15)

for
h\in[s,s+N_{1}]
the cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfy (
8.12
).

Taking
\theta=\Theta(S_{i})
, Lemma
8.4
ensures
that there exists some cube
T\in{\mathsf{Top}}
such that
R_{1}^{s}\supset T\supset R_{1}^{s+N_{1}}
which satisfies either

(8.16)

\Theta(T)\leq\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4N}}\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})\qquad\mbox{ or }\qquad\Theta(T)\geq\Lambda_{*}^{\frac{1}{4N}}\Lambda\,\Theta(S_{i}).

Now, let
T^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
be such that
S_{i+1}\subset T^{\prime}\subset e^{\prime}(S_{i})
and
\ell(T^{\prime})=\ell(T)
, where we use the fact that
\ell(S_{i})>\ell(R_{1}^{s})\geq\ell(T)\geq\ell(R_{1}^{s+N_{1}})\geq\ell(S_{i+1})
and
S_{i+1}\subset e^{\prime}(S_{i})
. Notice that

(8.17)

{\mathcal{P}}(T^{\prime})\approx{\mathcal{P}}(T)\approx\Theta(T),

because
2T\cap 2T^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
.

If the first option in (
8.16
) holds, we deduce that

{\mathcal{P}}(T^{\prime})\leq C\Theta(T)\leq C\Lambda_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4N}}\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})<\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i}).

This implies that

T^{\prime}
is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(S_{i})\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
, which ensures that

S_{i+1}\not\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
(notice that we are using the fact that
i<\tilde{k}
), which is a contradiction with (
8.11
). Thus,
T
must satisfy the second estimate of (
8.16
). But in this case (
8.17
) yields
{\mathcal{P}}(T^{\prime})>\Lambda_{*}\Theta(S_{i})
, and so
T^{\prime}
is strictly contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
. Hence,
S_{i+1}\not\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
, which again gives a contradiction.
In consequence, if
i<\tilde{k}
, then (
8.15
) does not hold for any
s
.

Case
i=\tilde{k}
. Assume again that
(
8.15
) holds for some
s\in[h_{0}+1,\,h_{1}-N_{1}]
, and let
s
be the smallest possible such that (
8.15
) holds. The same argument as above shows that the cube
T^{\prime}
from the preceding paragraph is contained in some cube
T^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
. Since we assumed that
s
is minimal,
R_{1}^{s}\supset T\supset R_{1}^{s+N_{1}}
, and
\ell(T^{\prime\prime})\geq\ell(T^{\prime})=\ell(T)
, we get that there are at most
N_{1}
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying (
8.12
) such that

\ell(S_{i})>\ell(R_{1}^{h})\geq\ell(T^{\prime\prime})
.
We claim that there is also a bounded number of cubes
R_{1}^{h}
such that

(8.18)

\ell(T^{\prime\prime})\geq\ell(R_{1}^{h})\geq\ell(P).

Indeed, by the definition of the family
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and Lemma

3.3
, if we denote by
T_{m}
the
m
-th descendant of
T^{\prime\prime}
which contains
P
, for
m^{\prime}\geq m\geq 0
,
it follows that

{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m^{\prime}})\leq A_{0}^{-|m-m^{\prime}|/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m})\leq A_{0}^{-m/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(T^{\prime\prime}).

Suppose then that there are two cubes
R_{1}^{h}
,
R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}
such that
\ell(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})\leq\ell(R_{1}^{h})\leq\ell(T^{\prime\prime})
.
Let
T_{m}
and
T_{m^{\prime}}
be such that
\ell(R_{1}^{h})=\ell(T_{m})
and
\ell(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})=\ell(T_{m^{\prime}})
.
By arguments analogous to the ones in (
8.17
), we derive that

{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m})\approx{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h})\approx\Theta(R_{0})\quad\text{ and }\quad{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m^{\prime}})\approx{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})\approx\Theta(R_{0}),

where we also used the fact that
\Theta(R_{1}^{h})=\Theta(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})=\Theta(R_{0})
.
On the other hand, since
|m-m^{\prime}|\geq|h-h^{\prime}|
, we have

\Theta(R_{0})\approx{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m^{\prime}})\leq A_{0}^{-|h-h^{\prime}|/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(T_{m})\approx A_{0}^{-|h-h^{\prime}|/2}\,\Theta(R_{0}),

which implies that
|h-h^{\prime}|\lesssim 1
. From this fact it follows that there is a bounded number of cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying (
8.18
), as claimed. Putting all together, we get (
8.10
).
∎

This ends the proof of Lemma
8.3
.
∎

9.
The proof of Main Lemma
7.1

We have to show that

\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})\leq C\,\big(\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\big).

Recall that by Lemma
8.2
, we have

\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})\lesssim B^{5/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|.

By Lemma
6.16
we know that, for each cube
Q
in the last sum, one of the following alternatives holds:

•

{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
is not typical, so that

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)>\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))),

or

•

\mu(Z(Q))>{\varepsilon}_{Z}\,\mu(Q)
,  where
Z(Q)
is the set
Z
appearing in (
6.3
) (replacing
R
by
Q
there), which implies that

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\leq\theta_{0}^{2}\,\mu(e(Q))\lesssim{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\theta_{0}^{2}\,\mu(Z(Q)),

or

•

\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\geq\Lambda^{-2}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

So the following holds in any case:

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\lesssim\Lambda^{2}\,\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\Lambda^{\frac{1}{3n}}\!\!\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)+{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\theta_{0}^{2}\,\mu(Z(Q)).

Consequently,

(9.1)

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{5/4}\,\Lambda^{2}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+B^{5/4}\,\Lambda^{\frac{1}{3n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)

\displaystyle\quad+\,B^{5/4}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\theta_{0}^{2}\,\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\mu(Z(Q)).

Next lemma deals with each of the summands above.

Lemma 9.1
.

We have

(9.2)

T_{1}:=\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2},

(9.3)

T_{2}:=\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P),

and

(9.4)

T_{3}:=\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\mu(Z(Q))\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|\mu\|.

From (
9.1
) and the estimates above, it follows that

\sigma({\mathsf{Top}})\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)+{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|.

Recalling that
\varepsilon_{Z}=\varepsilon_{Z}(\Lambda)
, and
\Lambda=\Lambda(M)
, this concludes the proof of Main Lemma
7.1
.

In order to prove Lemma
9.1
, a basic tool to estimate the terms
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
is Lemma
8.3
, which asserts that
for all
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and all
k\geq 0
,

(9.5)

\#\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}\leq C_{4}\,\log\Lambda.

9.1.
Estimate of
T_{1}

Recall that

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\chi_{P}(x)\,\big(m_{\mu,P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,2Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{Z(Q)}(x)\big({\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)-m_{\mu,2R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big).

For
Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, we write
S\prec Q
if
S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
is a maximal cube that belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
. Then we denote

{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu=\sum_{S\prec Q}(m_{\mu,S}({\mathcal{R}}_{\mu})-m_{\mu,2Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)\,\chi_{S}

and

{\widetilde{E}}(Q)=\bigcup_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}P,\qquad\quad{\widetilde{G}}(Q)={\widetilde{E}}(Q)\cup Z(Q).

Notice that it may happen that
{\widetilde{G}}(Q)\neq e^{\prime}(Q)
because of the presence of negligible cubes.
Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!\chi_{P}\,\big(m_{\mu,P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,2Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)

\displaystyle=\!\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!\chi_{P}\,\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q)):P\subsetneq S}\!\!\Delta_{S}{\mathcal{R}}\mu+{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\Delta_{S}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q)):P\subset S}\chi_{P}

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{{\widetilde{E}}(Q)}{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu

\displaystyle=\chi_{{\widetilde{E}}(Q)}\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\!\Delta_{S}{\mathcal{R}}\mu+{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\bigg).

A similar argument shows that

\chi_{Z(Q)}(x)\big({\mathcal{R}}\mu(x)-m_{\mu,2Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)=\chi_{Z(Q)}\bigg(\sum_{S\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\!\Delta_{S}{\mathcal{R}}\mu+{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\bigg).

Hence,

\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu=\chi_{{\widetilde{G}}(Q)}\bigg(\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu+{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\bigg).

It is also immediate to check that, for a fixed
Q
and
P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, the functions

{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
and
\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu
are mutually orthogonal in
L^{2}(\mu)
. Then, since all the cubes
P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
satisfy

P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, we get

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\widetilde{{\mathsf{End}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\|\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\|\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sum_{P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}\|\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle=:T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}.

Regarding the term
T_{1,1}
, by Fubini we have

\displaystyle T_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}}\|\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\,\#A(P,k),

where

(9.6)

A(P,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\text{ such that }P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}.

From the definition (
6.1
) and Lemma
8.3
, it follows that

\displaystyle\#A(P,k)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing\\
A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}\!\!\#\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\text{ such that }P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing\\
A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\log\Lambda\lesssim\log\Lambda.

Hence,

T_{1,1}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}}\|\Delta_{P}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}=\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Concerning
T_{1,2}
, we argue analogously:

\displaystyle T_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\,\#{\widetilde{A}}(Q,k),

where

{\widetilde{A}}(Q,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\big\}.

Since

\#{\widetilde{A}}(Q,k)\leq\#A(Q,k)\lesssim\log\Lambda,

we deduce that

T_{1,2}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

As the next lemma shows, the right hand side above is also bounded by
C\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}
.
So we have

T_{1}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Lemma 9.2
.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\mu)
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

We defer the proof of this result to Section
9.4
.

9.2.
Estimate of
T_{2}

By the same argument we used to deal with
T_{1,1}
above, we get

T_{2}\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\,\#A(P,k),

where
A(P,k)
is given by (
9.6
).
Since
\#A(P,k)\lesssim\log\Lambda
, we obtain

T_{2}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P).

9.3.
Estimate of
T_{3}

We have

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\!\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\mu(Z(Q))

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\!\int_{Z(Q)}\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,d\mu

\displaystyle=\int\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\bigg(\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\!\chi_{Z(Q)}\bigg)\,d\mu.

By Fubini, we have

\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\!\chi_{Z(Q)}\leq\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\,\#D(x,k),

where

D(x,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\text{ such that }x\in Z(Q)\big\}.

Observe now that, given
j\geq 1
, if we let

\displaystyle D_{j}(x,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}:\exists\,Q\in

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\text{ such that ${\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))$ contains}

\displaystyle\text{
every $P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}$ such that $x\in P$ and $\ell(P)\leq A_{0}^{-j}$}\big\},

then we have

D(x,k)=\bigcup_{j\geq 1}D_{j}(x,k),

and moreover
D_{j}(x,k)\subset D_{j+1}(x,k)
for all
j
.
From Lemma
8.3
we deduce that

\#D_{j}(x,k)\leq C\,\log\Lambda\quad\mbox{ for all $j\geq 1$.}

Thus,
\#D(x,k)\leq C\,\log\Lambda
too. Consequently,

\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\!\chi_{Z(Q)}\lesssim_{\Lambda}1,

and so

T_{3}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|\mu\|.

Together with the estimate we obtained for
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, this concludes the proof of Lemma
9.1
, modulo the proof of Lemma
9.2
.

9.4.
Proof of Lemma
9.2

For
Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
, denote by
{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
the family of cubes
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
such that
R\cap 2Q\neq\varnothing

and
\ell(R)=A_{0}\,\ell(Q)
.
Also, let
{\mathcal{F}}(Q)
be the family of cubes
P
which are contained in some cube from
{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
and satisfy

\ell(Q)\leq\ell(P)\leq A_{0}\,\ell(Q)
. Notice that, by Lemma
3.1
, the cubes from
{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
belong to
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{db}
. So taking into account that
Q\subset 2B_{R}
for any
R\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
, that
R\subset CQ
, and that

Q
is
{\mathcal{P}}
doubling,

(9.7)

\mu(Q)\approx\mu(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $R\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)$.}

Denote

\widecheck{\Delta}_{Q}f=\sum_{R\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)}\big(m_{\mu,R}(f)-m_{\mu,2Q}(f)\big)\,\chi_{R}.

It is immediate to check that

\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{F}}(Q)}\|\Delta_{P}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}+\|\widecheck{\Delta}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}.

Remark that the main advantage of the operator
{\widecheck{\Delta}}_{Q}
over
{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}
is that the cubes

R\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
involved in the definition of
{\widecheck{\Delta}}_{Q}
are doubling, which may not be the case for the cubes
S\prec Q
in the definition of
{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}
.
From the last inequality, we get

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\|{\widehat{\Delta}}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{F}}(Q)}\|\Delta_{P}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\|\widecheck{\Delta}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

Since

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}}\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{F}}(Q)}\|\Delta_{P}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}}\|\Delta_{P}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{MDW}}:P\in{\mathcal{F}}(Q)}1

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}}\|\Delta_{P}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2},

the lemma follows from the next result.

Lemma 9.3
.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\mu)
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}}\|{\widecheck{\Delta}}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Proof.

For
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
such that
P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
, let

{\varphi}_{Q,P}=\left(\frac{1}{\mu(P)}\,\chi_{P}-\frac{1}{\mu(2Q)}\,\chi_{2Q}\right)\,\mu(P)^{1/2}.

Observe that

\|{\widecheck{\Delta}}_{Q}f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}=\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)}\big|m_{\mu,P}(f)-m_{\mu,2Q}(f)\big|^{2}\,\mu(P)=\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)}\langle f,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\rangle^{2}.

So we have to show that

\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}}\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)}\langle f,\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\rangle^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

To shorten notation, we denote by
{\mathcal{I}}
the set of all pairs
(Q,P)
with
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)
, so that
the double sum above can be written as
\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Arguing by duality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\langle f,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\rangle^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sup\bigg|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\langle f,\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\rangle\,b_{Q,P}\bigg|=\sup\bigg|\bigg\langle f,\,\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\rangle\,\bigg|,

where the supremum is taken over all the sequences
b:=\{b_{Q,P}\}_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}

such that
\|b\|_{\ell^{2}}\leq 1
.
Since

\bigg(\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\langle f,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\rangle^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq\big\|f\big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\,\sup\bigg\|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)},

to prove the lemma it suffices to show that

\bigg\|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\lesssim 1\quad\mbox{ for all
$b=\{b_{Q,P}\}_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}$
such that $\|b\|_{\ell^{2}}\leq 1$.}

To this end, we write

(9.8)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(Q,P),(R,S)}\big\langle b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P},\,b_{R,S}\,{\varphi}_{R,S}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P),(R,S)\\
\ell(Q)\leq\ell(R)\end{subarray}}|b_{Q,P}\,b_{R,S}|\,\,\big|\big\langle{\varphi}_{Q,P},\,{\varphi}_{R,S}\big\rangle\big|.

Denote

a(Q)=\bigcup_{P\in{\mathcal{A}}(Q)}P.

Observe that, for some
C
depending just on
A_{0}
,

\operatorname{supp}{\varphi}_{Q,P}\subset\overline{a(Q)}\subset CQ,\qquad\operatorname{supp}{\varphi}_{R,S}\subset\overline{a(R)}\subset CR,

and

\big\|{\varphi}_{Q,P}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mu(Q)^{1/2}},\qquad\big\|{\varphi}_{R,S}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mu(R)^{1/2}},

taking into account (
9.7
).
Thus, for
(Q,P)
,
(R,S)\in\mathcal{I}
with
\ell(Q)\leq\ell(R)
, we have

\big|\big\langle{\varphi}_{Q,P},\,{\varphi}_{R,S}\big\rangle\big|=\left|\int_{a(Q)}{\varphi}_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{R,S}\,d\mu\right|\lesssim\frac{\mu(a(Q))}{\mu(Q)^{1/2}\,\mu(R)^{1/2}}\approx\bigg(\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Further, using that
{\varphi}_{Q,P}
has zero mean and that
{\varphi}_{R,S}
is constant in
2R\cap S
, in
2R\setminus S
,
and in
S\setminus 2R
,
it follows that
\big\langle{\varphi}_{Q,P},\,{\varphi}_{R,S}\big\rangle=0
in the following cases:

(i)

if
a(Q)\cap(2R\cup S)=\varnothing
,

(ii)

if
a(Q)\subset 2R\cap S
,

(iii)

if
a(Q)\subset 2R\setminus S
,

(iv)

if
a(Q)\subset S\setminus 2R
.

For
d>0
, denote

\mathcal{N}_{d}(S)=\{x\in\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus S:{\rm dist}(x,S)\leq d\}\cup\{x\in S:{\rm dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus S)\leq d\}

and, analogously,

\mathcal{N}_{d}(2R)=\{x\in\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus 2R:{\rm dist}(x,2R)\leq d\}\cup\{x\in 2R:{\rm dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus 2R)\leq d\}.

Observe that if none of the conditions (i), (ii), (iii), (iv), holds, then

(9.9)

a(Q)\subset\mathcal{N}_{{\rm diam}(a(Q))}(S)\cup\mathcal{N}_{{\rm diam}(a(Q))}(2R).

From the thin boundary condition (
2.4
) and the fact that
2R
is a finite number of
cubes of the same generation as
R
, using also that
R
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling and
S
is doubling, we deduce that

(9.10)

\mu\big(\mathcal{N}_{d}(S)\big)+\mu\big(\mathcal{N}_{d}(2R)\big)\lesssim\left(\frac{d}{\ell(R)}\right)^{1/2}\,\mu(R)\quad\mbox{ for all $d\in(0,C\ell(R)$),}

with the implicit constant depending on
C
.
Consequently, denoting by “
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)
" the situation when
\ell(Q)\leq\ell(R)
and (
9.9
) holds, by (
9.8
) we get

\displaystyle\bigg\|

\displaystyle\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\leq 2\sum_{(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)}|b_{Q,P}\,b_{R,S}|\,\,\bigg(\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\bigg(\sum_{(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}}|b_{R,S}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\,\Bigg(\sum_{(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!|b_{Q,P}|\,\bigg(\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}\bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Bigg(\sum_{(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!|b_{Q,P}|^{2}\,\bigg(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\bigg)^{1/4}\Bigg)\,\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(Q)}\bigg)^{1/4}\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}\Bigg)\Bigg)^{1/2}.

We consider now the last sum on the right hand side, which equals

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(Q)}\bigg)^{1/4}\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}=\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\\
\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)\end{subarray}}\!\!A_{0}^{k/4}\,\,\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}

Notice that, by (
9.9
) and (
9.10
), we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\\
\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)\end{subarray}}\mu(Q)\lesssim\mu\big(\mathcal{N}_{CA_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)}(S)\big)+\mu\big(\mathcal{N}_{CA_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)}(2R)\big)\lesssim A_{0}^{-k/2}\,\mu(R).

Therefore,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(Q)}\bigg)^{1/4}\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu(R)}\lesssim\sum_{k\geq 0}A_{0}^{k/4}\,\,\frac{A_{0}^{-k/2}\,\mu(R)}{\mu(R)}\lesssim 1.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg(\sum_{(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!|b_{Q,P}|^{2}\,\bigg(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\bigg)^{1/4}\Bigg)\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}|b_{Q,P}|^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\!\!\bigg(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\bigg)^{1/4}\Bigg)^{1/2}.

Since

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(R,S)\in{\mathcal{I}}:\\
(Q,P)\dashv(R,S)\end{subarray}}\!\!\bigg(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\bigg)^{1/4}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:\\
\ell(R)\geq\ell(Q)\\
a(Q)\cap a(R)\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\!\!\bigg(\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\bigg)^{1/4}\lesssim 1,

we infer that

\bigg\|\sum_{(Q,P)\in{\mathcal{I}}}b_{Q,P}\,{\varphi}_{Q,P}\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\lesssim 1,

as wished.
∎

10.
The proof of the First Main Proposition
3.6

Recall that, for a cube
R\in{\mathsf{Top}}
,
{\rm Tree}(R)
denotes the subfamily of the cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(R)
which are not strictly contained in any cube
from
{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R)
.
In this section we will prove the following result.

Lemma 10.1
.

For each
R\in{\mathsf{Top}}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)}\!\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\Theta(Q)\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\!\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)}\!\|\Delta_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\;+\!\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)+\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(R).

Observe that summing the above inequality over
R\in{\mathsf{Top}}
we get

\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}}\!\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\Theta(Q)\,\mu(Q)\leq\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)}\!\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\Theta(Q)\,\mu(Q)\\
\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)}\|\Delta_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R)\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)+\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(R)\\
\lesssim\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)+\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{Top}}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\mu(R).

Together with Main Lemma
7.1
, and recalling that
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\Lambda_{*})
and
\Lambda_{*}=\Lambda_{*}(M)
, this yields Main
Proposition
3.6
.

10.1.
The approximating measure
\eta
on a subtree
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)

To prove Lemma
10.1
for a given cube
R_{0}\in{\mathsf{Top}}
(in place of
R
), we will consider a corona
decomposition of
{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
into subtrees by introducing appropriate new stopping conditions.
In this section we will deal with the construction of each subtree and an associated AD-regular measure
which approximates
\mu
in that subtree.
To this end we need some additional notation.

First, for a cube

R\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
, we write
Q\in{\mathsf{BR}}(R)
(which stands for “big Riesz transform”) if
Q
is a
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling maximal cube which does not belong to
{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
and satisfies

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}(x_{Q})|\geq K\,\Theta(R),

where
K
is some big constant to be fixed below, depending on
\Lambda_{*}
,
\delta_{0}
, and
M
. Also, for a cube
Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
,
we denote by
{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)
the family of maximal cubes
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)\setminus\{Q\}
that satisfy one of the following conditions:

•

P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
, i.e.
P
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling, or

•

P\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
.

From Lemma
3.3
, it is immediate to check that if
Q
is not contained in any cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, then the cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)

cover
Q
, and also

(10.1)

\ell(P)\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\ell(Q)\quad\mbox{ for each $P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(P)$,}

Given
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
, we will construct a tree
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
inductively, consisting just of
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes and stopping cubes from
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
. At the
same time we will construct an approximating AD-regular measure for this tree. We will do this by “spreading”
the measure of the cubes from
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
among the other cubes from
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
. To this end, we will consider some coefficients
s(Q)
,
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\cap{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, that, in a sense, quantify the additional measure
\mu
spreaded on
Q

due to the presence of close cubes from
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
. The algorithm is the following.

First we choose
R
as the root of
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, and we set
s(R)=0
. Next, suppose that
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)

(in particular, this implies that
Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
), and assume that we have not
decided yet if the cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)
belong to
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
.
First we decide that
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
if one of the following conditions hold:

(i)

Q\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{BR}}(R)
, or

(ii)

s(Q)\geq\mu(Q)
and (i) does not hold, or

(iii)

\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(P)\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\mu(Q)
and neither (i) nor (ii) hold.

If
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
, no descendants of
Q
are allowed to belong to
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
.
Otherwise, all the cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)
are chosen to belong to
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, and for each
P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)
, we define

s(P)=-\mu(P)\quad\mbox{ if $P\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})$,}

and, otherwise, we set

(10.2)

t(Q)=\sum_{S\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(S)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad s(P)=\big(s(Q)+t(Q)\big)\,\frac{\mu(P)}{\mu(Q)-t(Q)}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)}s(P)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(P)+\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(P)

\displaystyle=-t(Q)+\big(s(Q)+t(Q)\big)=s(Q).

By induction, the coefficients
s(\cdot)
satisfy the following.
If
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
and
{\mathcal{I}}
is some finite family of cubes from
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(Q)

which cover
Q
and are disjoint, then

(10.3)

\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{I}}}s(P)=s(Q).

Further,
s(Q)\geq 0
for all
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
.

Now we are ready to define an approximating measure
\eta
associated with
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
.
First, we denote

{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)=R\,\setminus\bigcup_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}Q,

and for each
Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
we let
D_{Q}
be an
n
-dimensional disk passing through
x_{Q}

with radius
\frac{1}{2}\,r(Q)
(recall that
r(Q)
is the radius of
B(Q)
).
In case that
\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))=0
, we define

\eta=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\big(s(Q)+\mu(Q)\big)\,\frac{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}{\lfloor}_{D_{Q}}}{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(D_{Q})}.

Observe that
\eta(D_{Q})=\mu(Q)+s(Q)
for all
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
and, in particular
\eta(D_{Q})=0

if
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
.

In case that
\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))\neq 0
, we have to be a little more careful. For a given

N\geq 1
we let
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{N}(R)
be the family consisting of all the cubes from

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
with side length larger that
A_{0}^{-N}\,\ell(R)
, and we let
{\mathcal{I}}_{N}
be the family of the cubes from
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
which have side length smaller than
A_{0}^{-N}\,\ell(R)
and are maximal.
We denote

(10.4)

\eta_{N}=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{N}(R)}\big(s(Q)+\mu(Q)\big)\,\frac{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}{\lfloor}_{D_{Q}}}{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(D_{Q})}+\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{I}}_{N}(R)}\big(s(Q)+\mu(Q)\big)\,\frac{\mu{\lfloor}_{Q}}{\mu(Q)},

and we let
\eta
be a weak limit of
\eta_{N}
as
N\to\infty
.

As in Section
5.2
, we use the following notation.
To each
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
we associate another “cube”
Q^{(\eta)}
defined as follows:

Q^{(\eta)}=({\mathsf{G}}(R)\cap Q)\cup\bigcup_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R):P\subset Q}D_{P}.

We let

{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}^{(\eta)}(R)\equiv{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}^{(\eta)}(R^{(\eta)}):=\{Q^{(\eta)}:Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\}.

For
S=Q^{(\eta)}\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}^{(\eta)}(R)
with
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, we denote
Q=S^{(\mu)}
and we write

\ell(S):=\ell(Q)
.

Observe now that, from (
10.3
) and the definition of
\eta
, we have the key property

(10.5)

\eta(Q^{(\eta)})=\mu(Q)+s(Q)\quad\mbox{ for all $Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)$}.

So
s(Q)
is the measure added to
\mu(Q)
to obtain
\eta(Q^{(\eta)})
.

Lemma 10.2
.

The measure
\Theta(R_{0})^{-1}\eta
is AD-regular (with a constant depending on
\Lambda_{*}
and
\delta_{0}
), and

\eta(Q^{(\eta)})=0
for all
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cap{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
.

Proof.

The fact that
\eta(Q^{(\eta)})=0
for all
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cap{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
follows by construction and has already been mentioned above.
To prove the AD-regularity of
\eta
, by standard arguments, it is enough to show that

\eta(Q^{(\eta)})\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})\,\ell(Q)^{n}\quad\mbox{ for all $Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})$,}

taking into account (
10.1
).
Given such a cube
Q
, the fact that
Q\not\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
ensures that

\eta(Q^{(\eta)})\geq\mu(Q)\gtrsim\delta_{0}\,\Theta(R_{0})\,\ell(Q)^{n}.

To show the converse estimate we can assume
Q\neq R
. By
the condition (ii) in the definition of
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}({\widehat{Q}})
, where
{\widehat{Q}}
is the first ancestor of
Q
in
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)

(i.e.,
{\widehat{Q}}
is the smallest cube from
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
that strictly contains
Q
), we have

s({\widehat{Q}})\leq\mu({\widehat{Q}}).

Also, by (iii) (which does not hold for
{\widehat{Q}}
), the coefficient
t({\widehat{Q}})
in (
10.2
) satisfies

t({\widehat{Q}})=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}({\widehat{Q}})\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(P)<\frac{1}{2}\,\mu({\widehat{Q}}).

Therefore,

(10.6)

s(Q)=\big(s({\widehat{Q}})+t({\widehat{Q}})\big)\,\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu({\widehat{Q}})-t({\widehat{Q}})}\leq 2\big(\mu({\widehat{Q}})+\frac{1}{2}\,\mu({\widehat{Q}})\big)\,\frac{\mu(Q)}{\mu({\widehat{Q}})}=3\,\mu(Q),

and so

\eta(Q^{(\eta)})=s(Q)+\mu(Q)\leq 4\,\mu(Q)\leq 4\,\mu({\widehat{Q}})\lesssim\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{0})\,\ell({\widehat{Q}})^{n}\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})\,\ell(Q)^{n},

taking into account that
{\widehat{Q}}\not\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})
, by (i).
∎

Remark 10.3
.

For the record, notice that from (
10.6
) it follows that, for all
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, either

(10.7)

0\leq s(Q)\leq 3\,\mu(Q),

or

s(Q)=-\mu(Q).

The latter case happens if and only if
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
.

Remark 10.4
.

Consider the measure defined by

\eta^{\prime}=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q)\,\frac{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}{\lfloor}_{D_{Q}}}{{\mathcal{H}}^{n}(D_{Q})}+\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}.

This measure is mutually absolutely continuous with
\eta
. Further, since the
coefficients
s(Q)
, with
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, are uniformly bounded (by the previous remark), it turns out that

\eta^{\prime}=\rho\,\eta,

for some function
\rho\in L^{\infty}(\eta)
satisfying
\rho\approx 1
.
Consequently, by Lemma
10.2
,
\eta^{\prime}
is also AD-regular.

For a family of cubes
{\mathcal{I}}\subset{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
, we denote

{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}({\mathcal{I}})=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q).

For
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
, we write
Q\in(i)_{R}
, if
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
and the condition (i) in the definition of

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
holds for
Q
, and analogously regarding the notations
Q\in(ii)_{R}
and
Q\in(iii)_{R}
.

Lemma 10.5
.

The following holds:

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{BR}}(R))}\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R})\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q)+\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))\approx\mu(R).

Proof.

It is clear that the left hand side above is bounded by
\mu(R)
.
For the converse estimate, we write

(10.8)

\mu(R)=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\mu(Q)+\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))=\sum_{Q\in(i)_{R}}\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in(ii)_{R}}\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in(iii)_{R}}\mu(Q)+\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)).

By construction,

(10.9)

\sum_{Q\in(i)_{R}}\mu(Q)=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{BR}}(R))}\mu(Q).

Also, if
Q\in(iii)_{R}
, then

\mu(Q)\leq 2\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(Q)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(P),

and thus

(10.10)

\sum_{Q\in(iii)_{R}}\mu(Q)\leq 2\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}({\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R))\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(P).

On the other hand, if
Q\in(ii)_{R}
, then
0\leq\mu(Q)\leq s(Q)
, and so

\sum_{Q\in(ii)_{R}}\mu(Q)\leq\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(Q):s(Q)\geq 0}s(Q)=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(Q).

For a given
N\geq 1
, consider the families
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{N}(R)
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{N}
defined just above (
10.4
).
Notice that
{\mathcal{J}}_{N}:={\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{N}(R)\cup{\mathcal{I}}_{N}
is a finite family of cubes which cover
R
, and thus,
from the property (
10.3
),
it follows that

0=s(R)=\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}}s(Q)=\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(Q).

Since
s(Q)=-\mu(Q)
for all
Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, we deduce

\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(Q)=\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q).

Letting
N\to\infty
and taking into account that
s(Q)\geq 0
for all

Q\in{\mathcal{J}}_{N}\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, we get

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(Q)\leq\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q),

and thus

(10.11)

\sum_{Q\in(ii)_{R}}\mu(Q)\leq\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q).

The lemma follows from the splitting (
10.8
) and the inequalities (
10.9
), (
10.10
), (
10.11
).
∎

Lemma 10.6
.

The operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\eta)
, with

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}(\eta)\to L^{2}(\eta)}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R).

Proof.

To prove this lemma we will use the suppressed kernel
K_{\Phi}
introduced in Section
5.1
,
with the following
1
-Lipchitz function:

\Phi(x)=\inf_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)}\big(\ell(Q)+{\rm dist}(x,Q)\big).

We will prove first that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mu{\lfloor}_{2R}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})
by applying Theorem

5.1
, and later on we will
show that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\eta)
by approximation.

In order to apply Theorem
5.1
, we will show that

(a)

\mu(B(x,r)\cap 2R)\leq C\,\Theta(R)\,r^{n}
for all
r\geq\Phi(x)
, and

(b)

\sup_{r>\Phi(x)}\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{r}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)\big|\leq C\Theta(R)
,

with
C
possibly depending on
\Lambda_{*}
,
\delta_{0}
, and
K
. Once these conditions are proven, then Theorem
5.1
applied to the measure
\Theta(R)^{-1}\,\mu{\lfloor}_{2R}
ensures that

(10.12)

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mu{\lfloor}_{2R}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})\to L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R).

The proof of (a) is quite similar to the proof of Lemma
5.8
. However, we repeat
here the arguments for the reader’s convenience.
In the case
r>\ell(R)/10
we just use that

\mu(B(x,r)\cap 2R)\leq\mu(2R)\lesssim\Theta(R)\,\ell(R)^{n}\lesssim\Theta(R)\,r^{n}.

So we may assume that
\Phi(x)<r\leq\ell(R)/10
.
By the definition of
\Phi(x)
, there exists
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
such that

\ell(Q)+{\rm dist}(x,Q)\leq r.

Therefore,
B_{Q}\subset B(x,4r)
and so there exists an ancestor
Q^{\prime}\supset Q
which belongs to
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
such that
B(x,r)\subset 2B_{Q^{\prime}}
, with
\ell(Q^{\prime})\approx r
.
Then,

\mu(B(x,r)\cap 2R)\leq\mu(2B_{Q^{\prime}})\lesssim\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{0})\,\ell(Q^{\prime})^{n}\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)\,r^{n},

as wished.

Let us turn our attention to the property (b).
In the case
r>\ell(R)/10
we have

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{r}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)\big|\leq\frac{\mu(2R)}{r^{n}}\lesssim\Theta(R).

In the case
\Phi(x)<r\leq\ell(R)/10
we consider the same cube
Q^{\prime}\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
as above,
which satisfies
B(x,r)\subset 2B_{Q^{\prime}}
and
\ell(Q^{\prime})\approx r
. Further, by replacing

Q^{\prime}
by the first ancestor in
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
if necessary, we may assume that

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q^{\prime}}(x_{Q^{\prime}})|\lesssim K\,\Theta(R).

Since
|x-x_{Q^{\prime}}|\lesssim\ell(Q^{\prime})
, by standard arguments which use the fact that
K_{\Phi}
is a Calderón-Zygmund kernel (see (
5.2
)
and (
5.3
)),
it follows that

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q^{\prime}}(x_{Q^{\prime}})-{\mathcal{R}}_{r}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q^{\prime})\lesssim\Lambda_{*}\,\Theta(R_{0})\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R).

Thus,

\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{r}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)\big|\leq\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q^{\prime}}(x_{Q^{\prime}})\big|+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q^{\prime}}(x_{Q^{\prime}})-{\mathcal{R}}_{r}(\chi_{2R}\mu)(x)\big|\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R).

So both (a) and (b) hold, and then (
10.12
) follows.

Next we deal with the
L^{2}(\eta)
boundedness of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta}
.
First notice that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)})
with norm
at most
C\Theta(R)
because
\Phi(x)=0
on
{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)
. Since
\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}=\rho\,\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}
for some function
\rho\approx 1
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}}
is also bounded in
L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)})
with norm bounded by
C\Theta(R)
.
So it suffices to show that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})
.
This follows from the fact that if
\alpha
and
\beta
are Radon measures with polynomial growth of degree
n
such that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\alpha)
and

{\mathcal{R}}_{\beta}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\beta)
, then
{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha+\beta}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\alpha+\beta)
,
and then choosing
\alpha=\Theta(R)^{-1}\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}
and
\beta=\Theta(R)^{-1}\mu{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}}
.
See for example Proposition 3.1 from
[
NToV2
]
.

It remains to show that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})

with norm bounded above by
C\,\Theta(R)
.
Notice first that, by (
2.4
), there exists some constant
b>0
depending at most on

C_{0},A_{0},n
such that

\mu\bigl(\{x\in Q:{\rm dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus Q)\leq b\,\ell(Q)\}\bigr)\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\mu(Q).

We denote

(10.13)

Q^{(0)}=\{x\in Q:{\rm dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\mu\setminus Q)>b\,\ell(Q)\},

so that
\mu(Q^{(0)})\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\mu(Q)
.

We have to show that

(10.14)

\|{\mathcal{R}}(g\,\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R)\,\|g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}

for any given
g\in L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})
, with
{\mathcal{R}}(g\,\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})

understood in the principal value sense. To this end, we take
the function
f\in L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})
defined as follows:

(10.15)

f=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\int_{D_{Q}}\!g\,d\eta\,\,\frac{\chi_{Q^{(0)}}}{\mu(Q^{(0)})}.

We also consider the signed measures

\alpha=f\,\mu,\qquad\beta=g\,\eta,

so that
\alpha(Q)=\alpha(Q^{(0)})=\beta(D_{Q})
for all
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
.

As a preliminary step to obtain (
10.14
), we will show first

(10.16)

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R)\,\|g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}.

For that purpose, first we will estimate the term
|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(y)|
,
with
x\in Q^{(0)}
,
y\in D_{Q}
, for
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
, in terms of the coefficients

{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q):=\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\supset Q}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(P)^{n+1}}\,|\alpha|(2B_{P})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q):=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|\alpha|(P),

and

{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q):=\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\supset Q}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(P)^{n+1}}\,|\beta|(2B_{P})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q):=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,|\beta|(D_{P}).

We claim that

(10.17)

|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(y)|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q)+{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q)+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q)

for all
x\in Q^{(0)}
,
y\in D_{Q}
, with
Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
.
The arguments to prove this are quite similar to the ones in Lemma
6.1
, but we
will show the details for completeness.
By the triangle inequality, for
x
,
y
and
Q
as above, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(y)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x_{Q})\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x_{Q})-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(x_{Q})\big|+\big|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(x_{Q})-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(y)\big|

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

First we estimate
I_{1}
using the properties of the kernel
K_{\Phi}
in (
5.2
)
and (
5.3
), and taking into account that for
x\in Q^{(0)}
(and thus for
x_{Q}
)

\Phi(x)\approx_{b}\ell(Q)
, because of the separation condition in the definition of

Q^{(0)}
in (
10.13
). Then we get

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\int|K_{\Phi}(x,z)-K_{\Phi}(x_{Q},z)|\,d|\alpha|(z)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{2B_{Q}}+\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\supset Q}\int_{2B_{{\widehat{P}}}\setminus 2B_{P}}\bigg)|K_{\Phi}(x,z)-K_{\Phi}(x_{Q},z)|\,d|\alpha|(z)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\supset Q}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(P)^{n+1}}\,|\alpha|(2B_{{\widehat{P}}})\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q),

where above we denoted by
{\widehat{P}}
the parent of
P
.
