The same estimate holds for the term
I_{3}
(with
\alpha
replaced by
\beta
), using that
\Phi(x)\approx\ell(Q)
for all
x\in D_{Q}
,
since
D_{Q}\subset\frac{1}{2}B(Q)
and
B(Q)\cap\operatorname{supp}\mu\subset Q
. So

|I_{3}|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q).

Finally we deal with the term
I_{2}
.
Since
\alpha(P^{(0)})=\beta(D_{P})
for all
P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\left|\int K_{\Phi}(x_{Q}-z)\,d\big(\alpha{\lfloor}_{P^{(0)}}-\beta{\lfloor}_{D_{P}}\big)(z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\int|K_{\Phi}(x_{Q}-z)-K_{\Phi}(x_{Q}-x_{P})|\,d\big(|\alpha|{\lfloor}_{P^{(0)}}+|\beta|{\lfloor}_{D_{P}}\big)(z)

From the separation condition in (
10.13
) and the fact that
D_{P}\subset\frac{1}{2}B(P)
, we infer that, for
P,Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
with
P\neq Q
and
z\in P^{(0)}\cup D_{P}
,

|x_{Q}-z|\approx|x_{Q}-x_{P}|\gtrsim\ell(Q)+\ell(P).

Hence, in the case
P\neq Q
,

\int|K_{\Phi}(x_{Q}-z)-K_{\Phi}(x_{Q}-x_{P})|\,d\big(|\alpha|{\lfloor}_{P^{(0)}}+|\beta|{\lfloor}_{D_{P}}\big)(z)\lesssim\frac{\ell(P)}{D(P,Q)^{n+1}}\,\big(|\alpha|(P^{(0)})+|\beta|(D_{P})\big).

The same inequality holds in the case
P=Q
using (
5.2
) and the fact that
\Phi(x_{Q})\approx\ell(Q)
.
So we deduce that

I_{2}\lesssim{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q)+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q).

Gathering the estimates obtained for
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
,
I_{3}
, the claim (
10.17
) follows.

Now we are ready to show (
10.16
). By the claim just proven and using that

\eta(B_{Q})\lesssim\mu(Q)
, we obtain

(10.18)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\int_{B_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta|^{2}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\inf_{x\in Q^{(0)}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)|^{2}\,\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\big({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)^{2}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q)\big)^{2}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\big({\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q)^{2}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q)\big)^{2}\,\eta(D_{Q}).

Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi,\mu{\lfloor}_{2R}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})
with norm bounded by

C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K)\,\Theta(R)
, we infer that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\inf_{x\in Q^{(0)}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha(x)|^{2}\,\mu(Q)\leq\int_{R}|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\alpha|^{2}d\mu\leq\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}(f\mu)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu{\lfloor}_{2R})}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

To estimate the second sum on the right hand side of (
10.18
) we use the fact
that

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q).

This follows by the same argument as in Lemma
6.13
, with
p=2
. Indeed, in that lemma
one does not use any specific property of the measure
\mu
or the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}
, apart from the
fact the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}
are pairwise disjoint. So the lemma applies to
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
too.
Observe also that, for every
x\in Q
,
2B_{P}\subset B(x,2\ell(P))\subset CB_{P}
, for some
C>1
, and thus

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:P\supset Q}\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(P)^{n+1}}\,\frac{|\alpha|(B(x,2\ell(P)))}{\mu(B(x,2\ell(P)))}\,\mu(CB_{P})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}f(x)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}f(x).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\big({\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)^{2}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\alpha}(Q)\big)^{2}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)^{2}\int|{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}f|^{2}\,d\mu\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

The last sum on the right hand side of (
10.18
) is estimated similarly. Indeed,
by Lemma
6.13
we also have

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q)^{2}\,\eta(D_{Q})\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q)^{2}\,\eta(D_{Q}),

and as above,

{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}g(x).

Then it follows that

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\big({\mathcal{P}}_{\beta}(Q)^{2}+{\mathcal{Q}}_{\beta}(Q)\big)^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\|g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}^{2}.

By (
10.18
) and the preceding esitmates, to complete the proof of (
10.16
) it just remains
to notice that, by the definition of
f
in (
10.15
) and Cauchy-Schwarz,
\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}
.

In order to prove (
10.14
), we denote

{\mathcal{R}}^{r(Q)/4}\beta(x)={\mathcal{R}}\beta(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{r(Q)/4}\beta(x),

and we split

(10.19)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}(g\,\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\int_{D_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}\beta|^{2}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\!\bigg(\int_{D_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}^{r(Q)/4}\beta|^{2}\,d\eta+\int_{D_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}_{r(Q)/4}\beta-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta|^{2}\,d\eta\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}^{2}.

Using the fact that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\eta{\lfloor}_{D_{Q}}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{D_{Q}})
with norm
comparable to
\eta(B_{Q})/r(Q^{n})
(because
D_{Q}

is an
n
-dimensional disk), we deduce that

\int_{D_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}^{r(Q)/4}\beta|^{2}\,d\eta=\int_{D_{Q}}|{\mathcal{R}}^{r(Q)/4}(\chi_{D_{Q}}g\,\eta)|^{2}\,d\eta\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\|\chi_{D_{Q}}g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}^{2}.

Regarding the second integral in (
10.19
),
observe that, by (
5.4
), for all
x\in D_{Q}
with

Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
,

\bigl|{\mathcal{R}}_{r(Q)/4}\beta(x)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta(x)\bigr|\lesssim\sup_{r>\Phi(x)}\frac{|\beta|(B(x,r))}{r^{n}}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)\,{\mathcal{M}}_{\mu}g(x).

Then, by the last estimates and (
10.16
), we get

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}(g\,\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R)^{2}\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)}\int_{D_{Q}}\big(|g|^{2}+|{\mathcal{M}}_{\eta}g|^{2}\big)\,d\eta+\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}\beta\|_{L^{2}(\eta{\lfloor}_{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)^{c}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R)^{2}\,\|g\|_{L^{2}(\eta)}^{2},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next, for each
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
, we define the family
{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
and
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
, which can be considered as an enlarged version of
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)
.
First we define

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}_{*}}}(R)=(i)_{R}\cup(ii)_{R}\cup{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R}).

Let
{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
be the family of maximal
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cubes which are contained in the cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)
.
Notice that
R\not\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
. Further, the cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{BR}}(R)\cup(ii)_{R})

belong to
{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
because they are
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling.
Then we let
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
be the family of cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
which are contained in
R
and are not strictly contained in any cube from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
. Observe that we do not ask the cubes from

{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
to be
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling. Similarly, we define
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
as the family of cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
which are contained in
R
and are not strictly contained in any cube from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)
.

10.2.
Estimating the
\beta
numbers on
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)

Our goal is now to prove the following estimate.

Lemma 10.7
.

For each
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R_{0})\,\mu(R).

We split the proof into several steps. Fix
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
. First we deal with cubes in
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
.

Lemma 10.8
.

We have

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0})\mu(R).

Proof.

We use the trivial bound
\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\lesssim\theta_{\mu}(2B_{Q})
and Lemma
3.3
to get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}

\displaystyle\mu(Q)\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{Q})\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R):\,Q\subset P}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{Q})\mu(Q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\\
Q\subset P,\,\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-m}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}\theta_{\mu}(2B_{Q})\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\\
Q\subset P,\,\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-m}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}A_{0}^{-m/2}{\mathcal{P}}(P)\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}A_{0}^{-m/2}{\mathcal{P}}(P)\mu(P)\approx\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}{\mathcal{P}}(P)\mu(P).

Recall that for
Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
we have
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0})
, and so

\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}{\mathcal{P}}(P)\mu(P)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R)\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)}\mu(P)\leq\Theta(R_{0})\mu(R).

∎

It remains to prove

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R_{0})\,\mu(R).

Consider the set

\Gamma=\operatorname{supp}\eta={\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)\cup\bigcup_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}D_{Q}.

Denote
\nu={\mathcal{H}}^{n}|_{\Gamma}
. We showed in Lemma
10.2
that
\Theta(R_{0})^{-1}\eta
is an AD-regular measure, and so it follows by standard arguments (using e.g.
[
Ma
, Theorem 6.9]
) that
\Gamma
is an AD-regular set, and that
\eta=\rho\nu
for some density
\rho
satisfying
\rho\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})
. It is also immediate to check that Lemma
10.6
implies that
{\mathcal{R}}_{\nu}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\nu)
, with

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\nu}\|_{L^{2}(\nu)\to L^{2}(\nu)}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}1.

Hence, by the main result of
[
NToV1
]
we know that
\Gamma
is uniformly
n
-rectifiable. This allows us to use the
\beta
numbers characterization of uniform rectifiability
[
DS1
]
to get

(10.20)

\int_{B(z,r_{0})}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\beta_{\nu,2}(x,r)^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}d\nu(x)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}r_{0}^{n},\quad\text{for }z\in\operatorname{supp}\nu,r_{0}\in(0,{\rm diam}(\operatorname{supp}\nu)).

To transfer these estimates back to the measure
\mu
we will argue similarly as in Section 7 of
[
AT
]
. It will be convenient to work with regularized cubes, as we did in Section
5.2
. Consider a function

d_{R,*}(x)=\inf_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}({\rm dist}(x,Q)+\ell(Q)).

Note that
d_{R,*}(x)=0
for
x
in the closure of
{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R),
and
d_{R,*}(x)>0
everywhere else. Moreover,
d_{R,*}
is 1-Lipschitz. For each
x\in R\setminus\overline{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}
we define
Q_{x}
to be the maximal cube from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
that contains
x
and satisfies

\ell(Q_{x})\leq\frac{1}{60}\inf_{y\in Q_{x}}d_{R,*}(y).

The family of all the cubes
Q_{x},\ x\in R\setminus\overline{{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)},
will be denoted by
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
. We define also the regularized tree
{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
consisting of the cubes from
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
that are contained in
R
and are not strictly contained in any of the
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
cubes.

It follows easily from the definition of
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
that
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)\subset{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
.
Observe also that
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
consists of pairwise disjoint cubes and satisfies

\mu\bigg(R\setminus\bigg(\bigcup_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)}P\cup{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)\bigg)\bigg)=0.

The following is an analogue of Lemma
5.4
.

Lemma 10.9
.

The cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
satisfy the following properties:

(a)

If
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
and
x\in B(x_{P},50\ell(P))
, then
10\,\ell(P)\leq d_{R,*}(x)\leq c\,\ell(P)
,
where
c
is some constant depending only on
n
.

(b)

There exists some absolute constant
c>0
such that if
P,\,P^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
satisfy
B(x_{P},50\ell(P))\cap B(x_{P^{\prime}},50\ell(P^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing
, then

c^{-1}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq c\,\ell(P).

(c)

For each
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
, there are at most
N
cubes
P^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
such that

B(x_{P},50\ell(P))\cap B(x_{P^{\prime}},50\ell(P^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing,

where
N
is some absolute constant.

As before, we omit the proof.

Lemma 10.10
.

For all
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
there exists some
P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
such that
\ell(Q)\approx\ell(P)
and
2B_{Q}\subset CB_{P}\subset C^{\prime}B_{Q}
, where
C
and
C^{\prime}
are some absolute constants. In consequence,

(10.21)

{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0}).

Proof.

Let
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
. If
Q\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)\neq\varnothing,
then
Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
and we can take
P=Q
. If
Q\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)=\varnothing,
then there exists some
Q_{0}\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
such that
Q_{0}\subset Q
. By the definition of
d_{R,*}
and Lemma
10.9
(a), there exists
P_{0}\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
such that

{\rm dist}(x_{Q_{0}},P_{0})+\ell(P_{0})\leq 2d_{R,*}(x_{Q_{0}})\approx\ell(Q_{0}).

In particular,
\ell(P_{0})\lesssim\ell(Q_{0})\leq\ell(Q)
. If
\ell(P_{0})\geq\ell(Q),
set
P=P_{0}
, otherwise let
P
be the ancestor of
P_{0}
with
\ell(P)=\ell(Q)
. Clearly,
\ell(P)\approx\ell(Q)
, and moreover

{\rm dist}(x_{Q},x_{P})\leq{\rm dist}(x_{Q_{0}},P_{0})+\ell(Q)+\ell(P)\lesssim\ell(Q_{0})+\ell(Q)+\ell(P)\approx\ell(Q)\approx\ell(P),

which implies
2B_{Q}\subset CB_{P}\subset C^{\prime}B_{Q}
for some absolute
C
and
C^{\prime}
.

Finally, to see
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0})
recall that
{\mathcal{P}}(P)\lesssim\Lambda_{*}\Theta(R_{0})
for all
P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)\subset{\rm Tree}(R_{0})
, and we have
{\mathcal{P}}(Q)\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(P)
because
B_{Q}\subset CB_{P}
and
\ell(Q)\approx\ell(P)
.
∎

The following lemma states that the uniformly rectifiable set
\Gamma
lies relatively close to all the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
. This property will be crucial in our subsequent estimates.

Lemma 10.11
.

There exists
C_{*}=C_{*}(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})
such that for all
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
we have

(10.22)

\frac{C_{*}}{2}B_{Q}\cap\Gamma\neq\varnothing.

Proof.

Let
Q\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
and let
P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
be the cube from Lemma
10.10
.
In particular, we have

(10.23)

2B_{P}\subset CB_{Q}

for some absolute constant
C
.

If
P
contains some cube
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, then we are done, because in that case
D_{P_{1}}\subset 2B_{P}\subset CB_{Q}
, and
D_{P_{1}}\subset\Gamma
. Similarly, if
{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)\cap P\neq\varnothing
, then there is nothing to prove.

Now suppose that
P\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)=\varnothing
and
P
does not contain any cube from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
. Since
P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)
and
P\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)=\varnothing
, there exists some
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)
such that
P_{1}\subset P
. By our assumption
P_{1}\notin{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, and so

P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}_{*}(R)\setminus\left({\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\right)={\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R})\cup\left({\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\right).

There are two cases to consider. Suppose that
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R})
. Let
S\in(iii)_{R}
be such that
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(S)
. Since
S\notin{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
(otherwise we’d have
S\in(i)_{R}
), (
10.1
) gives
\ell(P_{1})\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\ell(S)
. Thus, there exists some constant
C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})
such that

D_{S}\subset C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{P_{1}}\subset C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{P}\overset{\eqref{eq:BPCBQ}}{\subset}C\,C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{Q}.

Since
D_{S}\subset\Gamma
, we get (
10.22
) as soon as
C_{*}\geq 2C\,C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})
.

Finally, suppose that
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
. Let
P_{0}\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{0}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
be the unique cube such that
P_{1}\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}(P_{0})
. By (
10.1
) we have
\ell(P_{0})\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\ell(P_{1})
, and so

2B_{P_{0}}\subset C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{P_{1}}\subset C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{P}\subset C\,C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})B_{Q}.

We claim that
2B_{P_{0}}\cap\Gamma\neq\varnothing
, and so (
10.22
) is satisfied if we assume
C_{*}\geq 2C\,C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})
. First, if
2B_{P_{0}}\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)\neq\varnothing
, then there is nothing to prove. Assume the contrary. In that case
P_{0}
is covered by cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)
. We claim that there exists some
S\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
such that
S\subset P_{0}
. Indeed, otherwise
P_{0}
would be covered by cubes from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, but then

-\mu(P_{0})=\sum_{P^{\prime}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}-\mu(P^{\prime})=\sum_{P^{\prime}\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}s(P^{\prime})\overset{\eqref{eqaq89}}{=}s(P_{0})\overset{\eqref{eq:aQmuQ}}{\geq}0,

which is a contradiction. Thus, there exists
S\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
such that
S\subset P_{0}
, which implies
D_{S}\subset 2B_{P_{0}}
. Since
D_{S}\subset\Gamma
, we are done.
∎

In the following lemma we define functions supported on
\Gamma
that approximate
\mu
at the level of
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
.

Lemma 10.12
.

There exist functions
g_{P}:\Gamma\to{\mathbb{R}},\ P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R),
such that each
g_{P}
is supported in
\Gamma\cap\overline{C_{*}B_{P}}
,

(10.24)

\int_{\Gamma}g_{P}\ d\nu=\mu(P),

and

(10.25)

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)}g_{P}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R).

Proof.

Assume first that the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
is finite.
We label the cubes from
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
in the order of increasing sidelength, that is we let
P_{1}
be a cube with the minimal sidelength, and then we label all the remaining cubes so that
\ell(P_{i})\leq\ell(P_{i+1})
.

The functions
g_{i}:=g_{P_{i}}
will be of the form
g_{i}=\alpha_{i}\chi_{A_{i}}
where
\alpha_{i}\geq 0
and
A_{i}\subset\Gamma\cap C_{*}B_{P_{i}}
. We begin by setting
\alpha_{1}=\mu(P_{1})/\nu(C_{*}B_{P_{1}})
and
A_{1}=C_{*}B_{P_{1}}\cap\Gamma
. Clearly, (
10.24
) holds for
P_{1}
. Moreover, using the fact that
\nu
is AD-regular and
\frac{C_{*}}{2}B_{P_{1}}\cap\Gamma\neq\varnothing
we get

\|g_{1}\|_{\infty}=\alpha_{1}=\frac{\mu(P_{1})}{\nu(C_{*}B_{P_{1}})}\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\frac{\mu(P_{1})}{\ell(P_{1})^{n}}\overset{\eqref{eq:treg dens bdd}}{\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}}\Theta(R_{0}).

We define the remaining
g_{k},\,k\geq 2
inductively. Suppose that
g_{1},\dots,g_{k-1}
have already been constructed, and they satisfy

(10.26)

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}\leq C^{\prime}\Theta(R)

for some constant
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0})
to be fixed below. Let
P_{i_{1}},\dots,P_{i_{m}}
be the subfamily of
P_{1},\dots,P_{k-1}
consisting of cubes such that
C_{*}B_{P_{k}}\cap C_{*}B_{P_{i_{j}}}\neq\varnothing
. Due to the non-decreasing sizes of
P_{i}
’s we have
P_{i_{j}}\subset C_{*}B_{P_{i_{j}}}\subset 3C_{*}B_{P_{k}}
. Hence, applying (
10.24
) to
g_{i_{j}}
we get

\sum_{j}\int_{\Gamma}g_{i_{j}}\ d\nu=\sum_{j}\mu(P_{i_{j}})\leq\mu(3C_{*}B_{P_{k}})\overset{\eqref{eq:treg dens bdd}}{\leq}C({\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}})\Theta(R_{0})\ell(P_{k})^{n}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0})\nu(\Gamma\cap C_{*}B_{P_{k}}),

for some
C^{\prime\prime}
depending on
\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}
. By the Chebyshev’s inequality

\nu\left(\Gamma\cap\big\{\textstyle{\sum_{j}}\,g_{i_{j}}\geq 2C^{\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0})\big\}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\nu(\Gamma\cap C_{*}B_{P_{k}}).

Set

A_{k}=\Gamma\cap C_{*}B_{P_{k}}\cap\big\{\textstyle{\sum_{j}}\,g_{i_{j}}\leq 2C^{\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0})\big\},

and then by the preceding estimate
\nu(A_{k})\geq\nu(\Gamma\cap C_{*}B_{P_{k}})/2.
We define

\alpha_{k}=\frac{\mu(P_{k})}{\nu(A_{k})},

so that for
g_{k}=\alpha_{k}\chi_{A_{k}}
we have
\int g_{k}\,d\nu=\mu(P_{k})
. Moreover, using AD-regularity of
\nu
and the fact that
\frac{C_{*}}{2}B_{P_{k}}\cap\Gamma\neq\varnothing

\alpha_{k}\leq 2\frac{\mu(P_{k})}{\nu(C_{*}B_{P_{k}})}\leq C({\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}})\frac{\mu(P_{k})}{\ell(P_{k})^{n}}\overset{\eqref{eq:treg dens bdd}}{\leq}C^{\prime\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0})

for some
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
depending on
\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}
. Hence, by the definition of
A_{k}

g_{k}(x)+\sum_{j}g_{i_{j}}(x)\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0})+2C^{\prime\prime}\Theta(R_{0}),\quad\text{for $x\in A_{k}$.}

For
x\not\in A_{k}
we have
g_{k}=0
, and so it follows from the above and the inductive assumption (
10.26
) that for
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime\prime\prime}+2C^{\prime\prime}
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{k}g_{i}\leq C^{\prime}\Theta(R),

which closes the induction.

Suppose now that the family
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
is infinite. We can relabel it so that
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)=\{P^{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. For each
N
we consider the family
\{P^{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
. We construct functions
g_{P^{1}}^{N},\dots,g_{P^{N}}^{N}
as above, so that they satisfy

\int g_{P^{1}}^{N}\ d\nu=\mu(P^{1}),\qquad\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{P^{i}}^{N}\leq C^{\prime}\Theta(R).

There exists a subsequence
I_{1}\subset{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\{g_{P^{1}}^{k}\}_{k\in I_{1}}
is convergent in the weak-
*
topology of
L^{\infty}(\nu)
to some function
g_{P^{1}}\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
. We take another subsequence
I_{2}\subset I_{1}
such that
\{g_{P^{2}}^{k}\}_{k\in I_{2}}
is convergent in the weak-
*
topology of
L^{\infty}(\nu)
to some
g_{P^{2}}\in L^{\infty}(\nu)
. Proceeding in this fashion we obtain a family
\{g_{P^{i}}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
such that
\operatorname{supp}g_{P^{i}}\subset\overline{C_{*}B_{P^{i}}}
, and the properties (
10.24
), (
10.25
) are preserved (because of the weak-
*
convergence).
∎

Recall that by the uniform rectifiability of
\nu
we have a good estimate on the
\beta_{\nu,2}
numbers (
10.20
). We will now use Lemmas
10.11
and
10.12
to transfer these estimates to the measure
\mu
and obtain

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R_{0})\,\mu(R).

In fact, we will show that

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R_{0})\,\mu(R),

and the former estimate will follow, since
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}_{*}(R)\subset{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
.

Let
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
, and let
L_{Q}
be an
n
-plane minimizing
\beta_{\nu,2}(C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q})
, where
C_{*}^{\prime}>2
is some constant depending on
C_{*}
, to be chosen in Lemma
10.13
. We estimate

(10.27)

\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\mu(Q)\lesssim\frac{\mu(Q)}{\ell(Q)^{n}}\int_{2B_{Q}}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\mu(x)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:treg dens bdd}}{\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}}\Theta(R_{0})\int_{2B_{Q}}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\mu(x)\\
=\Theta(R_{0})\left(\int_{2B_{Q}\cap R\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}\dots\,d\mu(x)+\int_{2B_{Q}\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}\dots\,d\mu(x)+\int_{2B_{Q}\setminus R}\dots\,d\mu(x)\right)\\
=:\Theta(R_{0})(I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}).

Concerning
I_{3}
, we use the trivial estimate

(10.28)

I_{3}\lesssim\mu(2B_{Q}\setminus R).

Estimating
I_{2}
is simple because on
{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)
we have
\mu=\rho^{\prime}\nu
for some
\rho^{\prime}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0})
, and so

(10.29)

I_{2}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*}}\Theta(R_{0})\int_{2B_{Q}\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\nu(x)\leq\Theta(R_{0})\int_{C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\nu(x)\\
\approx_{C_{*}^{\prime}}\Theta(R_{0})\beta_{\nu,2}(C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}.

Bounding
I_{1}
requires more work. First, we use the fact that
R\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)
is covered
\mu
-a.e. by
{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
:

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,P\cap 2B_{Q}\neq\varnothing}\int_{P}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,P\cap 2B_{Q}\neq\varnothing}\bigg(\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}g_{P}(x)\,d\nu(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\int\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}(\chi_{P}(x)d\mu(x)-g_{P}(x)d\nu(x))\bigg)

\displaystyle=:\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,P\cap 2B_{Q}\neq\varnothing}\left(I_{11}(P)+I_{12}(P)\right).

We need the following auxiliary result.

Lemma 10.13
.

If
P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
is such that
P\cap 2B_{Q}\neq\varnothing
, then
\ell(P)\lesssim\ell(Q)
and in consequence
\overline{C_{*}B_{P}}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}
for some
C_{*}^{\prime}=C_{*}^{\prime}(C_{*})\geq C_{*}
.

Proof.

If
\ell(P)\leq\ell(Q)
then there is nothing to prove, so suppose
\ell(P)>\ell(Q)
(in particular
\ell(P)\geq A_{0}\ell(Q))
. In that case we have
2B_{Q}\subset 2B_{P}
.

Note that if we had
Q\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)=\varnothing
, then
d_{R,*}(x_{Q})=0
, but by Lemma
10.9
(a) we know that
d_{R,*}(x_{Q})\geq 10\ell(P)
.
Hence, there exists some
S\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)
such that
S\subset Q
. Together with the fact that
2B_{Q}\subset 2B_{P}
this implies
B_{S}\cap 2B_{P}\neq\varnothing
. By Lemma
10.9
(b) this gives

\ell(P)\approx\ell(S)\leq\ell(Q).

∎

By the lemma above and the preceding estimate we get

(10.30)

I_{1}\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}I_{11}(P)+\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}I_{12}(P).

We estimate the first sum as follows:

(10.31)

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}g_{P}(x)\,d\nu(x)\\
=\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\,C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}g_{P}(x)\,d\nu(x)\\
\overset{{\operatorname{supp}g_{P}\subset C_{*}B_{P}}}{\leq}\int_{\Gamma\cap C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)}g_{P}(x)\,d\nu(x)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:sumgP}}{\lesssim}_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})\int_{\Gamma\cap C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}}\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\,d\nu(x)\approx_{C_{*}^{\prime}}\Theta(R_{0})\beta_{\nu,2}(C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}.

Concerning
I_{12}(P)
, observe that since
\int g_{P}\,d\nu=\mu(P)
by (
10.24
), we have

I_{12}(P)=\int\left(\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x_{P},L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\chi_{P}(x)d\mu(x)-g_{P}(x)d\nu(x)\right).

For
x\in\operatorname{supp}(\chi_{P}(x)d\mu(x)-g_{P}(x)d\nu(x))\subset C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}
we have

\left|\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{{\rm dist}(x_{P},L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{2}\right|\leq\frac{|x-x_{P}|}{\ell(Q)}\cdot\frac{{\rm dist}(x,L_{Q})+{\rm dist}(x_{P},L_{Q})}{\ell(Q)}\\
\lesssim\frac{C_{*}\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\cdot\frac{C_{*}^{\prime}\ell(Q)}{\ell(Q)}\approx_{C_{*},C_{*}^{\prime}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}.

Hence,

(10.32)

I_{12}(P)\lesssim_{C_{*},C_{*}^{\prime}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\mu(P).

Recall that
C_{*}
depends on
\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}
, and
C_{*}^{\prime}
depends on
C_{*}
. Thus, putting together the estimates (
10.30
), (
10.31
), and (
10.32
) yields

I_{1}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})\beta_{\nu,2}(C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\\
C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\mu(P).

Together with (
10.27
), (
10.28
), and (
10.29
) this gives

\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})^{2}\beta_{\nu,2}(C^{\prime}_{*}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}\\
+\Theta(R_{0})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\\
C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\mu(P)+\Theta(R_{0})\mu(2B_{Q}\setminus R).

Summing over
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
we get

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})^{2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\nu,2}(C^{\prime}_{*}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}\\
+\Theta(R_{0})\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R):\\
C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\mu(P)+\Theta(R_{0})\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\mu(2B_{Q}\setminus R)\\
=:\Theta(R_{0})^{2}S_{1}+\Theta(R_{0})S_{2}+\Theta(R_{0})S_{3}.

Concerning
S_{1}
, note that by (
10.22
) we know that if
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
, then
\nu(C_{*}B_{Q}\cap\Gamma)\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\ell(Q)^{n}
and for all
x\in C_{*}B_{Q}\cap\Gamma
we have
C^{\prime}_{*}B_{Q}\subset B(x,2C_{*}^{\prime}\ell(Q))
. Thus,
\beta_{\nu,2}(C^{\prime}_{*}B_{Q})\lesssim\beta_{\nu,2}(x,r)
for
2C_{*}^{\prime}\ell(Q)<r<3C_{*}^{\prime}\ell(Q)
. Observe also that the sets
C_{*}B_{Q}\cap\Gamma
corresponding to cubes of the same generation have bounded intersection. It follows easily that

S_{1}=\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\nu,2}(C^{\prime}_{*}B_{Q})^{2}\ell(Q)^{n}\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\int_{5C_{*}^{\prime}B_{R}}\int_{0}^{5C_{*}^{\prime}\ell(R)}\beta_{\nu,2}(x,r)^{2}\,\frac{dr}{r}d\nu(x)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:betasUR}}{\lesssim}_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\ell(R)^{n}.

To estimate
S_{2}
we change the order of summation:

S_{2}=\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)}\mu(P)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R):\\
C_{*}B_{P}\subset C_{*}^{\prime}B_{Q}\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}.

Note that the inner sum is essentially a geometric series, and so

S_{2}\lesssim_{C_{*},C_{*}^{\prime}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{Reg}}_{*}(R)}\mu(P)\leq\mu(R).

Finally, we can bound
S_{3}
using the small boundaries property of the David-Mattila lattice (
2.1
). To be more precise, note that for
Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)
if
2B_{Q}\setminus R\neq\varnothing
and
\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)
, then necessarily
Q\subset N_{k-1}(R)
, and even
2B_{Q}\subset N_{k-1}(R)
. Furthermore, the balls
2B_{Q}
for cubes of the same generation have only bounded intersection. Thus,

S_{3}\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\subset N_{k-1}(R),\\
\ell(Q)=A_{0}^{-k}\ell(R)\end{subarray}}\mu(2B_{Q})\lesssim\sum_{k\geq 1}\mu(N_{k-1}(R))\overset{\eqref{eqsmb2}}{\lesssim}\mu(90B(R))\approx\mu(R).

Putting the estimates for
S_{1},\ S_{2}
and
S_{3}
together we arrive at

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Treg}}_{*}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\mu(Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},K}\Theta(R_{0})^{2}\ell(R)^{n}+\Theta(R_{0})\mu(R)\lesssim_{\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})\mu(R),

where in the last estimate we used the fact that
\Theta(R_{0})\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}\Theta(R)
(note that
R\not\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
because
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\subset{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
and we assume
R\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})
). This finishes the proof of Lemma
10.7
.

10.3.
The corona decomposition and the proof of Lemma
10.1

Now we
define
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}={\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}(R_{0})
inductively.
We set
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}_{0}=\{R_{0}\}
and, assuming
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}_{k}
to be defined, we let

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}_{k+1}=\bigcup_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}_{k}}({\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{End}}_{*}(R_{0})).

Then we let

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}=\bigcup_{k\geq 0}{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}_{k}.

In this way, we have

{\rm Tree}(R_{0})=\bigcup_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R).

Lemma 10.14
.

We have

\sigma({\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}})\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sigma(R_{0})+\!\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})}\!\|\Delta_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q).

Proof.

By Lemma
10.5
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{BR}}(R))}\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R})\cap{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})}\mu(Q)+\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))\approx\mu(R).

By the construction of
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
, the cubes from

{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cup{\widehat{{\mathcal{C}h}}}((iii)_{R})
belong to
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
and the ones from
{\widehat{{\mathsf{Stop}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)
belong to
{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
, and so

\mu(R)\approx\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0}))}\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\!\!\mu(Q)+\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R)).

Notice that the families
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)
, with
R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}
, are disjoint, with the possible exception of
the roots and ending cubes of the trees
{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(\cdot)
, which may belong to two different trees.
Then we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\mu(R)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\bigg(\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\cap({\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})\cup{\mathsf{HD}}_{*}(R_{0}))}\!\!\!\!\mu(Q)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\!\!\!\mu(Q)\bigg)+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\!\mu({\widehat{{\mathsf{G}}}}(R))

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu(R_{0})+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\,\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\!\!\mu(Q).

Since the cubes
Q\in{\mathsf{BR}}(R)
do not belong to
{\mathsf{LD}}(R_{0})
, we have
\Theta(Q)\approx_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\Theta(R_{0})
for such cubes. The same happens for
R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}
, and thus

(10.33)

\sigma({\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}})\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sigma(R_{0})+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\Theta(R)^{2}\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\mu(Q).

To estimate the last sum above, we claim that for a given
Q\in{\mathsf{BR}}(R)\cap{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)
we have

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x_{Q})-\big(m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)\big|\lesssim{\mathcal{P}}(R)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+{\mathcal{P}}(Q)+\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2}.

This is proved exactly in the same way as Lemma
6.2
(see also (
6.4
)) and so we omit the arguments.
In case that both
R,Q\not\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, then

\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}\leq M\,\Theta(R)\quad\text{ and }\quad\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2}\leq M\,\Theta(Q),

and so
we get

\big|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x_{Q})-\big(m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big)\big|\leq C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},M)\Theta(R).

Thus, by the
{\mathsf{BR}}(R)
condition,

K\,\Theta(R)\leq|{\mathcal{R}}(\chi_{2R\setminus 2Q}\mu)(x_{Q})|\leq\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big|+C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},M)\Theta(R).

Hence, for
K\geq 2\,C(\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0},M)
, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}K\,\Theta(R)\leq\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big|.

In the general case where
Q
and
R
may belong to
{\mathsf{DB}}
, by analogous arguments, we get

\frac{1}{2}K\,\Theta(R)\leq\big|m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\big|+\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(R)\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\right)^{1/2}+\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(Q)\left(\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)}{\mu(Q)}\right)^{1/2},

where
\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(P)=1
if
P\in{\mathsf{DB}}
and
\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(P)=0
otherwise. Since

m_{\mu,Q}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)-m_{\mu,R}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)=\chi_{Q}\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu),

assuming
K\geq 1
, we get

(10.34)

\displaystyle\Theta(R)^{2}\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\int_{Q}\Big|\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\Big|^{2}\,d\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(R)\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(4R)}{\mu(R)}\,\mu(Q)+\!\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}(2Q).

By orthogonality, the first sum on the right hand is bounded by

\int\Big|\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\Big|^{2}\,d\mu=\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\|\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Also, it is clear that the second sum on the right had side of (
10.34
) does not exceed

\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(R)\,{\mathcal{E}}(4R)
. Therefore,

\Theta(R)^{2}\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{BR}}(R)}\mu(Q)\\
\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\|\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\chi_{\mathsf{DB}}(R)\,{\mathcal{E}}(4R)+\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}(2Q).

Plugging the previous estimate into (
10.33
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma({\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sigma(R_{0})+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\sum_{P\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)\setminus{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)}\|\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(4R)+\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\,\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{End}}}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!{\mathcal{E}}(2Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sigma(R_{0})+\sum_{P\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})}\|\Delta_{P}({\mathcal{R}}\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}(4Q),

as wished.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10.1
.

Given
R_{0}\in{\mathsf{Top}}
, combining Lemmas
10.7
and
10.14
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})}\!\!\!\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\Theta(Q)\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\Theta(R)\sum_{Q\in{\widehat{{\rm Tree}}}(R)}\beta_{\mu,2}(2B_{Q})^{2}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sum_{R\in{\widehat{{\mathsf{Top}}}}}\Theta(R)^{2}\mu(R)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda_{*},\delta_{0}}\sigma(R_{0})+\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})}\!\|\Delta_{Q}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\rm Tree}(R_{0})\cap{\mathsf{DB}}}\!\!{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q).

∎

Part 4: The proof of the Second Main Proposition

In this part of the paper, corresponding to Sections
11
–
13
we choose
{\mathsf{OP}}(R)={\mathsf{NDB}}(R)
for any
R\in{\mathsf{MDW}}
in the
construction of tractable trees. Our goal is to prove Main Proposition
3.7
.

11.
The good dominating family
{\mathsf{GDF}}

Let

(11.1)

M\geq M_{0}\coloneq A_{0}^{k_{0}n}\quad\mbox{ for some $k_{0}\geq 4$ big enough.}

For each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
we choose the minimal
k(Q,M)\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that (
3.12
) holds with
k=k(Q,M)
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Assume
k_{0}
big enough in (
11.1
). For each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
, we have

k(Q,M)>\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}\,k_{0}+4.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that for
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{j}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq CA_{0}^{2nj}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,m_{j}(Q)^{\!1/2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{j}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}A_{0}^{2nj-\frac{j}{2}}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\mu(9Q),

where we used (
3.4
) and we applied (
3.11
) to estimate
m_{j}(Q)
.
Then, for
0\leq j\leq\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}\,k_{0}+4
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{j}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}A_{0}^{(2n-\frac{1}{2})\left(\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}\,k_{0}+4\right)}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\mu(9Q)

\displaystyle=C_{1}M_{0}^{2}\,A_{0}^{-\frac{k_{0}}{4}+8n-2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\mu(9Q).

So, for
k_{0}
big enough, the right hand side above is smaller than
M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\mu(9Q),

which ensures that
k(Q,M)>\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}\,k_{0}+4
.
∎

Let

k_{\Lambda}=\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}\,k_{0},

so that
k(Q,M)>k_{\Lambda}
for each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
, by the preceding lemma.
Assuming
k_{0}
to be a multiple of
8n-2
, it follows that
k_{\Lambda}
is natural number.
Notice also that
k_{\Lambda}
is the mean of
k_{0}
and
4nk_{0}/(4n-1)
, so that, if we let

\Lambda=A_{0}^{k_{\Lambda}n},

we have that
\Lambda
is the geometric mean of
M_{0}
and
M_{0}^{\frac{4n}{4n-1}}
, that is

(11.2)

\Lambda=M_{0}^{1/2}\,M_{0}^{\frac{2n}{4n-1}}=M_{0}^{\frac{8n-1}{8n-2}}>M_{0}.

Note that this choice is consistent with (
4.1
) for
M=M_{0}
, assuming that
k_{0}=k_{0}(n)
is big enough.

Observe that, for
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
, taking into account that
k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}>4
,

(11.3)

\displaystyle M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(9Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\,\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{2}\,\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\!\!\Theta(S)^{2}\!\!\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\mu(P).

Given
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
and
S\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)
, we write
S\in G(Q,M)
if

(11.4)

\mu(S)\leq 2\Lambda^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\!1/2}\mu(P).

We also denote
B(Q,M)={\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)\setminus G(Q,M)
.

Given
\lambda>0
, for
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
and
S\in G(Q,M)
, we denote

{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)=\{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S,\,\ell(P)\geq\lambda\,\ell(S)\}.

Lemma 11.2
.

If
\lambda>0
satisfies

(11.5)

\lambda\leq\frac{c_{3}}{\Lambda^{4}}=:\lambda_{0}(\Lambda)

for some small absolute constant
c_{3}\in(0,1)
,
then, for each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)
we have

M^{2}\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{S\in G(Q,M)}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\Theta(P)^{2}\mu(P).

Also, each
S\in G(Q,M)
satisfies

\Theta(S)^{2}\,\mu(S)\leq 4\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\Theta_{\mu}(P)^{2}\,\mu(P).

Proof.

Arguing as in (
11.3
), by the definition of
B(Q,M)
we get

\displaystyle M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(9Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in G(Q,M)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda^{2}\,\sum_{S\in B(Q,M)}\Theta(S)^{2}\!\!\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in G(Q,M)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{S\in B(Q,M)}\Theta(S)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\mu(S).

Using that
B(Q,M)\subset{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)
and that, by the definition of
k(Q,M)
,

\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}(9Q)}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(S)^{2}\mu(S)\leq M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(9Q),

we get

M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(9Q)\leq 2\sum_{S\in G(Q,M)}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P).

Next, for
S\in G(Q,M)
, we split

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)=\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\cdots\;+\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q)\setminus{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S):P\subset S}\!\!\!\cdots.

We estimate the last sum:

\displaystyle\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q)\setminus{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{1/2}\,\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\!\!\!\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\!\!\!\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{1/2}\,\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\,\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(S)^{2}\,\mu(S)

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}^{1/2}\,\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\,\Theta(S)^{2}\,\mu(S),

taking into account the choice of
\lambda
for the last estimate.
By (
11.4
), since
S\in G(Q,M)
, we have

(11.6)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\,\Theta(S)^{2}\,\mu(S)

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda^{2}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\,\Theta(S)^{2}\,\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\!1/2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

Hence, for
c_{3}
small enough,

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q)\setminus{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P).

Consequently, for every
S\in G(Q,M)
,

(11.7)

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)\leq 2\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P).

From the conditions
P\subset S
and
\Theta(P)=\Lambda\,\Theta(S)
we also get

\Theta(P)\leq\Theta(S)\,\frac{\ell(S)^{n}}{\ell(P)^{n}}=\Lambda^{-1}\,\Theta(P)\,\frac{\ell(S)^{n}}{\ell(P)^{n}}.

Thus,

(11.8)

\ell(P)\leq\Lambda^{-1/n}\ell(S).

Then we derive

\displaystyle M^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q)

\displaystyle\leq 4\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{S\in G(Q,M)}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P),

which proves the first statement of the lemma.

Concerning the second statement, notice that by (
11.6
), (
11.7
), and (
11.8
), we have

\displaystyle\Theta(S)^{2}\,\mu(S)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)}(Q):P\subset S}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\left(\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(S)}\right)^{\!1/2}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P)

\displaystyle\leq 4\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\Theta(P)^{2}\,\mu(P).

∎

We denote

{\mathsf{DB}}:={\mathsf{DB}}(M_{0})=\bigcup_{M\geq M_{0}}\big({\mathsf{DB}}(M)\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}(2M)\big).

Remark that the last identity holds because of the polynomial growth of
\mu
.
For each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, choose
M(Q)
such that
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}(2M(Q))
.
We denote by
{\mathsf{GDF}}
(which stands for “good dominating family") the family of the cubes
S\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}
belonging to
G(Q,M(Q))
for some
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}
.
In particular, by the preceding lemma, the cubes
S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}

satisfy the property that there exists a family
{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{\mathcal{P}}(S)

such that

(11.9)

\Theta(P)=\Lambda\,\Theta(S)\quad\text{ and }\quad\ell(P)\geq\lambda_{0}\,\ell(S)\quad\mbox{ for all $P\in{\mathcal{I}}_{S}$}

(with
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0}(\Lambda)
as in (
11.5
)),
and

(11.10)

\mu(S)\leq 4\Lambda^{2-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{I}}_{S}}\mu(P).

Observe that, for
S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}
,

(11.11)

\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{S})=\Lambda^{2}\Theta(S)^{2}\,\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{I}}_{S}}\mu(P)\geq\frac{1}{4}\,\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2n}}\sigma(S),

by (
11.10
).

Lemma 11.3
.

We have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{S}).

Proof.

For each
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, choose
M=M(Q)
such that
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}(M)\setminus{\mathsf{DB}}(2M)
.
By the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)
, we have

{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim M(Q)^{2}\,\Theta(Q)^{2}\,\mu(Q).

Then, by Lemma
11.2
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}\sum_{S\in G(Q,M(Q))}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\sum_{P\in{\rm big}_{\lambda}(S)}\Theta(P)^{2}\mu(P)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}:S\subset 9Q}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}\,\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{S})

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{S})\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}^{{\mathcal{P}}}:9Q\supset S}\left(\frac{\ell(S)}{\ell(Q)}\right)^{\!1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{S}).

∎

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
.
Let
S,P,P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
be such that
P\subset P^{\prime}\subset S
. Suppose that

\Theta(P)\geq\Lambda\,\Theta(S)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\Theta(P^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S).

Then we have

c_{4}\ell(P)\leq(\delta_{0}\,\Lambda^{-1})^{1/n}\ell(P^{\prime})\leq(\delta_{0}\,\Lambda^{-1})^{1/n}\ell(S).

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of the following:

\Theta(P^{\prime})\,\frac{\ell(P^{\prime})^{n}}{\ell(P)^{n}}\geq c\,\Theta(P)\geq c\Lambda\,\Theta(S)\geq c\Lambda\delta_{0}^{-1}\Theta(P^{\prime}).

∎

Remark 11.5
.

Let

(11.12)

\lambda=\lambda_{0}(\Lambda)=\frac{c_{3}}{\Lambda^{4}}.

By the preceding lemma, if

(\delta_{0}\Lambda^{-1})^{1/n}<c_{4}\lambda,

or equivalently,

(11.13)

\delta_{0}<c^{\prime}\Lambda^{1-4n},

then, for any
S\in{\mathsf{GDF}}
and
P\in{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
,
there does not exist any cube
P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
satisfying

P\subset P^{\prime}\subset S\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\Theta(P^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S).

Another easy (but important) property of the family
{\mathsf{GDF}}
is stated in the next lemma.

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
S_{1},S_{2}\in{\mathsf{GDF}}
be such that
S_{2}\subsetneq S_{1}
and
\Theta(S_{1})=\Theta(S_{2})
. Then
there exist
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}
and
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
such that
Q^{\prime}\subset 9Q
,
\ell(Q)=\ell(Q^{\prime})
and

S_{1}\supset Q^{\prime}\supset S_{2}
, with
S_{2}\in G(Q,M(Q))
for some
M(Q)\geq M_{0}
.

Proof.

This is due to the fact that, by definition, there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}
such that
S_{2}\in G(Q,M)
for
some
M=M(Q)\geq M_{0}
. So
S_{2}\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)
and
S_{2}\subset 9Q
. Then

\ell(Q)\leq\ell(S_{1})
, because otherwise
S_{2}\not\in{\mathsf{hd}}^{k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda}}(Q)
since
S_{2}
would not be
maximal among the cubes
S
contained in
9Q
such that
\Theta(S)\geq A_{0}^{(k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda})n}\,\Theta(Q)
(as
S_{1}
is also contained in
9Q
and
\Theta(S_{1})=A_{0}^{(k(Q,M)-k_{\Lambda})n}\,\Theta(Q)
). The fact that
\ell(S_{1})\geq\ell(Q)\geq\ell(S_{2})
implies the existence of a cube
Q^{\prime}
such as the one in the lemma.
∎

Lemma 11.7
.

For any cube
R\in{\mathsf{GDF}}
, we have
{\mathcal{I}}_{R}\subset\big({\mathsf{HD}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{NDB}}(R)\big)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)
, and thus

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{NDB}}(R))\geq\frac{1}{4}\,\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2n}}\,\sigma(R).

Proof.

Recall that, by (
11.11
),

\sigma({\mathcal{I}}_{R})\geq\frac{1}{4}\,\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2n}}\,\sigma(R).

By the choice of
\delta_{0}
and Remark
11.5
, there does not exist any
Q\in{\mathsf{LD}}(R)
which
contains any cube from
{\mathcal{I}}_{R}
. Also, the cubes from
Q\in{\mathcal{I}}_{R}
satisfy
\ell(Q)\geq\lambda\ell(R)
and so there does exist any cube
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{NDB}}(R)
such that
Q\subset Q^{\prime}\subset R
. So
{\mathcal{I}}_{R}\subset{\mathsf{HD}}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Stop}}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{NDB}}(R)
. The last statement in the lemma follows from (
11.11
).
∎

Note that the lemma above implies that
{\mathsf{GDF}}\subset{\mathsf{MDW}}
.

12.
The layers
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
and
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
and the typical tractable trees

In this section we set
{\mathsf{F}}={\mathsf{GDF}}
and we consider the associated subfamilies
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}
,
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
and
{\mathsf{L}}
,
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
,
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
defined
in Section
8
, so that we have the splitting

{\mathsf{GDF}}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,\bigcup_{h\geq 1}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}.

Recall the properties shown in Lemma
8.1
for the families
{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
and
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}
.
Notice that, by (
8.1
),

\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R))\leq m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\!\!\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R))),

where we wrote
{\mathsf{L}}={\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})
to emphasize the dependence of
{\mathsf{L}}
on
{\mathsf{GDF}}
.

Observe that since
{\mathsf{GDF}}\subset{\mathsf{MDW}}
by Lemma
11.7
, the families
{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
introduced in Section
4.3
are well-defined for
R\in{\mathsf{GDF}}
. Our next objective consists of proving the following lemma, which is the main technical achievement in this section.

Lemma 12.1
.

Choose
{\mathsf{F}}={\mathsf{GDF}}
.
There exists some constant
C_{6}
such that, for all
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and all
k\geq 0
,

\#\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}}):\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}\leq C_{6}\,(\log\Lambda)^{2}.

Although the statement above looks similar to Lemma
8.3
, the proof is very different.
The cubes from the
{\mathsf{NDB}}(\,\cdot\,)
play an important role in the arguments. In fact, the main reason for
the introduction of the stopping condition involving the family
{\mathsf{NDB}}(\,\cdot\,)
in Section
5
is that it allows to prove
this lemma.

Proof.

First notice that if
R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})
and
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
are such that
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, then there exists some
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube that contains
P
and belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, by the definition of
the family
{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
. We denote by
{\widetilde{P}}
the smallest such
{\mathcal{P}}
-doubling cube. This satisfies

\delta_{0}\,\Theta(Q)\lesssim\Theta({\widetilde{P}})\leq\Lambda^{2}\,\Theta(Q),

or equivalently,
\Lambda^{k}\Theta(R)\in[\Lambda^{-2}\Theta({\widetilde{P}}),C\delta_{0}^{-1}\Theta({\widetilde{P}})]
.
Hence, if
\Theta(R)=A_{0}^{nj}
, it follows that

-C\log\Lambda\leq j+c\,k\log\Lambda-c^{\prime}\log\Theta({\widetilde{P}})\leq C|\log\delta_{0}|=C^{\prime}\log\Lambda.

Thus,
R
belongs at most to
C^{\prime\prime}\log\Lambda
families
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
such that
there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
such that
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\setminus{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))

Suppose now that there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
such that
P\in{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\subset{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.
In this case, by Lemma
4.3
,
\ell(P)\gtrsim\delta_{0}^{-2}\,\ell(Q)
. Hence, there
are at most
C\,|\log\delta_{0}|\approx\log\Lambda
cubes
Q
such that

P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
, which in turn implies that again there are at most

C^{\prime\prime\prime}\log\Lambda
families
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
such that
there exists
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
satisfying
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\cap{\mathsf{Neg}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
.

By the previous discussion, to prove
the lemma, it is enough to show that, for each
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
,
k\geq 0
,

(12.1)

\#{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\leq C_{7}\log\Lambda,

where

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}:\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}.

To prove (
12.1
), let
R_{0}
be a cube in
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
with maximal side length, and let
h_{0}
be such that
R_{0}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{0}}(P,k)\equiv{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\cap{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{0}}
.

Claim 12.2
.

Let
R_{1}
be another cube from
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
, and let
h_{1}
be such that
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}(P,k)
.
Then
h_{1}\geq h_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
h_{1}<h_{0}
.
Let
R_{0}^{h_{1}}
be the cube that contains
R_{0}
and belongs to

{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h_{1}}
. Observe that, by Lemma
4.5
,

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R_{0}))\cap B(e^{\prime\prime}(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{0}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{0}))\cap B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1})).

Since
\ell(R_{0})\geq\ell(R_{1})
, we infer that

B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{0}},\tfrac{9}{2}\ell(R_{0})).

As
x_{R_{0}}\in B(x_{R_{0}}^{h_{1}},\frac{1}{2}\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}}))
and

\ell(R_{0})\leq A_{0}^{-1}\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle B(x_{R_{0}},\tfrac{9}{2}\ell(R_{0}))

\displaystyle\subset B(x_{R_{0}}^{h_{1}},\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}})+\tfrac{9}{2}\ell(R_{0}))\subset B(x_{R_{0}}^{h_{1}},\tfrac{1}{2}\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}})\!+\tfrac{9}{2}A_{0}^{-1}\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}}))

\displaystyle\subset B(x_{R_{0}}^{h_{1}},(\tfrac{1}{2}+8A_{0}^{-1})\ell(R_{0}^{h_{1}}))\subset B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}^{h_{1}})),

where the lat inclusion follows from the definition of
B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}^{h_{1}}))
.
Then we deduce that

B(e^{(4)}(R_{1}))\subset B(x_{R_{1}},\tfrac{3}{2}\ell(R_{1}))\subset B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}^{h_{1}})),

which contradicts the property (i) of the family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}
in Lemma
8.1
, because
R_{1}\neq R_{0}^{h_{1}}
.
∎

Claim 12.3
.

Let
R_{1}
be another cube from
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)
, and let
h_{1}
be such that
R_{1}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{1}}(P,k)
.
Then

(12.2)

h_{1}\leq h_{0}+C\,\log\Lambda.

Proof.

Suppose that
h_{1}>h_{0}+1
. This implies that there are cubes

\{R_{1}^{h}\}_{h_{0}+1\leq h\leq h_{1}-1}
such that
R_{1}^{h}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h}
, with

R_{1}^{h_{0}+1}\supset R_{1}^{h_{0}+2}\supset\ldots\supset R_{1}^{h_{1}-1}\supsetneq R_{1}^{h_{1}}=R_{1}.

Observe now that
\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})\leq\ell(R_{0})
. Otherwise, there exists some cube

R_{1}^{h_{0}}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{j}^{h_{0}}
that contains
R_{1}^{h_{0}+1}
with

\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})\geq A_{0}\,\ell(R_{0}),

Since
P\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R_{0}))\cap B(e^{\prime\prime}(R_{1}))
, arguing as in the previous claim, we deduce that

B(e^{(4)}(R_{0}))\subset B(e^{(4)}(R_{1}^{h_{0}}))
, which contradicts again the property (i) of the family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h_{0}}
, as above. So we have

\ell(R_{1}^{h})\leq\ell(R_{1}^{h_{0}+1})\leq\ell(R_{0})\quad\mbox{ for $h\geq h_{0}+1$.}

By the construction of
{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R_{0})
in Section
4.3
, there exists a sequence of cubes

S_{0}=R_{0},S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k}=Q
such that

S_{i+1}\in{\mathsf{GH}}(S_{i})\;\mbox{ for $i=0,\ldots,k-1$,}

and
P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(S_{k}))
. In case that
P
is contained in some
Q^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(Q))={\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{k}))
, we write
S_{k+1}=Q^{\prime}
, and we let
\tilde{k}:=k+1
. Otherwise, we let
\tilde{k}:=k
.
In any case, obviously we have
\ell(S_{i+1})<\ell(S_{i})
for all
i
.
So, for each
h
with
h_{0}+1\leq h\leq h_{1}
there is some
i=i(h)
such that

(12.3)

\ell(S_{i})>\ell(R_{1}^{h})\geq\ell(S_{i+1}),

with
0\leq i\leq\tilde{k}
, where we understand that
S_{\tilde{k}+1}=P
.
We claim that either
i\lesssim 1
or
i={\widetilde{k}}
, with the implicit constant depending on
n
.
Indeed, in the case
i<{\widetilde{k}}
, let
T\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
be such that
T\supset S_{i+1}
and
\ell(T)=\ell(R_{1}^{h})
.
Notice that, since
2R_{1}^{h}\cap 2T\neq\varnothing
(because both
2R_{1}^{h}
and
2T
contain
P
) and

\ell(R_{1}^{h})=\ell(T)
, we have

(12.4)

{\mathcal{P}}(T)\approx{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h})\approx\Theta(R_{1}^{h})=\Theta(R_{0}).

On the other hand,

(12.5)

{\mathcal{P}}(T)\geq\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})

because otherwise
either
T\in{\mathsf{LD}}(S_{i})
or it is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{LD}}(S_{i})\cup{\mathsf{NDB}}(S_{i})
. In any case, this would imply that
S_{i+1}
does not belong to
{\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
.
Thus, from (
12.4
) and (
12.5
)
we derive that

\Theta(R_{0})\gtrsim\delta_{0}\,\Theta(S_{i})=\delta_{0}\,\Lambda^{i}\,\Theta(R_{0}).

Hence
\Lambda^{i}\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}
, which yields
i\lesssim_{n}1
if
i<{\widetilde{k}}
, as claimed.

The preceding discussion implies that, in order to prove (
12.2
), it suffices to show that, for each fixed

i=0,\ldots,\tilde{k}
, there are at most
C\log\Lambda
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
12.3
). To this end, suppose first that
i<\tilde{k}
. It is easy to check that there is at most one
cube
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
12.3
) such that

(12.6)

\ell(R_{1}^{h})\leq\lambda\,\ell(S_{i}).

Indeed, if otherwise
R_{1}^{h}
and
R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}
are such that

(12.7)

\lambda\,\ell(S_{i})\geq\ell(R_{1}^{h})>\ell(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})>\ell(S_{i+1}),

then, by Lemma
11.6
,
there exist
T_{a}\in{\mathsf{DB}}
and
T_{b}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
such that
T_{b}\subset 9T_{a}
,
\ell(T_{a})=\ell(T_{b})
and

R_{1}^{h}\supsetneq T_{b}\supsetneq R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}
.
Now, let
T_{c}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
be such that
T_{c}\supset S_{i+1}
and
\ell(T_{c})=\ell(T_{a})
.
Since
T_{a}\in{\mathsf{DB}}
, we infer that
T_{c}\in{\mathsf{NDB}}(S_{i})
, taking into account that

2T_{b}\cap 2T_{c}\neq\varnothing
,
T_{b}\subset 9T_{a}
, and
\ell(T_{c})<\lambda\,\ell(S_{i})
.
This is a contradiction, because this would imply that either
T_{c}\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
or

T_{c}
is contained in some cube from
{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{i}))
, which ensures that

S_{i+1}\not\in{\mathsf{GH}}(S_{i})
(notice that we are using the fact that
i<\tilde{k}
).
So (
12.6
) holds. Clearly this implies that
there are at most
C|\log\lambda|\approx\log\Lambda
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
12.3
).

In the case
i=\tilde{k}
, the same argument as above shows that if

R_{1}^{h}
and
R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}
satisfy (
12.7
),
then the cube
T_{c}
in the preceding paragraph belongs to
{\mathsf{NDB}}(S_{\tilde{k}})
again.
So again either
T_{c}\in{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{\tilde{k}}))
or
T_{c}
is contained in some cube from

{\mathsf{Stop}}(e^{\prime}(S_{\tilde{k}}))
. As a consequence, by the definition of the family
{\mathsf{End}}(e^{\prime}(R))
and Lemma

3.3
, if we denote by
T_{m}
the
m
-th descendant of
T_{c}
which contains
P
, it follows that

\Theta(T_{m})\lesssim A_{0}^{-m/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(T_{c})\approx A_{0}^{-m/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(T_{b})\leq A_{0}^{-m/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})\quad\mbox{for all $m\geq 1$.}

The last inequality follows from the fact that we can assume that
R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}\in G(T_{a},M)
, for some
M\geq M_{0}
, by Lemma
11.6
. Since any cube
R_{1}^{h^{\prime\prime}}
with
h^{\prime\prime}>h^{\prime}
is contained in a cube
2T_{m}
with
\ell(T_{m})\approx\ell(R_{1}^{h^{\prime\prime}})
for some
m\geq h^{\prime}-h^{\prime\prime}-1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Theta(R_{1}^{h^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Theta(2T_{m})\lesssim\Theta(T_{m-1})\lesssim A_{0}^{-(m-1)/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{0}^{-(h^{\prime}-h^{\prime\prime})/2}\,{\mathcal{P}}(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})\approx A_{0}^{-(h^{\prime}-h^{\prime\prime})/2}\,\Theta(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}}).

Since
\Theta(R_{1}^{h^{\prime}})=\Theta(R_{1}^{h^{\prime\prime}})
, we get
|h^{\prime}-h^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim 1
. Consequently, in the case
i=\tilde{k}
there are again at most
C|\log\lambda|\approx\log\Lambda
cubes
R_{1}^{h}
satisfying
(
12.3
).
∎

To prove the lemma, notice that each family
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)
consists of a single cube, at most.
Indeed, if
R\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)
, then
P\subset B(e^{\prime\prime}(R))
. Thus if
R,R^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{h}(P,k)
,
then
B(e^{\prime\prime}(R))\cap B(e^{\prime\prime}(R^{\prime}))\neq\varnothing
, which cannot happen if
R\neq R^{\prime}
.
From this fact and the preceding claims, we infer that
\#{\mathsf{L}}_{j}(P,k)\leq C\log\Lambda
, so that (
12.1
) holds.
∎

Recall that, for
R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})
,
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)
, we write
P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
if there exists some

P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
such that

A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\,\ell(P)\quad\text{ and }\quad 20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing.

Recall also that
{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))
is called a typical tractable tree, and we write
Q\in{\mathsf{Ty}}
, if

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\leq\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{3n}}\,\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

Lemma 12.4
.

We have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{2n}}(\log\Lambda)^{2}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

Proof.

By Lemmas
11.3
and
11.7
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R)).

Also, by (
8.1
) and Lemma
4.5
,

\displaystyle\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{GDF}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(R))

\displaystyle\leq m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(R)))

\displaystyle\leq m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

Therefore,

(12.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

\displaystyle=m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Ty}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

\displaystyle\quad+m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Ty}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))

\displaystyle=:S_{1}+S_{2}.

By definition, for
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Ty}}
, we have

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\leq\Lambda^{\frac{1}{3n}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(R))}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P).

Hence, the term
S_{2}
in (
12.8
) does not exceed

m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{3n}}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\setminus{\mathsf{Ty}}}\,\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}:P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\\
\lesssim m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6n}}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\,\#A(P,k),

where

A(P,k)=\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}}):P\sim{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\text{ for some }Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\big\}.

From the definition (
6.1
) and Lemma
12.1
, it follows that

(12.9)

\displaystyle\#A(P,k)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing\\
A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}\!\!\#\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}}):\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\text{ such that }P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}:20P^{\prime}\cap 20P\neq\varnothing\\
A_{0}^{-2}\ell(P)\leq\ell(P^{\prime})\leq A_{0}^{2}\ell(P)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!(\log\Lambda)^{2}\lesssim(\log\Lambda)^{2}.

Therefore, the term
S_{2}
in (
12.8
) satisfies

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6n}}\,(\log\Lambda)^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\lesssim m_{0}\,B^{1/4}\,\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6n}}\,(\log\Lambda)^{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P).

Since
B=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{100n}}
, we deduce that

S_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)

if
\Lambda
is big enough.
So we get
8
8

8

Here we are assuming that

\sum_{P\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9P)<\infty
. To ensure that this holds, if necessary we may replace the measure
\mu
by
another approximating measure of the form
\mu_{\ell}={\varphi}_{\ell}*\mu
, where
{\varphi}_{\ell}
is a
C^{\infty}
bump function supported on
B(0,\ell)
, with
\|{\varphi}_{\ell}\|_{1}=1
. Then we prove the Main Proposition
3.6
for
\mu_{\ell}
with bounds independent of
\ell
, and finally we let
\ell\to\infty
.

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\leq 2S_{1},

which proves the lemma.
∎

13.
The proof of the Second Main Proposition
3.7

We have to show that

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\leq C\big(\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|\big),

with
C
possibly depending on
\Lambda
and other parameters.
Recall that by Lemma
12.4
, we have

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim\Lambda^{\frac{-1}{2n}}(\log\Lambda)^{2}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}}\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q))).

Also, by Lemma
12.1
, it turns out that, for all
P\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}
and all
k\geq 0
,

(13.1)

\#\big\{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}}):\exists\,Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\mbox{ such that }P\in{\mathcal{T}}(e^{\prime}(Q))\big\}\leq C_{6}\,(\log\Lambda)^{2}.

Observe now that, by Lemma
6.16
, for each
Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)\cap{\mathsf{Ty}}
,
either

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\lesssim\theta_{0}^{2}\,\mu(Q)\leq\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\,\mu(Z(Q)),

where
Z(Q)
is the set
Z
appearing in (
6.3
) (replacing
R
by
Q
there), or

\sigma({\mathsf{HD}}_{1}(e(Q)))\leq\Lambda\,\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\Lambda}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\|\Delta_{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}(e^{\prime}(Q))}{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\sum_{R\in{\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{GDF}})}\,\sum_{k\geq 0}B^{-k/2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{Trc}}_{k}(R)}\mu(Z(Q))

\displaystyle=:S_{1}+S_{2}.

By the same arguments used to bound the term
T_{1}
in Lemma
9.1
, using (
13.1
) instead of (
9.5
),
we derive

S_{1}\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}.

Also, by estimates similar to the ones for the term
T_{3}
in Lemma
9.1
, we get

S_{2}\lesssim\theta_{0}^{2}\,{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\|\mu\|.

So we obtain

\sum_{Q\in{\mathsf{DB}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty}(9Q)\lesssim_{\Lambda}\|{\mathcal{R}}\mu\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}+{\varepsilon}_{Z}^{-1}\theta_{0}^{2}\,\|\mu\|,

which concludes the proof of Second Main Proposition
3.6
.
